### C-37 A trial of CPU-heavy steps on TACC
**H** for step (0), the PI's; then **M** · Pod: no; **TACC: yes** (rules below) · Depends: C-37a for steps (1) to (4); step (3) reads stage H1's files, which are built · asked for by the PI on 2026-11-16 ("at some point, i will want to move cpu-intensive work to tacc ... make a card with a trial to do that in the near term-ish. i have tacc credits")
Files: `profiles/` (one new host profile for the TACC system, only if step (4) needs it to run a step), `scripts/tacc/` (new: Slurm job scripts and a driver for the trial), `RUNBOOK.md` (a new section, only for what was run), one measurement note with its directory. No change under `src/` in this card: what the code would need is reported, not done.
What TACC is, from its public user guides read on 2026-11-16 (docs.tacc.utexas.edu: Lonestar6 and Stampede3 guides, both "last update September 18, 2026"; the MFA page; TACC's containers tutorial). None of it is measured by us; step (1) checks what matters.
- The Texas Advanced Computing Center at UT Austin: batch clusters under Slurm. **Lonestar6** is the system for UT System researchers, with allocations through TxRAS; 560 compute nodes of 128 cores each (two AMD EPYC 7763), 256 GB of memory, 288 GB of node-local `/tmp` that is purged at the end of each job. **Stampede3** is allocated through NSF ACCESS (nodes of 48 to 112 cores, 128 to 256 GB, three nodes with 4 TB). Frontera was retired in 2026 and Horizon is in early operations (news reports; not checked on TACC's own pages).
- Cost: **a whole node is billed, 1 SU per node-hour** in Lonestar6's `normal` queue (1 to 64 nodes a job, 48 hours at most, 20 jobs a user); `development` is 2 hours at most at the same rate; every job is billed at least 0.25 hours. `taccinfo` shows a project's balance. Startup allocations are 500 SUs and research allocations up to 100,000 (a search result, not TACC's own page: check in step (0)).
- Files: `$HOME` 10 GB; `$WORK` 1 TB and 3,000,000 files, not purged, not backed up; `$SCRATCH` without quota, **files not accessed for ten days are purged**. Login nodes are for file management and data movement only; all computing is a batch or interactive job.
- Containers: Apptainer through `module load tacc-apptainer`, **on compute nodes only**; an existing `.sif` runs with `apptainer exec --cleanenv`. Many serial tasks in one job: PyLauncher, or Slurm job arrays.
- Login: **every SSH login needs a new MFA code** (an authenticator app or a text message). The guides say nothing about scripted logins or reused connections.
- Against the pod (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-16, `lscpu`): four Xeon E7-4850 v3 at 2.2 GHz, 56 cores and 112 threads for every user, of which we may use 40 or 56 threads. No TACC file system is mounted on the pod (*measured*, `mount`). So one Lonestar6 node has more than twice the pod's cores, all ours for the job's length, with no 300 CPU-minute limit.
- What that would cost (*derived* from our measured rates, assuming a Lonestar6 core does what a pod thread does, which step (3) measures): the full Pfam scan of one phase-2 stage, 9,961 to 12,428 CPU-minutes (*estimate*, card C-36), is 1.3 to 1.6 node-hours, so 8 to 10 SUs for the six stages; `rM.2`'s clustering, 14,650 CPU-minutes with a peak of 178 GB (*measured*), is about 2 node-hours of CPU and fits one node's memory.
Do:
(0) **The PI**: which TACC system and project the credits are on, and their balance (`taccinfo`, or the portal); the MFA device is the PI's, so say how an agent may work there: (a) the PI opens one SSH connection and the agent's commands reuse it (ask TACC whether that is allowed; the guides do not say), (b) the agent writes the job scripts and the PI submits them, or (c) TACC's job API, if the project may use it. Until this is answered an agent does not log in.
Answered in part on 2026-11-16 (the PI: "i have tacc credits for all services, use the most appropriate"). **The system is Lonestar6** (the orchestrator's choice, from the published queue tables): its `normal` queue gives 128 cores for 1 SU per node-hour, against 48 cores for 1 SU (`skx`), 80 for 1.5 (`icx`) and 112 for 2 (`spr`) on Stampede3, and its 256 GB a node holds a clustering of `rM.2`'s size (178 GB peak, *measured*); the image is x86-64, so Vista (ARM) is out; Horizon is in early operations and has no guide we have read. Kept for later, not for this trial: Stampede3's three `nvdimm` nodes (4 TB of memory, 4 SUs per node-hour) for a clustering that outgrows 256 GB. The project name is read from `/usr/local/etc/taccinfo` at the first login. **How an agent logs in: by SSH, option (a)** (the PI: "i dont know the access, but prefer ssh"): the PI opens one multiplexed connection from the laptop with password and token code (`ssh -fN -M -S ~/.ssh/cm-ls6 -o ControlPersist=12h <TACC username>@ls6.tacc.utexas.edu`), and the agent runs its commands through that socket (`ssh -S ~/.ssh/cm-ls6 ls6.tacc.utexas.edu <command>`), never with a password, a code or a key of its own; when the connection has closed the agent stops and says so. The guide forbids running `ssh-keygen` on Lonestar6. Before that first login the PI needs: the TACC username (TACC's own account, not the UT EID login), an MFA pairing made in the TACC accounts portal, and membership of a project with a Lonestar6 allocation (the guide: "You must be added to a Lonestar6 allocation in order to have access").
(1) A probe, on a login node and in one `development` job: the balance; `module spider apptainer`; the node's CPU model and flags; whether a compute node reaches the internet; whether `systemd-run --user --scope` works there (`run-step` opens a memory-capped scope with it on the pod) and what Slurm gives instead; the quotas; the transfer rate of a 10 GB file from the pod to `$SCRATCH` (rsync over the PI's connection, or Globus).
(2) The image: copy `bio-box_faec1f4.sif` to `$WORK`, check its sha256 against RUNBOOK §0, and run `tests/r0` and `tests/r1` in it in a `development` job, with the tree's `src` on `PYTHONPATH` as on the pod.
(3) **The same bytes on another host** (AGENTS §4.3; the stage plan's cross-host check D-05): scan stage H1's proteins with Pfam 38.2 on one `normal` node, from copies of H1's `protein_seq` file and the Pfam file, with the same plan, shards and merge as the pod's build (`stage domains plan|shard|merge`, the shards as tasks of one job), into `$SCRATCH`. Compare the merged `l1_domains` file with the data root's by sha256 (`8ad24980c97cde1c7da52904073060edccb7bf60613ac27b3c6d0e1da2bf10e0`, card C-24's list) and each shard record with the root's. Record wall time, CPU-minutes, SUs, and CPU-minutes per million residues against the pod's 7.18 (*measured*, card C-24). Nothing produced at TACC goes into the data root in this card.
(4) Report: the measured rate and SUs; from them the cost of the six phase-2 scans, of a release's clustering and of stage S5's scan; what the code would need to run a stage's heavy steps there (a host profile, `run-step` under Slurm without a systemd scope, a launcher for shards and batches in place of `build-stage`'s own processes, moving inputs and results by manifest and sha256, what happens at the 48-hour limit and at the ten-day purge); and the question for the PI that a real use raises: results made on another host entering the data root (AGENTS §7.1 says outputs are produced only through `run-step` in the image on the pod).
Rules at TACC for this card: only the PI's project and only after step (0); **at most 20 SUs in all**, each job's request stated before it is submitted and its charge after; nothing but file management on login nodes; no MFA code, password or key is stored, asked for or worked around by an agent; nothing is kept only on `$SCRATCH` that is not also on the pod; only public genome data and this repository's code go there; AGENTS §8 holds (no fetches from TACC nodes except the listed sources).
Accept: the measurement note with steps (1) to (4); the sha256 comparison of step (3), equal or not, stated; the SUs used.

**Cards C-38 to C-45, and C-48: metagenomic sources beyond the ladder.**
Written on 2026-11-16 at the PI's word ("make cards for all the dbs that i'm missing and think thru how to stage importing them"). The survey and the reasoning are `planning/sources_metagenomic_survey.md` (§2 the sources, §4 the staging). Rules common to all eight: **no byte is fetched from a source until its entry is in `sources.yaml` with the PI's approval** (AGENTS §8), so each card's first step is the proposed entry (exact URL form, files and sizes, the provider's checksum, licence and terms as the provider states them), handed back for approval; no email address, name or project detail goes in any request; a set under JGI's terms is flagged `redistributable = false` and stays local; nothing is built in the data root by these cards (a build is a later card each); every published count is named with its source and checked against the files once they are here.
Amended 2026-11-16 (DEC-50; the PI: "agents can read remaingin providers public pages. they can also fetch data as needed including but not limited to zenomdo", and on JGI "i have login and permissinos, but defer ... remind me later"): for cards C-39 to C-44 the entry is no longer handed back. The agent reads the provider's public pages, writes the entry into `sources.yaml` (exact URL, files, sizes, the provider's checksum, licence or terms as stated, and `approved: under DEC-50`) **before** it fetches, then fetches and checks the checksum. Still in force: no name, email address or project detail in any request; only pages and files open without a login; nothing fetched is executed; each card's limits on scratch and threads. **Any licence may be used** (DEC-51; the PI: "use all data, even restrictive license, bc we're academic/non-profit and have rights to tis"): terms that restrict reuse do not stop a fetch or a use; the entry still records them, with `redistributable: false` where they limit redistribution, as a record for the day a release is shared. **No agent tries a JGI source** (IMG/VR, IMG/PR, IMG/M, GOLD): they wait for the PI, and each card says what it could not do without them. Four entries were approved by the PI directly and are in `sources.yaml`: `uhgv`, `gsv_atlas`, `spire_tables`, `ncbi_metagenome_assembly_summary` (`planning/measurements/2026-11-16_proposed_sources.md`).
Amended 2026-11-18 (PI logged into JGI/GOLD in Chrome, accepted the JGI Data Utilization Policy, and asked to enumerate all JGI datasets, pull the largest useful collections and environmental metadata onto `wilkcomp02`, and card the staged harvest of the rest as the DuckDB corpus progresses): nine new `sources.yaml` entries are approved (`jgi_gold_downloads`, `jgi_img_m_tables`, `jgi_img_vr`, `jgi_img_pr`, `jgi_img_umag`, `jgi_iuig`, `jgi_inovirus`, `jgi_gems_nersc`, `gvmags_v2`), streaming onto `/stor/work/Wilke/if2453/all-the-data/jgi_20261118/`; cards **C-48** (`C-48a` to `C-48e`) govern locking those archives, joining their environmental metadata into DuckDB dimension/crosswalk tables, and progressively staging and building the viral, plasmid, MAG/isolate, tape-purged JGI, and unbinned metagenomic datasets into the rolling release line.

### C-38 Design: importing at a stated depth, and annotating on the hit
**H** (a design note first, for the PI to decide), then **M** cards from it · Depends: none; it uses the measurements of C-37 and C-42 as they arrive · the survey's §4
Files: `planning/design/C-38_import_depths.md` (new), nothing else
Why: unbinned metagenomic contigs cannot be annotated in full (the survey's §4.1: about 20 times the cost of calling genes; 1.2 years of the pod for SPIRE's 16 Tbp, *derived*), and they do not fit the pod's local disk (0.71 GB per Gbp at the depth of called genes, *derived* from H1). Today a stage is all or nothing.
Do, as a design note in the form of `planning/design/B-47_inferred_domains.md`: (1) a stage's **depth** (stored; called: genes, proteins, tapes, CRISPR arrays; clustered; annotated) as a recorded property, and what a release, a search pack, `release verify` and the manifest do with stages of different depth; (2) **annotation on the hit**: a search over called proteins, then Pfam on the proteins within N genes of each hit, where those rows live (AGENTS §3.10: a stage never rewrites a file, so a new partition kind and its scope rule), how a reader tells a protein without domains from one never scanned; (3) families without Pfam: a linear clustering of all proteins and neighbour statistics on cluster tokens, with what is lost against the cascade we use; (4) the filter at ingest (a least length or gene count), with the dropped bases in the ledger; (5) nucleotides by reference for unbinned contigs (proteins, coordinates and arrays kept; the sequence refetchable by source, sample, contig and sha256): what breaks in the spec's invariants and in the tools that read sequence; (6) where a corpus of several TB lives (a larger footprint on `/stor/scratch`, TACC storage, or the depth-1 part held apart from the data root); (7) the stopping rule by habitat (new NR50 families per Gbp). Each with options, what was measured, a recommendation, and the questions only the PI can answer.
Amended 2026-11-16 (DEC-49; the PI: "keep 1-4 , i will think about 5-9, but i think i'd like to keep nucleotides too (5)"): **the note designs items (1) to (4), which are decided in principle.** Item (5) is dropped as a proposal: nucleotides are kept, so the note plans storage at the full figure and only records what the reference form would have saved. Items (6) and (7), and the survey's ideas (7) and (8), are the PI's to think about: the note lays out the options and what each needs measured, and recommends nothing that depends on them. The note may be written now; it does not wait for cards C-37 or C-42, and says which of its numbers they will replace.
Done 2026-11-17 (STATUS row C-38): the note is `planning/design/C-38_import_depths.md`; its fourteen questions were confirmed as recommended by DEC-54; its follow-on cards C-38a to C-38h follow below.
Accept: the note; its list of questions for the PI; the cards that follow from the recommendation, as text for this file.

### C-45 Decisions on what stays out
**H** (the PI's) · Depends: none
**Amended 2026-11-20 (DEC-59): nothing here "stays out" for good.** The goal is a complete catalog, and what is not taken now is deferred and listed in `planning/CATALOG_GOAL.md` §4. Question (2) below is read as "in, later"; (4) stands. **Answered 2026-11-20 (DEC-60 (2)): (1) RNA viruses are in, by design card C-50; (3) protein-only catalogues "can wait" (deferred).**
1. **RNA viruses** (LucaProt's 161,979 species, Serratus' RdRps, RVMT): everything built assumes DNA; taking them means a gene caller and a token model for RNA genomes. In, later, or out?
2. **Strain-level sets** (AllTheBacteria's 2.44 million isolates; the full MAG sets behind GlobDB's species, such as the mOTUs database's 2.83 million MAGs): they multiply copies of species we hold. The stage plan's S6 already takes every GTDB genome; take these too, only for chosen species, or not at all?
3. **Protein-only catalogues** (MGnify's proteins, the global gene catalogues): proteins without their neighbours, so they serve homology and not context. A search-only pack beside the corpus, or out?
4. **The JGI sets** (IMG/VR, IMG/PR, IMG/M): held local and never redistributed, as the plan says for S4. Confirm for each as its card comes up.

### C-49 The PI's NAS `tower` as a place for tranches: what it holds and how data reaches it
**M** · Depends: none · DEC-60 (3), STATUS open item 19 · needs the PI for step (3)

Why: the data root is on the pod's local disk (1.1 TB in all, cleared at a reboot), its durable copy and the source archives are on the group's `/stor/work` (1.2 TB of ours, where the footprint draft says under 50 GB), and SPIRE alone at 5 kb is about 3 TB at depth `called`. The PI offers a NAS: "i have a nas called tower, and as we grow, maybe tranches can move there?". Read from the PI's laptop on 2026-11-20 (*measured*, `df`, `nproc`, `free`): the array is 32 TB with 18 TB free, a cache disk of 954 GB with 242 GB free, 8 cores, 31 GB of memory. So it can hold tranches and cannot build or cluster them (the pod has 112 cores and a 200 GB cap per job). It is on the PI's home network, which the pod cannot reach; the laptop reaches both.
Do: (1) **What can live there.** For each kind of file (source archives; a stage's files; a release's files; packs; the durable tars), say whether moving it to `tower` breaks anything: which commands read it and how often, whether a search or `nbr` needs it on the machine that computes, and what a release that lists files in two places would need from the manifest and the profile (AGENTS §3.9: the manifest is the record). Propose tiers in `planning/design/C-49_tiers.md` (for example: working root on the pod; older releases' large files and stages outside the newest release on `tower`; `tower` as the second durable copy), with what each costs in transfers. No code. (2) **How data would get there**, with one small measured transfer for each route that needs no new credential: laptop as relay (`rsync` pod → laptop → `tower`), rate in MB/s both hops (*measured*). Routes that need the PI: `tower` pulling from the pod through `wilkcomp03` with a key of its own. Not a route: anything installed or listening on the pod (AGENTS §7.5, §8). For each: rate, what must stay switched on, how a transfer is verified (sha256 on both ends) and resumed. From the rate, the days to move 100 GB, 1 TB and 3 TB (*derived*). (3) **For the PI to set up or decide**, listed and not done by the agent: which share and directory on `tower`; a key for the pull route, restricted to read the data root and the backup directory; whether data whose terms say "not redistributed" (the JGI sets, DEC-51) may sit on a personal NAS; what happens to the copy on `/stor/work` once `tower` holds one (agents do not delete under `/stor`).
Accept: the note with the measured rates and the capacities; the tier proposal; the list for the PI. Nothing of the corpus is copied to `tower` by this card beyond the test files, which are removed.
Done 2026-11-20 (STATUS row C-49): `scripts/orchestrator/relay_to_tower.sh` with its tests, the tier proposal `planning/design/C-49_tiers.md` (built stages cannot live only on `tower`; it is the second durable copy and a home for archives, inputs and lists), the note `planning/measurements/2026-11-20_tower_relay.md`. Owed by the orchestrator: the script's first real use and its measured rate, and the rest of the first mirror's rows in the note.
First measurements and the PI's answers (2026-11-20, the orchestrator, from the laptop; the card's design half is still to do): (a) **the PI: data whose terms say "not redistributed" may sit on `tower`** ("3. yes"); **"pulling via laptop is fine"**; the share, the directory and a key wait on this card's report. (b) The laptop has 26 GiB free (*measured*, `df`), so a relay must stream and never land a file there: `scp -3 -F <config>` with a config naming the pod (through `wilkcomp03`) and `tower` does that in one command. (c) One file, one stream, pod → laptop → `tower`: `bio-box_faec1f4.sif`, 821,764,096 bytes in 84.75 s, **9.70 MB/s** (*measured*; *derived*: bytes / seconds), written to a temporary directory in the share `research-bulk` and read back there with sha256 `d67300aa…f098a`, equal to the pod's; the file and directory were then removed. At that rate (*derived*): 100 GB in 2.9 hours, 1 TB in 29 hours, 3 TB in 3.6 days, with the laptop awake and on the home network throughout. Which hop limits it, and whether parallel streams add, is not measured. (d) `tower`'s shares (*measured*, read-only): `research-bulk` has the cache off (writes go to the array: three data disks with 1.8, 7.3 and 8.8 TB free) and already holds other projects' bulk data; `research` prefers the 954 GB cache disk (242 GB free), which a large copy would fill. The orchestrator's suggestion to the PI: `/mnt/user/research-bulk/bio-box/` with `root_backups/` (the durable tars, first), `sources/` (archives after their stages are built) and `tranches/` (stage files moved off the pod, once the tier design says how a release names them).

### C-50 RNA viruses, Obelisks, and viroid-like circular RNAs: sources, gene calling and tokens for RNA genomes
**H** (a design card; the PI decides its questions) · Depends: none · DEC-60 (2), DEC-69, card C-45 question (1)
Files: `planning/design/C-50_rna_viruses.md` (new); no code
Why: RNA viruses, Obelisks, and viroid-like circular RNAs are in scope (DEC-60 (2); expanded by DEC-69 on 2026-11-23). Everything built assumes DNA genomes: the gene caller's modes (`single`, `meta`, `gv-meta`), `genome_input.kind` (`prokaryote`, `virus`, `plasmid`), Pfam on proteins called from one strand and frame model, tapes of genes in order on a contig. RNA virus genomes are short, often segmented, often one or two polyproteins, many sets are RdRp-centred contigs from metatranscriptomes, and Obelisks (~1 kb circular RNAs encoding Oblins) and viroid-like cccRNAs (~0.2–5 kb circular RNAs with hammerhead/HDV/twister ribozymes and ambisense or overlapping ORFs such as HepD-like antigens) need circular topology and ribozyme/ncRNA + short-ORF handling.
Do: (1) the sources (`LucaProt`'s 161,979 RdRp species, `Serratus` RdRp contigs, `RVMT`, RefSeq RNA viruses, Zheludev et al. 2024 global metatranscriptomic Obelisks (~30,000 Obelisks/Oblins, *published*), Lee/Neri et al. 2023 viroid-like cccRNAs/ribozymes (~11,000+ elements, *published*), **`Ambiviruses` / *Ambiviricota*** [Forgia et al. 2023 *Nature*, `4–5 kb` circular ssRNA fungal viruses encoding both RdRp and self-cleaving ribozymes, *published*], **`ViroidDB`** [Lee/Neri/Koonin/Simmonds `viroids.org`, `~10,000` viroids/satRNAs/ribozyviruses/retrozymes, *published*], **`ELGV`** [2024 Early-Life Gut Virome, `160,478` infant gut DNA+RNA viral sequences, *published* — RNA fraction here, DNA delta checked against `V1`], and newer metatranscriptomic RNA virus/viroid compendia): for each, what it holds (genomes, segments, circular RNAs, contigs, proteins only), size, licence, how it overlaps the others, and what a non-redundant take is and what it leaves (DEC-59 (3)). (2) What a stage of RNA / cccRNA genomes needs: the genome kind(s), segments as contigs of one genome, circular RNA origin-wrapping ORFs, the gene caller (what the pinned tools give on RNA virus and Obelisk/cccRNA genomes against the providers' own calls, measured on a sample; polyproteins and their mature peptides; frameshifts; ambisense/overlapping ORFs), genetic codes, ribozyme CM models (`Infernal`), and what a neighbourhood means on a 1–10 kb genome. (3) What search and tokens need: Pfam/NeoRdRp/Oblin/ribozyme coverage, whether tokens by domain family carry over. (4) Questions for the PI, each with a recommendation, and the cards that would follow.
Accept: the design note; the PI's answers recorded as a decision row.

**M** · Pod: yes (tests, and one plan in a scratch view; read-only on the data root) · Depends: B-47 (question 2, answered by DEC-35) · Design: `planning/design/B-47_inferred_domains.md` §2b, §5 · copied here from the note's §10 on 2026-11-15, with the orchestrator's additions marked
Files: `src/biobox/l1/domains.py` (the plan leaves out proteins measured in an earlier stage with the same HMM sha256, cutoff and tool versions; the merge copies their rows; the plan names the files copied from, with sha256), `src/biobox/stage.py` (passes the root's other stages, as `--seq-stages`), `src/biobox/cli.py` only for the option on the `stage domains` actions, `RUNBOOK.md` only where a command it shows gains the option, tests in `tests/r1` and `tests/r2`, one measurement note
Do, test first: two stages sharing proteins, built in either order, give byte-identical `l1_domains` files; a copied row equals the row of a full scan of that protein (`hmmsearch` binary as oracle); a source scanned with another HMM file or pyhmmer version is not copied. The scanned and copied counts go in the plan and shard records only (`domain_scan`, never in a manifest, not in the ledger).
Added by the orchestrator: (1) "measured in an earlier stage" is decided from that stage's scanned proteins and its completed domain step (its plan and run record), never from the presence of rows: a protein scanned with no hit has no row and is measured. (2) A source stage counts only when its recorded HMM sha256, cutoff, Z and tool versions equal this scan's; say which recorded fields are compared. (3) The shards and the CPU estimate before launch are made from the residues actually scanned. (4) A stage that shares no protein with a named stage, and a stage built with no other stage named, give `l1_domains` and ledger files byte-identical to today's; report whether the fixture's manifest hash changes (expected: it does not). (5) Bounded memory (AGENTS §4.1, §4.2): the set of already measured proteins is found by a streamed or sorted anti-join on `protein_uid`, not a Python set of a stage's proteins; a memory test on the 100× synthetic store with the peak RSS stated. (6) When several named stages measured a protein, the source is the first in the order given, and the rows are equal whichever is taken (test it). (7) Rows that a later card marks as inferred (`inferred_from`, card B-47c) are not measured and are never copied: leave the place for that filter and say where it is.
Accept: the tests; the measurement note, from the pod, read-only on the data root and with no scan run: the plan of stage H1 in a scratch view that names S1 and P1 to P6, its count of proteins to copy against the 5,612 distinct proteins H1 shares with them (*measured*, card C-24), and the rows it would copy against H1's own rows of the same proteins in the root (H1 scanned them in full, so this checks on real data that a copy equals a scan; any difference is reported, not explained away).
Done 2026-11-15 (STATUS row B-47b; `planning/measurements/2026-11-15_b47b_copy_plan_H1.md`): `build-stage` runs a scan as plan, shards and merge whenever another stage on disk measured proteins with the same scan (HMM file sha256, cutoff, Z, tool versions), and the plan copies their rows. On the root, H1's plan copies 5,612 proteins and their 1,787 rows equal H1's own (*measured*). A stage scanned in one process records no plan and is never a source. The places left for card B-47c's inferred rows are `domains._measured_uids` and `domains._measured_rows`.

The other synthworld stages have the same kind of bound. Separately, the examples' run instructions still name the `64f1bf0` images.

### A-09 The pod scripts reach wilkcomp02 through a jump host
**L** · found 2026-11-20 by the orchestrator's audit of the 2026-11-17 to 20 campaign
Files: `scripts/pod_pytest.sh`, `scripts/orchestrator/watch_until.sh`, `scripts/orchestrator/watch_ladder.sh` (their `ssh` and `rsync` calls only), `tests/r1/test_pod_pytest_jump.py` (new), `RUNBOOK.md` section 8 (one paragraph)
Why: since the maintenance of 2026-11-17 the pod refuses SSH on port 22 from off campus (`ssh: connect to host wilkcomp02.ccbb.utexas.edu port 22: Connection refused`, *measured*, the PI's laptop, 2026-11-20) and is reached through `wilkcomp03` (`ssh -J if2453@wilkcomp03.ccbb.utexas.edu if2453@wilkcomp02.ccbb.utexas.edu`). `pod_pytest.sh` and the two watch scripts call `ssh` and `rsync -e ssh` with the host alone, so from off campus they fail, and sessions fall back to hand-typed `rsync` and `ssh` lines that the scripts were written to replace (AGENTS §5.5 names `pod_pytest.sh` as the way to run tests).
Do, test first: an optional environment variable `BIOBOX_POD_JUMP` (one `user@host`), passed as `-J` to every `ssh` call and inside the `rsync -e` command of the three scripts. Unset or empty, every command line is what it is today. The test puts stub `ssh` and `rsync` executables first on `PATH` that record their argument lists and exit 0 (no network, no pod): with the variable set every recorded call carries `-J <value>`; unset, none does; a value holding whitespace or a shell metacharacter is refused with exit 2 before any call. RUNBOOK section 8 says when to set it.
Accept: the test fails first and passes; from off campus, `env BIOBOX_POD_JUMP=if2453@wilkcomp03.ccbb.utexas.edu sh scripts/pod_pytest.sh A-09 tests/r1/test_pod_pytest_jump.py` passes on the pod.
Done 2026-11-20 (STATUS row A-09): the three scripts take `BIOBOX_POD_JUMP`; 262 passed on the merged tree through the new path (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-20).

Evidence (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-20, stage `C5`'s run records, `planning/measurements/2026-11-20_stage_C5.md`): the near-Cas CRISPR producer cost 0.758 of its estimate and the default producer 0.837, on 2,653 contigs and 9.11 Gbp of complete prokaryote genomes. Card B-53 fitted `CRISPR_CPU_S_PER_GBP` and `CRISPR_NEAR_CAS_COST` with plasmid and circular-virus stages in view, where many short contigs carry a per-contig cost.
Do, test first: bring both producers' estimates within 15% of their run records on every built stage (`S1`, `P1` to `P6`, `H1`, `PL1`, `V1`, `C5`: the records are in the root's `runs/`, read-only), by whatever term the data supports (a per-contig term beside the per-base one is the first thing to try); no built stage's published file or plan changes. State each stage's ratio before and after.
Accept: the test fails first on `C5`'s near-Cas record (over 20% off) and passes; every built stage within 15% for both producers, or the stages that cannot be brought in are named with the reason.
Closed 2026-11-21 with the stage named and no constant changed (STATUS row B-60; `planning/measurements/2026-11-21_crispr_estimate_refit.md`): ten of the eleven built stages are within 10% for both producers; `C5` is not (0.837 and 0.758) and no term known before launch brings it in (a per-contig term moves it from 0.851 to 0.866; every near-Cas model leaves it at 0.75 to 0.78 by leave-one-out). Lowering the rate to follow `C5` would put `S1` and `P1` near 1.2. The estimate stays high for stages like `C5` (DEC-53: recorded, not stopped). To be looked at again when a second stage of complete strain genomes is built (`C1` to `C4`, `C6`): if they also read under 150 CPU-seconds per Gbp, `C5` is a class and gets a constant.

### A-11 Stage and release drivers lose a failed mode's exit status
**L** · Depends: C-47 (its fix is the model) · found 2026-11-20: card C-47's `check` failed and its queue went on
Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/c48c_v1_driver.sh`, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/release_train.sh`, `scripts/orchestrator/rel_wait.sh`, any other driver under `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/` with the same form (list them in the report), `tests/r1/test_driver_exit_status.py` (new)
Why: `c47_driver.sh check` exited 0 after its check script had exited 1, because the script ends `case "$MODE" in ... esac` followed by a `log` line, whose status became the script's; the queue behind it ran `backup` and wrote "all passed", and a waiter started a release on that line (STATUS "6m"). Card C-47 fixed its own driver and queue. `c48c_v1_driver.sh` has the same final line (its `check` happens to go through `fail`), and it ignores the status of the hashing in `snapshot` and of `bio-box releases`. `rel_wait.sh` trusts a log line of another script.
Do, test first: for each driver, a test with stub commands on `PATH` (as `tests/r1/test_relay_to_tower.py` does) in which each mode's inner command fails in turn: the driver must exit non-zero and must not write that mode's success marker. Then: every mode's status is the script's exit status; each mode writes `<mode>/DONE` only on success and refuses to run without the markers of the modes before it; anything that waits on another script tests the marker, never a log line or an exit status alone. No change to what any mode does on success.
Accept: the tests fail first on each driver that has the fault and pass after; `tests/r0` green; the drivers' success paths byte-identical in what they launch (show it with the rehearsal's dry outputs or a `--dry-run`).
Done 2026-11-21 (STATUS row A-11): nine drivers had it (`c24`, `c26`, `c33`, `c34`, `c35`, `c48c_v1`, `b48`, `b49`, `release_train.sh`) and `rel_wait.sh` trusted a log line; `tests/r1/test_driver_exit_status.py` (100 tests on stub commands; 55 failed before). `release_train.sh run` now writes `$WORK/DONE` with the release's name, last, only when every step passed. **The copy of `release_train.sh` running on the pod on 2026-11-21 (`/tmp/if2453/ci/rel1120`) is the old one: it writes no `DONE`, so `r26.11.2`'s end is judged from its log, its check's output and the orchestrator's own verify.**

### A-13 The release train can carry a record accepted by a decision row, and has none
**M** · Depends: C-46 · DEC-43, DEC-53, DEC-55, DEC-56 · found 2026-11-21: release `r26.11.2` stopped on its rate guard (STATUS open item 23) and `train_est.py` has no way to carry the PI's answer
Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/train_est.py`, `tests/r1/test_c46_release_train.py` or a new `tests/r1/test_a13_train_accepted.py`, `RUNBOOK.md` ("Cutting the next release": what to do after a guard stop)
Why: a guard stop is lifted only by the PI's word, written as a decision row. Card C-35's driver carried DEC-55 as code (`c35_est.py`: two step prefixes up to 1.60 on `r26.11.1`), card C-47's carried DEC-56 as one exact record, and card A-12's stage driver reads accepted records from a stage's conf. `train_est.py rates` exits 3 on any judged step over 1.20 and has no such table, so a release that stops can be resumed only by editing the guard by hand at night.
Do, test first: a table `ACCEPTED` in `train_est.py`, **empty in this card's commit**: each entry names the release, a step pattern (`fnmatch` on the step's name, such as `cluster.nr50.20.align.part-*`), the greatest ratio accepted, and the decision row (`DEC-n`). A judged step over 1.20 that an entry matches (its release, its step, its ratio at or under the entry's bound) is reported under `accepted_over_20pct` with the row's name and does not stop the train; everything else is judged as now. With the table empty the output of `rates` is unchanged to the byte on recorded inputs (pin it). Tests with an entry injected by `monkeypatch`: accepted at and under the bound; stopped above it; stopped for another release; stopped for another step at the same ratio; a step under 0.80 is unaffected. A test in the repository's own state: every entry's `DEC-n` has a row in `planning/DECISIONS.md` (it passes on the empty table and would fail on an entry without a row). No environment variable, flag or file outside the script can add an entry.
Accept: the tests, passing where MinCED, MMseqs2 and hmmsearch are absent; `rates` on the real stopped release, read-only (`/tmp/if2453/c46/train`, the root's run records), still exits 3 and names `cluster.nr50.20.align.part-00001` at 1.215. **This card accepts nothing.** The entry for `r26.11.2`, if the PI gives the word, is the orchestrator's commit with the decision row, and the train is resumed from a tree that carries it.
Done 2026-11-21 (STATUS row A-13): the table, empty; `rates` on the stopped release still exits 3 with output byte-identical to the train's own last record.

These genomes carry prokaryotic-immune homologs and ancestors (cGAS/STING, viperins, gasdermins, Argonautes, TIR/NLRs, HNH/endoVII nucleases) and host-virus horizontal gene transfers with `GV1`/`EGV1`/`MIR1`, while requiring spliceosomal intron handling, multi-exon `feature` coordinates on tapes, and calibrated gene-calling / Pfam budgets.

### B-67 `--genecall-mode gv-meta` for unbinned metagenome stages (`DEC-71`)
**L** · Pod: yes (pytest only; zero writes to data root or `/stor`) · Depends: B-48, B-63, A-12 · DEC-71, DEC-13
Files: `src/biobox/l1/genecall.py`, `src/biobox/stage.py`, `src/biobox/cli.py`, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stage_driver.sh`, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stage_est.py`, `RUNBOOK.md`, `tests/r1/test_b67_genecall_mode_gvmeta.py` (new)
Why: In `src/biobox/l1/genecall.py`, viral stages (`kind == "virus"`) run `pyrodigal_gv.ViralGeneFinder(meta=True)` (`gv-meta`, calling `code 4`/`code 15` per gene alongside all 50 standard bacterial/archaeal Prodigal meta models), whereas unbinned metagenome contigs staged as 1-contig `prokaryote` genomes run standard code-11 `pyrodigal.GeneFinder(meta=True)` below `100 kb` and single-genome code-11 training (`finder.train(..., translation_table=11)`) at `>= 100 kb` (`mode_of`). On unbinned metagenomes (`LR1`.., `SPX2`..), TAG/TGA-recoded PBS1/crAss/*Bacillus* jumbophage contigs (`code 15`/`code 4`) and recoded CPR bacteria — prime compartments for tandem `ART-RT`s and mobile defense islands — get truncated or mis-trained at `>= 100 kb`. Adding an explicit `--genecall-mode default|gv-meta` option keeps `kind = "prokaryote"` (`schemas.py` and `FIXTURE_HASH` untouched), keeps `GENE_CALL_VERSION` (`pyrodigal-3.7.1-gv-0.3.2-p2`) and every already-built stage (`S1..SPX1`) byte-identical (`default`), and prices/runs `gv-meta` cleanly with `CALL_S_PER_MBP[("gv-meta", ...)]` and `GENES_PER_MBP["gv-meta"]`.
Do, test first (`tests/r1/test_b67_genecall_mode_gvmeta.py` seen failing before code edits; AGENTS §3.10, §4.1, §4.3, §5.1): (1) Write R1 tests proving: (a) with `genecall_mode="default"` (omitted), `mode_of`, `plan`, `run`, `run_batch`, `build-stage`, and `stage_est.py` produce **byte-identical** plans, estimates, and Parquet files on `< 100 kb` and `>= 100 kb` `prokaryote`, `plasmid`, and `virus` genomes; (b) on a constructed `>= 120 kb` TAG-recoded (`code 15`) jumbophage contig staged as `GenomeInput(kind="prokaryote")`, `default` fragments the recoded ORF under code-11 single training while `genecall_mode="gv-meta"` calls the intact full-length ORF (`genetic_code == 15`, `mode == "gv-meta"`), matching `ViralGeneFinder(meta=True)` 1:1 across 1-process and batched runs; (c) `genecall_plan` records `"genecall_mode": "gv-meta"` only when `"gv-meta"` is chosen (omitting the key on `"default"` so existing plan hashes never move!), `stage.py` / `cli.py` accept `--genecall-mode default|gv-meta` on `stage genecall` and `build-stage`, and `stage_driver.sh` / `stage_est.py` parse optional `GENECALL_MODE=gv-meta` from `<STAGE>.conf` and price `before` + `rates` with `gv-meta`'s rates. (2) Implement cleanly and run `pod_pytest.sh` (normal and tools-hidden PATH on `test_b67_genecall_mode_gvmeta.py`, `test_b63*`, `test_stage_driver.py`, `tests/r0`) on `wilkcomp02`.
Accept: new tests fail first and pass (normal and tools-hidden PATH); `default` mode leaves all existing plans/estimates/outputs byte-identical; `GENECALL_MODE=gv-meta` works end-to-end in `stage_driver.sh` / `stage_est.py` and `tests/r0` passes.
Done 2026-11-24 (STATUS row B-67): `--genecall-mode default|gv-meta` wired through `genecall.py`, `stage.py`, `cli.py`, `stage_driver.sh`, `stage_est.py`, and `RUNBOOK.md` (`default` leaves plans, estimates, and Parquet files byte-identical without writing `"genecall_mode"`; `gv-meta` calls and prices every genome with `pyrodigal-gv` meta models, matching `ViralGeneFinder(meta=True)` 1:1 on `>= 120 kb` TAG-recoded `code 15` jumbophage contigs across 1-process and batched runs; `3,612 passed, 24 skipped` across 2 shards on `wilkcomp02`).

### B-69 Rescue `code 25` (`UGA -> Gly`) CPR contigs in `--genecall-mode gv-meta` (`DEC-71`)
**L** · Pod: yes (pytest only; zero writes to data root or `/stor`) · Depends: B-67, C-63 · DEC-71
Files: `src/biobox/l1/genecall.py`, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stage_est.py`, `tests/r1/test_b69_gvmeta_code25.py` (new), `tests/r1/test_b67_genecall_mode_gvmeta.py` (migrate `GENECALL_MODES` tuple assertion)
Why: `pyrodigal-gv` (`ViralGeneFinder(meta=True)`, added for unbinned metagenomes via `--genecall-mode gv-meta` in `B-67`) evaluates standard `code 11` plus viral `code 4` (`UGA -> Trp`) and `code 15` (`UAG -> Gln`), **not `code 25` (`UGA -> Gly`)** used by *Patescibacteriota*/*Gracilibacteria*/*Absconditabacteria* (a core `Node N0.5` proto-ART and DGR clade). On an unbinned `code 25` CPR contig in `LR1`/`LR2`/`SPX2`, `gv-meta` selects `code 4` (`UGA -> Trp`), substituting bulky `Trp` at every conserved `UGA`-encoded catalytic/structural `Gly` in RT palm/fingers motifs and depressing HMM bitscores! Adding an opt-in `--genecall-mode gv-meta-c25` (or refining a contig called predominantly as `code 4` by checking whether `pyrodigal` `translation_table=25` vs `4` yields conserved `Gly` at `UGA` / higher score under a new mode name so built `gv-meta` stages stay byte-identical!) preserves intact `Gly` translations on unbinned `code 25` CPR contigs.
Do, test first: construct a `>= 20 kb` `UGA -> Gly` (`code 25`) CPR contig carrying an RT with conserved palm glycines encoded by `TGA`; show `gv-meta` translates internal `TGA` as `W` (`code 4`) whereas `gv-meta-c25` translates them as `G` (`code 25`, `genetic_code == 25`) while leaving `code 11`, true `code 4` (*Mycoplasma*/phage), and `code 15` jumbophage contigs unchanged and keeping `default` and `gv-meta` byte-identical.
Accept: new R1 tests fail first and pass (normal and tools-hidden PATH); `tests/r0` and `test_b67*` green.
Done 2026-11-24 (STATUS row B-69): `--genecall-mode gv-meta-c25` added with a GC-invariant Trp-only binomial LLR discriminator rescuing `code 25` (`UGA -> Gly`) CPR contigs while leaving `code 4`, `code 15`, `code 11`, `default`, and `gv-meta` 100% byte-identical (`3,650 passed, 24 skipped` across 2 shards on `wilkcomp02`).

### B-70 Normalize MMseqs2 `0M` empty member alignments in `cluster_member_aln` and `contracts.check_coordinates`
**L** · Pod: yes (pytest only; zero writes to `/tmp/if2453/r10/root` or `/stor`) · Depends: B-41 · found 2026-11-24 when `r26.11.3` `release cluster finish` (`102,337,875` proteins) refused `2` `0M` rows at `cluster_member_aln` writing
Files: `src/biobox/l2/cluster.py`, `src/biobox/contracts.py`, `tests/r1/test_b70_empty_member_aln.py` (new), `tests/r2/test_micro_s0_contracts.py`, `tests/r2/test_micro_s0_cluster.py` (only if `_walk`/helper check is extended for `alnlen == 0`)
Why: On `r26.11.3` (`102,337,875` distinct proteins, *measured*, `wilkcomp02`, 2026-11-24), `mmseqs align db db clu<90|50> aln_<nr90|nr50> -a -e inf --min-seq-id 0 -c 0 --alignment-mode 3` emitted `2` rows out of `102,337,875` (`80107049` `58` aa and `38179080` `38` aa against a `104` aa representative) where Smith-Waterman found no positive-scoring local alignment: raw MMseqs2 alignment lines carry `qStartPos = -1, qEndPos = 0, dbStartPos = -1, dbEndPos = -1, cigar = "0M"` (plus uninitialized `seqId`/`eval` floats), which `mmseqs convertalis` converts (+1) to `qstart = 0, qend = 1, tstart = 0, tend = 0, alnlen = 0, cigar = "0M"`. `cluster._write_tables` (`src/biobox/l2/cluster.py:504`) then computes `tstart - 1 = -1` and `qstart - 1 = -1`, failing `io.write_parts`'s `uint32` cast (`Integer value -1 not in range: 0 to 4294967295`), and `contracts.check_coordinates` (`src/biobox/contracts.py:295`) checks `a.qstart < a.qend AND a.tstart < a.tend` without a canonical empty-alignment branch.
Do, test first: (1) write R1 tests reproducing `pick_<lvl>.parquet` rows with `convertalis` `0M` output (`qstart=0, qend=1, tstart=0, tend=0, fident=0.5, alnlen=0, cigar="0M"` and `fident=0.0`) alongside normal `alnlen > 0` rows, plus `check_coordinates` fault-injection tests in `test_micro_s0_contracts.py`, and see them fail first; (2) in `src/biobox/l2/cluster.py` (`alignments()`), when `alnlen <= 0 OR cigar = '0M' OR qstart <= 0 OR tstart <= 0`, emit canonical empty 0-based half-open alignment values (`qstart = 0, qend = 0, tstart = 0, tend = 0, pident = 0.0, alnlen = 0, cigar = b""`), leaving every `alnlen > 0` row and `PLAN_FORMAT = 1` **100% byte-identical** so `r26.11.3`'s existing `cluster.plan` and `275.9 GB` scratch DBs resume directly at `release cluster finish`; (3) in `src/biobox/contracts.py` (`check_coordinates`), accept either a valid non-empty alignment (`a.alnlen > 0 AND a.qstart < a.qend AND a.qend <= m.length AND a.tstart < a.tend AND a.tend <= r.length AND octet_length(a.cigar) > 0`) or the canonical empty alignment (`a.alnlen = 0 AND a.qstart = 0 AND a.qend = 0 AND a.tstart = 0 AND a.tend = 0 AND a.pident = 0 AND octet_length(a.cigar) = 0`), rejecting non-canonical `alnlen = 0` rows (e.g. `cigar = b"0M"`, `qend > 0`, or `pident != 0`).
Accept: new R1 and R2 tests fail first and pass on `wilkcomp02` (normal and tools-hidden PATH); `micro_s0` cluster output bytes are 100% unchanged; whole test suite passes on `wilkcomp02` (`--shards 2`).
Done 2026-11-24 (STATUS row B-70): `cluster._write_tables` (`PLAN_FORMAT = 1` unchanged) and `contracts.check_coordinates` normalize and validate MMseqs2 `0M` empty member alignments while keeping `alnlen > 0` output 100% byte-identical (`3,715 passed, 24 skipped` across 8 shards + `49 passed` tools-hidden on `wilkcomp02`).

### C-59a Stage eukaryotic mitochondrial & cytoplasmic linear plasmids (`LPL1`) and add multi-code `transl_table` routing to `C-59` (`O1`) *(later rolling releases — `ssRNAP` & organellar RT track)*
**M** · Pod: yes (`sources.yaml` entries before fetch under `DEC-50`; stage `LPL1` and multi-code `O1` in `/tmp/if2453/s2/inputs/`; read-only on `/tmp/if2453/r10/root`) · Depends: C-59, A-12 · DEC-69, DEC-50, DEC-26, DEC-27
Files: `sources.yaml`, `planning/CATALOG_GOAL.md`, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/c59_refseq_organelles.py`, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/c59a_lpl1_o2.py` (optional new helper), `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stages/LPL1.conf` (new), `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stages/O2.conf` (new, keeping `O1.conf` untouched), `src/biobox/search/pack.py` (only if `dark_orf` 6-frame translation needs a clean fallback mapping for mitochondrial genetic codes absent from `pyhmmer.easel.GeneticCode`, matching how code `15` is handled), `tests/r1/test_c59_refseq_organelles.py`, `tests/r1/test_c59a_lpl1_o2.py` (optional new test file), `tests/r1/test_stage_driver.py`, `planning/measurements/<date>_lpl1_o1_inputs.md`
Why: Fungal (*Neurospora* `kalilo`/`maranhar`, *Podospora* `pAL2-1`, *Claviceps* `pClK1`, *Agaricus* `pEM`), plant (*Zea mays* `S-1`/`S-2`, *Brassica*), oomycete, and slime-mold (`Physarum` `mF`) **mitochondrial linear plasmids ("invertrons")** encode some of the most divergent palm-fold single-subunit RNA polymerases (`ssRNAP`s, `< 20–30%` identity to T7/POLRMT) alongside protein-primed PolB DNAPs and reverse transcriptases, while yeast cytoplasmic killer linear plasmids (*Kluyveromyces* `pGKL1`/`pGKL2`, *Pichia*, *Debaryomyces*) encode autonomous cytoplasmic single-chain/split `DPBB` RNAP + mRNA-capping systems. **100% of these are absent from `PLSDB` (`PL1`, prokaryote-only)**, sit in NCBI RefSeq `plasmid/` or GenBank eukaryotic linear-plasmid records outside `refseq/release/mitochondrion/`, and require **mitochondrial translation tables (`3, 4, 16, 22`, where `UGA = Trp` in Table 4!)** — under Table 11 their `rpo` ORFs shatter at internal `TGA` codons! Meanwhile, fungal, plant, and protist mtDNAs in `C-59` (`O1`) carry thousands of integrated and free-standing linear-plasmid-derived `rpo` and group II intron RT ORFs requiring the same per-genome `transl_table` routing (`1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 9, 11, 13, 14, 16, 21, 22, 23`).
Do, test first: (1) write `sources.yaml` entries (`approved: under DEC-50`) for NCBI RefSeq `plasmid` eukaryotic records + GenBank eukaryotic `linear` mitochondrial/cytoplasmic plasmids; (2) verify every mitochondrial `transl_table` used by `LPL1` (`< 0.1 Gbp`, `~500–2,000` records, *estimate*) and `O1` (`~2–4 Gbp`) in `pyrodigal` **and** `pack build` (`dark_orf` translation table handling, ensuring zero `pyhmmer.easel.GeneticCode` crashes!), emit a sidecar TSV distinguishing mitochondrial palm-fold invertrons from cytoplasmic `DPBB` killer plasmids (`pGKL1`/`pGKL2`), and stage `LPL1` + multi-code `O1` at `DEPTH=annotated` (Pfam 38.2 + `--crispr`), mirrored to `tower`.
Accept: unit tests fail first and pass (including Table 4 `kalilo`/`pAL2-1` `rpo` intact ORF recovery and `pack build` safety across all staged `genetic_code` values); `LPL1` and `O1` pass `s2_check_inputs.py` and `stage_driver.sh ... estimate`; `tests/r0` green.
Done 2026-11-24 (STATUS row C-59a): `O2` (`2,412` distinct multi-code RefSeq r238 organellar genomes, `transl_table` `1/3/4/16/22`, `337.98 Mbp`, `2,411` net new, `100%` of non-table-11 homing/RT ORFs, leaving `O1` untouched) and `LPL1` (`573` distinct eukaryotic mitochondrial invertron & cytoplasmic `DPBB` killer linear plasmids, `4.35 Mbp`, `408` net new, Table 4 `kalilo`/`maranhar`/`pAL2-1` intact `893–1,197` aa ORFs verified) staged at `DEPTH=annotated` with `O2.conf` + `LPL1.conf` and mirrored to `tower` (`451 passed` on `wilkcomp02`).

Why: Two high-yield `redistributable: true` virome sources plug major blind spots for biotechnology-grade `ssRNAP`s (cold-active `VSW-3`-like, low-dsRNA `KP34`/`EM1`-like *Autographiviridae*, roseophage `Phage_Mu_F+RNA_pol` fusions, and *Schitoviridae* N4-like phages):

  2. **`V4` (`PEONY 1.3` / `PIGEON 3.0`, Dryad `10.5061/dryad.prr4xgz35`, `CC BY 4.0`, `redistributable: true`)**: **`1,351,223` PIGEON vOTUs** (`9.13 GB` `.fa.gz`; `1,072,861` virome-only `PEONY` vOTUs `>= 10 kb`, `~30–35 Gbp` uncompressed *estimate*), cleanly replacing the unreachable PNNL `GSV Atlas` (`sources.yaml`) with massive soil, peatland, wetland, psychrophilic, and freshwater virome breadth (`DEPTH=called`, `CRISPR=yes`).
Do, test first: write `sources.yaml` entries before fetch, apply `DEC-65` exact `20..1,000` bp DTR trimming on metagenomic records, deduplicate `contig_uid` against `S1, H1, V1, V2, CG1, MV1, V3`, stage `LRV1` (`DEPTH=annotated`) and `V4` (`DEPTH=called`), mirror to `tower`, and commit `.conf`s + measurement note.
Accept: unit tests fail first and pass; `contig_uid` overlap vs `V1/V2/MV1/V3` recorded; `LRV1` and `V4` pass `s2_check_inputs.py` and `stage_driver.sh ... estimate`; `tests/r0` green.
Part 1 done 2026-11-24 (STATUS row C-67 (1)): `LRV1` (`2,535` distinct complete long-read marine ONT `AFVGs` + freshwater HiFi phages, `104.49 Mbp`, `669` `DEC-65` exact DTR trims, `0` contigs held across `S1..LPL1 + MV1 + V3` = `100.0%` new) staged at `DEPTH=annotated` with `LRV1.conf` and mirrored `9/9` to `tower` (`448 passed` on `wilkcomp02`); `V4` (`PEONY 1.3` / `PIGEON 3.0`, Dryad `10.5061/dryad.prr4xgz35`) `fetch-v4` and `stage-v4` implemented & unit-tested, awaiting Dryad ORCID Bearer token or local file drop (Dryad `/download` returns `HTTP 401`, `/downloads/file_stream/` `HTTP 403` AWS WAF).

Curated **spliced mRNA nucleotide transcriptome assemblies** — **`MMETSP`** (`zenodo.1212585`, `678` marine protist transcriptomes across all major unicellular eukaryotic supergroups, `~7.2 GB` tar.gz, `CC BY 4.0`) and **`1KP`** (`GigaDB 100627`/`100910` curated `FNA.zip`, `~5.5 GB`, `~1,300` green algal, bryophyte, fern, and plant transcriptomes, `CC BY 4.0`) — hold **intron-free continuous cDNA contigs** (`2.5–4.5 kb` for full-length `RpoT` transcripts!), unlocking the entire eukaryotic nuclear `ssRNAP` radiation immediately with standard/ciliate translation-table ORF calling (`transl_table=1`, `6`, `10`, `29`, `30`). Paired with a targeted **`Logan` (`logan50` -> `s3://logan-pub/c/`)** pull (`LGNRNAP1`, `< 5 Gbp`) baited by `W-40`'s `ssRNAP` panel (mitochondrial invertron `rpo`, N4 `vRNAP` + split `RNAP2`, psychrophilic `VSW-3`/`EM1`, and basal protist `RpoT`), this captures unbinned environmental and metatranscriptomic `ssRNAP`s across all of SRA.

Accept: unit tests fail first and pass; known protist/plant `RpoT`/`RPOTm`/`RPOTp` controls recovered full-length without intron breaks; `ET1` and `LGNRNAP1` pass `s2_check_inputs.py` and `stage_driver.sh ... estimate`; `tests/r0` green.

  2. **`proMGE2` delta (`MGE3`, Grekova, Bork & Khedkar, Nov 14, 2026, *NAR*, `doi:10.1093/nar/gkag1163`, `http://promge.embl.de/` OA)**: `>5M` non-redundant prokaryotic MGE islands/ICEs/transposons/integrons (*published*); take the `contig_uid`- and cluster-deduplicated delta beyond `S1..PL3, MGE1, MGE2`.
  3. **`HLRMDB` + Wirbel/Bhatt (Jan 2026) long-read human gut delta (`LR3` additions)**: `HLRMDB` (Zhai et al. Jan 2026 *NAR*, `doi:10.1093/nar/gkaf1152`, `18,721` human PacBio/ONT MAGs across `38` studies *published*, `http://www.inbirg.com/hlrmdb/` OA) + Wirbel/Bhatt (Jan 2026 *Nature*, `PRJEB88320` ENA OA), deduplicated at `96%` ANI against `GlobDB`/`LR1`/`LR2`.
  4. **`ArchaeaHQ` (2026) post-`GlobDB` delta (`ARC1`, VEE Lab Radboud, Figshare `10.6084/m9.figshare.32266599` OA)**: 4-kingdom QC-filtered (`>=70%` comp, `<=10%` contam) archaeal MAG/isolate delta missed by `GTDB R226` / `GlobDB r226` (`~2–5 Gbp` *estimate*).
  5. **Niche-biome & holobiont batch (`NX3`/`NX4` additions, `~15–20 Gbp` `skani`-deduped *estimate*)**: Termite gut bacterial MAGs (`PMC11481507` `2,223` MAGs + 2026 wood-feeding termite HiFi cMAGs `doi:10.64898/2026.10.06.756956`), Red Sea hydrothermal vents (Hatiba Mons 2026, `>1,200` MAGs), Great Barrier Reef sediment (`GBR-MGD`, `2,698` MAGs), Antarctic vascular plant rhizosphere (`doi:10.1128/mra.00171-25`, `1,484` MAGs), and Air metagenome plasmids (May 2024 Zenodo).
Do, test first: stage in bounded-`/tmp` tranches (`Part 1`: `GHM1`+`VHM1`+`ARC1`; `Part 2`: `MGE3`+`HLRMDB`/`PRJEB88320`+`NX3`), recording `sources.yaml` entries before fetch, applying `DEC-65` DTR/topology handling and `skani` `>= 96%` ANI dedup on MAG bins against `GTDB R226` + `GlobDB r226` + earlier built/staged stages, verifying `s2_check_inputs.py` and `stage_est.py`, and mirroring to `tower`.
Accept: unit tests fail first and pass (normal and tools-hidden PATH); exact dedup counts vs `GlobDB r226` and earlier stages recorded; staged tranches pass `s2_check_inputs.py` and `stage_est.py`; `tests/r0` green.
2026-11-26 (card C-72, `planning/measurements/2026-11-26_missed_diversity_sources.md` §2.1, §2.2, §5.3): GlobDB r232 (June 2026, `346,233` genomes, `26` resources) and GTDB R232 (April 2026, `199,923` species, `3,154` more archaeal species than R226) are out, so every dedup this card names against `GlobDB r226` or `GTDB R226` should run against r232 and R232 (`ARC1` above all; and `NX3`'s Tibetan and river additions, which r232 now holds as `TPLM`, `QXLSG`, `CRLG`). Priority unchanged (`r26.11.6+`), except `GBR-MGD`, held until its licence is read (the `tower` job list records `{}`) and the three records of it agree (`2,698` or `5,283` MAGs; `zenodo:24187765` embargoed to 2027-02-01 per `2026-11-25_pi_login_pulls.md`). The proposed `r26.11.6` of card C-72 puts `SPX2`, `MGS1`, `MGM3` and the long-read `LR3` wave (`MFD_LR`, `MIDAS_LR`, `LR_COMPOST`) ahead of this card's Part 2. Proposed, for the PI. Decided the same day: DEC-79 (1) moves `SPX2` and the long-read sets to `r26.11.6` and holds `GBR_MGD` on its licence; DEC-79 (3) makes R232 and r232 the bases of this card's dedup; the MGnify catalogues (`MGS1`, `MGM3`) are not approved yet (DEC-79 (4)).

### C-72 Sources the earlier surveys missed (GlobDB r232, GTDB R232, groundwater virome, OMZ SAGs, wetlands, new MGnify catalogues) and a revised `r26.11.5` *(proposal; the PI chooses)* (`DEC-59`, `DEC-69`)
**H** for the choice of sources and of the release order (the PI's); **M** for each staging part once chosen · Pod: yes for the staging parts (`sources.yaml` entries before fetch; stage under `/tmp/if2453/s2/inputs/` or `/stor/scratch/Wilke/if2453/bio-box-staging/` and mirror to `tower`; read-only on `/tmp/if2453/r10/root`) · Depends: A-12, A-26, R-11 (the `tower` audit, for the landed sets) · DEC-50, DEC-51, DEC-59, DEC-65, DEC-69, DEC-70, DEC-71, DEC-72
Files: `sources.yaml`, `planning/CATALOG_GOAL.md`, `planning/measurements/2026-11-26_missed_diversity_sources.md` (the survey, done), per part a new `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/c72_<part>.py` with `tests/r1/test_c72_<part>.py`, `tests/r1/test_stage_driver.py`, the new `.conf`s in `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stages/`, and `planning/measurements/<date>_<stage>_inputs.md`
Why: the read-only survey of 2026-11-26 found that every plan dedupes against and cuts from GlobDB r226 and GTDB R226 while **GlobDB r232** (`fileshare.lisc.univie.ac.at/globdb/globdb_r232/`, June 2026, `346,233` genomes from `26` resources, CC BY-SA 4.0; `46,920` genomes from `12` resources r226 lacked, led by gcMeta 2025 `24,063` and CNCB-NGDC GWH `13,287`, *derived* from its dataset list; genome tarball about `299.1 GB`, *derived* from the index) and **GTDB R232** (`199,923` species, `56,309` more than R226, *derived*) are out. It also found six open sources that no note names: the **Groundwater Virome Catalogue** (`zenodo.11230969`, `280,420` vOTUs of `5 kb` and more, one `1,166,850,604`-byte FASTA, CC BY 4.0), the **OMZ single-cell compendium** (`figshare.20459526`, `3,570` assembled SAGs, one `747,416,178`-byte archive, CC BY 4.0), the **GWM global wetland catalogue** (`zenodo.20263536`, `4,164` dereplicated MAGs, `3,639` putative novel species, one `3,885,785,718`-byte zip, CC BY 4.0), MGnify **`marine` v3.0** (2026-11-09, `11,313` new species since v2.0), **`marine-sediment` v1.0** (`6,158` species) and **`marine-eukaryotes` v1.0** (`74` species, for card C-60). Sizes *measured* from the providers' listings; counts *published*.
Grab priority relative to the releases, each with its ease (E1 on the pod, E2 landed on `tower`, E3 one to ten anonymous files, E4 one stream over 100 GB or thousands of files, E5 login, embargo, or licence or location not stated):
  1. **`r26.11.5` preparation (E3):** GlobDB r232's four small tables (`taxonomy`, `checkm2`, `dataset_list`, `dictionaries`; about `24 MB`) and GTDB R232's `bac120`/`ar53` metadata (`282 MB`); re-key `SYM1_post_r226_left_for_later.tsv.gz`, the `REFSEQ-NEW` list and the `G2..G41` remainder to R232 and r232 before any further cut.
  2. **`r26.11.5` (E3):** `GWV1` (groundwater virome; `~3.6–4.0 Gbp` *estimate*; `gv-meta`; DEC-65 repeat rule; overlap with `MV1`, `V2`, `V3` measured first), `SAG3` (OMZ SAGs; `~2.3–2.5 Gbp` *estimate*; dedup by its Table S1 GenBank accessions and `skani` against GTDB R232), `WET1` (wetlands; `~12–13 Gbp` *estimate*; `skani` 96% against GlobDB r226 and r232).
  3. **`r26.11.5` if the PI approves the stream, else `r26.11.6` (E4):** `G232-A`, one pass over `globdb_r232_genome_fasta.tar.gz` (as `c41_stream_stage.py` did for r226), taking the new-resource genomes in archaea, in the unclassified domain, and in phyla or classes absent from held stages, capped near `12 Gbp`.
  4. **`r26.11.6` (E4):** `MGS1` (`marine-sediment` v1.0), `MGM3` (`marine` v3.0's new species, after dedup against r232 and `OMD2`), and `G232-B` (the rest of the `46,920`; `~105 Gbp` *estimate*; two or three tranches into `r26.11.7`).
  5. **With card C-60:** `MGE-EUK1` (MGnify marine eukaryotes).
  6. **Later (E5):** the *Nature Water* 2026 activated-sludge catalogue (`doi:10.1038/s44221-025-00576-8`; no repository found).
Proposed `r26.11.5` (note §5; **proposed, for the PI to ratify**; it changes the order of the handoff of 2026-11-26 07:15, `STATUS.md` H2 step 3, which no DECISIONS row records): (a) **keep** `G-RARE1..3`, `MV2` + `MV-EUKV`, `KAV-HIFI` + `INPHARED-2604`, `LR2` + `SAG1` + `NX1` (all E1); (b) **enter** the sets already staged or landed that cards C-69 and C-70 and the 2026-11-25 survey place in `r26.11.5` but the handoff omits: `GV1`, `EGV1`, `ECE2`, `MIR1`, `ASG1`, `SAG2`, `DPN1`, `SYM2`, `LRP1`, `MIR2`, `MGE4` (E1, E2); (c) **enter** `GWV1`, `SAG3`, `WET1` and, conditionally, `G232-A`; (d) **move to `r26.11.6`**: `SPX2` (about 1,500 per-sample fetches; it leads `r26.11.6` on its measured yield), `RMD1` and `O4` (queued in batch 6b behind batches 5 and 6), `GBR_MGD` (licence not stated), `LGRT2` (an overnight scan and hundreds of run fetches), `REFSEQ-NEW` (complete isolates; low NR50 per Gbp by DEC-70's reasoning), and the `tower` long-read cMAG sets as `LR3` (`MFD_LR` alone `55–65 Gbp` *estimate*). Total about `108–120 Gbp` new (*derived* from estimates; `96–108` without `G232-A`); every entrant is on disk or one anonymous file. Before staging: `MIR1`'s GOEV archive deduped against `GV1` and stripped of its *Poxviridae* and *Asfarviridae* references; `ASG1` deduped against GTDB R232 and r232 archaea; `DPN1` staged from genome FASTA only once R-11 has read its `9.99 GB` archive; `GWV1` checked against MetaVR. Outside `r26.11.5`: `GFS1` repeats GlobDB r226's `GFSMAG` (`1,377` non-GTDB species on `/stor`) and carries `14` eukaryotic assemblies; `WWV1` mixes realms; `CFMD1`, `CRBC_ISO` and `QXLSG1` are inside GlobDB r232 at species level; `uhgv_extended` is `92.0%` in `V3`; `jgi_img_pr` adds no plasmid to `PL3`.
Decisions that are the PI's: (1) which new sources enter (each needs its `sources.yaml` entry and approval under DEC-50 before any fetch; GlobDB r232 is CC BY-SA 4.0 like r226); (2) the revised `r26.11.5` membership and order, which amends the handoff's order and the release placement in cards C-69 and C-70 and in `CATALOG_GOAL.md` §2 items 3 and 4, and wants a DECISIONS row; (3) whether `r26.11.5` streams the `299 GB` GlobDB r232 tarball (`G232-A`) or leaves it to `r26.11.6`; (4) whether later `G` tranches are cut from r232 instead of the r226 archive on `/stor`.
Do, test first (per staging part, once chosen): the `sources.yaml` entry before fetch; fetch to `tower` or the pod; dedup by exact `contig_uid` against every built and staged stage, and by `skani` 96% ANI (AF 50%) against GTDB R232 and GlobDB r232 for MAGs and SAGs; DEC-65 topology and repeat handling for viruses; the remainder recorded in `CATALOG_GOAL.md` §4; stage, verify with `s2_check_inputs.py`, `stage_est.py conf` and read-only `stage_est.py before`; mirror to `tower`.
Accept: per part, unit tests fail first and pass (normal and tools-hidden PATH); exact overlap counts recorded; `s2_check_inputs.py` and `stage_est.py` (`refusals: []`) exit `0`; `relay_to_tower.sh` verifies the files; `tests/r0` green. Survey part done 2026-11-26: the note holds a record per candidate, two rankings, a merged grab order, a not-found list, a login list and the roadmap review.
Decided 2026-11-26 (DEC-79, the PI's words "for each of the four decisions, go ahead with your recommendations"): (1) the proposed `r26.11.5` above is the plan, without `G232-A`; (2) `r26.11.5` does not stream the GlobDB r232 genome archive, and `G232-A` is planned with `r26.11.6`; (3) `G` tranches after `G-RARE1..3` are cut from r232, and dedups run against GTDB R232 and GlobDB r232; (4) approved for listing and fetching: `GWV1`, `SAG3` (with its table), `WET1` (dereplicated genomes and the MAG table), GlobDB r232's small tables, GTDB R232's two metadata tables; the MGnify catalogues are not approved yet (no licence in their READMEs). Part 1 of the staging work is therefore: the `sources.yaml` entries for those five, a job list that passes `fetch_to_tower.sh --check`, the fetch to `tower`, and its record.

Cards C-73 to C-81 and A-29 below stage `r26.11.5` as DEC-79 (1) fixes it; the plan, with one record per member, the
chain, the transfer from `tower`, the disk budget and the risks, is `planning/measurements/2026-11-26_r26_11_5_staging_plan.md`.
Rules common to all of them (plan §2, §7, §8): large files (64 MB and more) go to
`/stor/scratch/Wilke/if2453/bio-box-staging/<STAGE>/`, metadata and smaller files to `/tmp/if2453/<card>/`, and nothing
enters `/tmp/if2453/s2/inputs/` before card A-29; each conf is written as `stages/templates/<STAGE>.conf` (real `SUM=`
lines, `INPUTS=/tmp/if2453/s2/inputs/<STAGE>`, `AFTER=` as plan §5, `EXPECT=` over the root and the `r26.11.4` stages)
and promoted by A-29, so that no staging card edits `tests/r1/test_stage_driver.py`; stage names have no hyphen
(`GRARE1..3`, `MVEUKV`, `KAVHIFI`, `INPH2604`); while `r26.11.4` builds, the pod's threads in all (build and staging)
stay at most 56, at most two staging cards compute at once, none while `MV1` or `V3` call genes, and none in weekday
hours 08:00 to 20:00 on a day the PI's word "night limits apply during day today" (2026-11-26) does not cover (plan Q8); tests run on the pod as one test file in
one process (STATUS W1), and the whole suite on GitHub Actions and on a quiet pod; every selection records its
remainder in `planning/CATALOG_GOAL.md` §4 (DEC-59 (3)); a set whose licence no record confirms is not staged.
2026-11-26, DEC-80 (the PI: "all personal use, no worries re license i approve"): the last rule is lifted. A set with no confirmed licence is staged like any other; its `sources.yaml` entry says what the record states (or that none is stated) and carries `redistributable: false`. So `SYM2` is staged by card C-78 without waiting, and card C-70 takes the Bellas part of `MIR1`.

(3) Run `--dry-run` for `tower:/mnt/user/research-bulk/bio-box/sources_incoming/EGV1/` with its `SHA256SUMS` as the list.

### A-15 The release train resumes its own stopped release
**M** · Pod: yes (a rehearsal in scratch; nothing in the data root) · Depends: A-13, A-14 · found 2026-11-21 when DEC-61 allowed `r26.11.2` to resume
Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/release_train.sh`, `tests/r1/test_driver_exit_status.py` or `tests/r1/test_c46_release_train.py`, `RUNBOOK.md` ("After a guard stop")
Why: the train's stop message says "rerun to resume", and a rerun of `release_train.sh run` does plan the same release again while it has no `release_def` (the same name, the same stages, the same `build-stage` command, whose published steps are skipped). But the rerun would (1) list the root again into `$WORK/before/listing.tsv`, so that the files the first launch published are no longer "added" and the release's durable copy would miss them; (2) verify the earlier releases again into the same logs, replacing the hashes taken before anything was written; (3) compute `estimate/before.json` again on a root that now holds half the release; (4) launch a unit of the same name, `<prefix>-rel-1`, whose log already holds `pod_run: <unit> exited 143`: card A-14's watch reads a unit's own exit line first, so it would take the new unit for ended at once, fail on "exited 143", and leave the unit building unguarded.
Do, test first with the stubs: `run` recognises a resume (this `$WORK` has a `plan.json` whose release and stages equal the new plan's, and the root has run records of that release and no manifest for it) and then: keeps `before/listing.tsv`, the `before/verify-*.log` files and `estimate/before.json` of the first launch untouched (and refuses if any is missing); still verifies every earlier release now, into a directory of its own, and fails unless each manifest hash equals the first launch's; numbers the unit `<prefix>-rel-<n>` from the units already recorded under `$WORK/run/`, never reusing a name; judges the rate guard against the first launch's estimate. A `$WORK` whose `plan.json` names another release or other stages, with records of an unfinished release in the root, is refused with both named. A first launch is unchanged (the stub command lines' hash). `--dry-run` says `Action: resume <release> (<k> run records published; unit <n>)` in that state. The pack and example waits get the same unit-ended rule as the watch (card A-14 left them).
Accept: the tests fail first on each of the four faults and pass; in scratch on the fixture (`release_train.sh rehearse`'s form), a release is stopped by a `STOP` or by a stubbed guard mid-way and then resumed by `run` alone, and its `root_added.tsv` equals that of an uninterrupted run of the same release (same files, sizes and sha256); `tests/r0` green.
