### A-12 One stage driver for every stage
**M** · Pod: yes (the rehearsal on the fixture, in scratch; nothing in the data root) · Depends: A-11 · found 2026-11-21 by card A-11: the same faults were in nine drivers copied from one another, and five more stages are queued (`V2`, `GV1`, `PL2`, `G1`, `SPX1`)
Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stage_driver.sh`, `stage_queue.sh`, `stage_est.py`, `stage_check.py` (new, made from `c47_driver.sh`, `c47_queue.sh`, `c47_est.py`, `c47_check.py` and `c48c_v1_*`), `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stages/<STAGE>.conf` (new: one small file per queued stage), `scripts/orchestrator/rel_wait.sh` only for the unit patterns it lists, `tests/r1/test_stage_driver.py` (new), `RUNBOOK.md` (a section "Building a stage" and its Appendix A rows)
Why: each stage so far had a driver written for it by copying the last one, with its stage name, input sums, releases and backup name written into the script. Card A-11 fixed one fault in nine copies. A stage's build is the same sequence every time; what differs is a short list of values.
Do, test first with stub commands on `PATH` as `tests/r1/test_driver_exit_status.py` does: one driver that takes `STAGE` and reads `stages/<STAGE>.conf` (the inputs directory and the sha256 of `genomes.json` and `objects.json`; depth; `--min-contig-len` if any; the library arguments or none; `--crispr` or not; the stages a later stage must wait for; estimates the stage's note recorded, where the code's estimate is to be judged against another figure, as `GV1`'s gene calling against 236 CPU-minutes; accepted records, each with its decision row, as `c47_est.py`'s `ACCEPTED`). Modes, each exiting with its own status, writing `<mode>/DONE` only on success and refusing to run without the markers before it: `estimate` (from the code, never a copied number), `rehearse` (the fixture, in scratch), `before` (the root's listing, `release verify` of every release the root defines, read from the root and not from a list in the script), `run` (`build-stage` through `pod_run.sh` in the image by digest, the `STOP` check, the guards of DEC-43 and DEC-53, 280 CPU-minutes per process, free space), `after`, `check` (what `c47_check.py` asserts, with the within-stage ledger rows read as card C-47 corrected them; the list of files added with sha256), `backup` (the tar on `/stor/work` under a temporary name, read back, `mv -n`; never overwrites). `stage_queue.sh` runs them in order on the markers. Unit names are `<stage>-*` in lower case, and `rel_wait.sh` takes the pattern from its argument. The thread cap comes from the profile and the hour, as `build-stage`'s allowance does; nothing in the driver names a thread count above the profile's.
The values of the queued stages come from their notes and are written into their `.conf` files with the note named: `V2` and `GV1` (`planning/measurements/2026-11-20_virus_v2_gv1_inputs.md`, `2026-11-20_virus_gv1_inputs.md`; `GV1` after `V2`), `PL2` (card C-40's note), `G1` (card C-41's), `SPX1` (`2026-11-20_spire_tranche_1.md`: `--depth called --min-contig-len 5000`, after `G1`). A stage whose note leaves a value open gets no `.conf`; name it in the report.
Accept: the tests (each mode's inner command failing in turn: non-zero exit, no marker; the success path's command lines recorded); the rehearsal on the fixture builds a stage with the new driver and its `check` passes; `c47_driver.sh`'s and the new driver's `run` launch the same `build-stage` command for `C5`'s values apart from the unit name (shown from the stubs' records). No stage is built in the data root by this card: the first real use is `V2`, the orchestrator's, after release `r26.11.2` is audited.
**Amended 2026-11-21 after an independent review of the first hand-back (`task/A-12` at `d772cb7`; an Opus reviewer read the code and ran nothing; it found no path by which a mode exits 0 or writes its marker after a failure, and these defects). Not merged until each is fixed, test first, or answered with evidence.** Files added for this: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/release_train.sh` (only the list of units its own lock looks for).
(1) **`run` can abandon a build that is still running.** Its loop ends on any non-zero exit of `systemctl --user is-active`, a bus error included; the driver then writes `run/FAILED` and exits while the unit builds on in the data root with no guard. Leave the loop only on the unit's own exit line or an explicit inactive or not-found state; otherwise keep polling. `stop_unit` must see the unit gone before it says so. A trap stops the unit if the driver itself exits during `run`.
(2) **A release can start while the next stage builds.** `rel_wait.sh` looks for active units once, before a wait of hours, and `release_train.sh`'s own lock knows no stage unit. Both take the unit patterns of every `stages/*.conf` (and the older names), and `rel_wait.sh` looks again immediately before the train starts.
(3) **`stage_check.py` and `stage_est.py rates` and `complete` are run by no test** (every driver test stubs them). On a constructed root, each assertion of the check is seen to fail once (a ledger count that differs from the contigs held, an uncovered contig, gene rows for a contig not stored, copied domain rows that differ from their source, a CRISPR count that differs, a record of a step the stage's depth does not have, a contig under the least length) and to pass; `rates` runs on a root with a published gene-call plan, scan shards, a `JUDGE` line and an `ACCEPTED` record.
(4) **A marker is not tied to what it was made for.** Each `DONE` records the sha256 of the conf; a mode refuses a marker made for another conf, the rehearsal's included; `run` checks the inputs' sums again.
(5) **The unit's quota is the night cap at any hour.** The kernel quota is the only hard limit on the account's threads. The driver sets the unit's `CPUQuota` at launch, and again at each poll when the hour's cap changes, to the profile's cap of that hour less what the driver's own helpers may use, so that the unit and the helpers together stay inside the cap by day and by night.
(6) **Checks the model drivers made against their notes.** A conf line `EXPECT=held_by <STAGE> <n> <note>`: `estimate` stops when the contigs an earlier stage holds differ from the note's (for `GV1`: `V2` holds 17,664). The check keeps each run's report (a numbered directory, as card C-47's `CHECK_DIR` did). The check reads the ledger's reason strings as `c47_check.py` corrected them, no looser, and uses every index row where `c47_check.py` did.
(7) **Silent passes in the check**: a missing scan plan when the conf has a library fails; CRISPR paths that match nothing when the conf has `CRISPR=yes` fail unless the ledger says no arrays were found; a judged-size step that `rates` could not name fails.
(8) **The environment.** The root is compared after `realpath` (a trailing slash made the data root pass as a scratch root, with the rehearsal gate skipped); in the data root the conf must be the tree's own `stages/<STAGE>.conf`, `MEM` is at most 200G, the free-space floors cannot be lowered, and an `ACCEPTED` or `JUDGE` line's decision row or note must exist (`| DEC-n |` in `planning/DECISIONS.md`; the note's file). A record stopped against a `JUDGE` figure can be accepted by a decision row as any other record can, by name and value, without deleting the `JUDGE` line.
(9) Smaller: the backup compares two hashes that must each be 64 hex characters; a `STOP` during `run` still judges the records published before it exits; each rehearsal starts with its own logs; `rates` takes from `estimate/before.json` what does not change during a run and its time per call is reported for the fixture; the queue reads `run/FAILED` before it re-runs `estimate` and `before`.
**Amended again 2026-11-21 after the second review (`4996467`: the first round's defects closed, each with a test).** Six further points went to the agent (the release side read its confs from the suite's frozen tree; the watch had no time limit; a stop during a long helper call; a rehearsal-only conf accepted in the data root; `PROFILE` free in the data root; assertions of the check without a test) and are done at `2f0cc64` but for one, which needs this line: **`tests/r1/test_driver_exit_status.py` joins the card's Files for a migration of exactly two tests and one helper.** `rel_wait.sh` is to refuse a tree without `stages/*.conf` and to take the suite as a required argument (no default `rel1120`); `test_rel_wait_starts_the_release_when_every_marker_is_there` calls it with no argument, and `test_rel_wait_takes_the_driver_directory_and_the_suite_as_arguments` gives it a tree without `stages/`. Migrate both: the suite is passed, the tree has a `stages/` with one conf; what each asserts about markers, the dry run and the run is unchanged, and no other test of the file is touched. Add beside them a test that the bare call and the tree without confs are refused. The helper `World.driver` writes through an existing symlink when one `World` copies two drivers: unlink first. **And the file's other `rel_wait.sh` tests** (`test_rel_wait_does_not_start_on_a_log_line_when_a_marker_is_missing`, six cases; `..._when_the_queue_failed`; `..._when_a_suite_shard_failed`), which call the helper `_wait` with no suite and so now stop at the argument check instead of the check each was written for: migrate the helper the same way (the suite passed, a tree with a conf), and have each assert the message of its own refusal, so that none can pass at an earlier exit.
Done 2026-11-21 (STATUS row A-12; `task/A-12` at `e5ddc66`, the last script change `1e0a55e`): the driver, queue, estimates and check, confs for `V2` and `GV1`, 198 tests of its own and the migrated `rel_wait.sh` tests; two independent reviews; the rehearsal of the final scripts passed for both shapes (`V2`: annotated, Pfam, CRISPR; `ZX1`: called, 5 kb, no library). Its first real use, stage `V2` in the data root, is the orchestrator's, after release `r26.11.2` is settled and audited. No conf yet for `PL2` (open item 22), `G1` (decided by the orchestrator: annotated; its conf is to be written from its note) and `SPX1` (card B-61).
(10) To report, not to fix here: whether batched ingest, judged as one group against the one-process formula, would stop a stage of `SPX1`'s size (find a built stage with batched ingest and give its ratio, or say none exists); the memory of `stage_est.py before` on `genomes.json` of 2.83 M genomes (`json.loads` of the whole file).

### A-10 The OOM-killed search's recorded peak can miss its bound on GitHub Actions
**M** · Depends: none · found 2026-11-20 on a commit that changed only documents (`b17935e`, Actions run 44761392180)
Files: `src/biobox/search/runner.py` (the peak it records for a worker the kernel killed), `src/biobox/runstep.py` only if the sampling helper is shared, `tests/r2/test_micro_s0_search.py` (the test below; no bound is widened), a note in `planning/measurements/`
Why: `tests/r2/test_micro_s0_search.py::test_c16_an_oom_killed_search_records_a_peak_within_20_percent_of_its_cap` failed with `recorded 596.7 MiB (scope_memory_peak_sampled); cap 953.7 MiB; status oom`, so `0.8 * cap <= peak` did not hold (*measured*, Actions, 2026-11-20; 1 failed, 2,501 passed, 252 skipped). The same test passed on the two pushes before it (`352424f`, `becdc52`) and the commit touched no code, so the outcome depends on timing: the recorded value is a sample of the scope's `memory.peak` taken while the worker ran, and the kernel killed the worker between two samples, after the allocation had grown past the last one. The sibling test for a step (`test_c16_an_oom_killed_step_records_a_peak_within_20_percent_of_its_cap`) read 256.0 MiB against a cap of 256.0 MiB in the same run, with source `scope_memory_peak` (read after the end, not sampled). A record that can be 37% under the true peak is also a wrong number in a run record (AGENTS §6), apart from the test.
Do, test first: find out why the search worker's scope gives a sampled value where the step's gives the final one (the scope collected before it is read, a different unit type, the order of kill and read), and make the search runner record the final `memory.peak` of the worker's scope when the kernel killed it, as `runstep` does; keep the sampled value only as the stated fallback, with its source named. Reproduce the miss deterministically first (a fault that allocates in one step between two samples, or a sampling interval the test sets long), see the bound fail, then pass. Do not widen the 20% bound, add a retry or skip the test.
Accept: the reproduction fails before and passes after; `scripts/orchestrator/ci_repeat.sh` shows the test green on 10 consecutive Actions runs of the branch.
**Diagnosed 2026-11-20, not fixed: the card's "Why" is half wrong and its "Do" cannot be done inside its files** (an Opus agent read the code and 31 Actions logs and stopped before any change, AGENTS §9; its reading, with what is measured marked). (1) The search runner and `run-step` use the same helper, placed the same way inside the scope (`runstep.measured`, `runstep.step_peak`), and both normally read the final `memory.peak` after the worker is reaped: on Actions the search's OOM test recorded `953.7 MiB (scope_memory_peak)`, exactly the cap, in 30 of 31 job attempts, and the step's test `256.0 MiB (scope_memory_peak)` in 31 of 31 (*measured*, `gh run view --log` of runs 44514423589 to 44775879527, read 2026-11-20). The sampled value is a fallback used only when **the helper itself** dies before writing its final file; the sampling interval is not the cause. (2) In the failing attempt the helper was killed at about the moment of the worker's OOM kill (status `oom`, no final file, no sample after 596.7 MiB although it samples every 0.2 s). No path in the runner sends that kill. Who did is not settled by the code or the log: the kernel's memory-cgroup OOM killer taking the helper as a second victim, systemd stopping the scope (`OOMPolicy=stop`), or `systemd-oomd`; the worker's `stderr.txt` is deleted with its work directory. (3) No ordering inside the helper fixes it: the final value needs a survivor reading `memory.peak` while the scope's cgroup still exists, and the cgroup goes when its last live process does. Ten green runs would not show a fix either: at 1 failure in 31, ten runs pass by chance about 72% of the time (*derived*: (30/31)^10). (4) **Exposure is small**: a run record under the root's `runs/` is published only on exit 0, which implies the final file was written; the pod's root has 0 sampled sources in 1,908 records (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-20). A sampled source can reach only the record of a failed step in staging and `search_run` rows of runs that ended `oom`, `timeout` or killed. (5) Design choices, for a decision (STATUS open item 20): (a) a keeper process in the scope that allocates nothing, or the helper moved outside the scope; (b) the peak from systemd's own accounting of a named unit (`MemoryPeak`; whether it outlives the unit on the Actions image is not verified); (c) a sampler that charges nothing to the scope (one open file read with `pread`, a pre-faulted output file), which helps only if the second-victim reading is right. Card A-10a gathers the evidence that chooses between them.

### A-10b A workflow that repeats only the memory-peak tests
**L** · Depends: A-10a · the Actions part of cards A-10 and A-10a without running the whole suite sixty times
Files: `.github/workflows/peak-evidence.yml` (new), `tests/r0/test_runbook.py` or the r0 test that lists the workflows only if one must learn the new file's name (say which), `RUNBOOK.md` section 8 (three lines)
Why: the sampled case appeared in 1 of 31 `ci` jobs, and a `ci` job is the whole suite, about 55 minutes of a hosted runner (*measured*, Actions run 44761392180: 3,355 s of pytest). Sixty of those to see it once more is about 55 runner-hours; the four C-16 tests alone take about a minute.

It runs `uv run pytest -q tests/r2/test_micro_s0_search.py tests/r2/test_runstep.py -k c16 -rA -W always` `repeats` times in a shell loop that **does not stop at a failure**, appends each run's output to a log, and at the end prints, for every iteration, the `C16PeakEvidence` lines (they hold `peak_evidence`), the count of iterations whose OOM tests recorded a sampled source, and the count that failed; the job **fails if any iteration failed** (nothing is hidden, nothing is retried: every iteration's result is reported). ### C-28 The night thread allowance mistakes our own load for someone else's
**M** · found 2026-11-10 (`phase2/FAILED.2315-refused-48`) and again 2026-11-12 · Depends: C-12
Files: `src/biobox/runstep.py` or wherever the hour's allowance is computed (`scan_allowance`), `src/biobox/stage.py` if it calls it, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/r50_driver.sh` (`NIGHT_JOBS`), `RUNBOOK.md`, tests
Why: AGENTS §7.2 allows 56 threads at night "only when `ps` or `top` shows no other compute". The code tests the load average instead (above 8 means the day cap, 40), and our own scan holds the load near 42, so a build can never use more than 40 threads at night: on 2026-11-12 at 23:24 CST the account used 41.1 CPUs, every other user together under 0.5 (*measured*, `ps`), on a 112-core host. A scan at 13 shards of 4 threads instead of 10 would be 30% faster at night (*derived*).
Do, test first: the night allowance asks what the rule asks: the CPU other users are using now (summed `pcpu` of processes not owned by the account, over a short window, with a stated threshold such as one core), not the load average; the day cap is unchanged; the total stays a cap over everything the account runs (AGENTS §7.2 and the memory "pod thread cap is a total"): the allowance of a step is the cap minus what the account's other processes use, so tests and agents are not squeezed out; say how a step that started under 56 behaves when the day window opens (it must not run on at 56: the existing re-read before each launch, card C-12). A test with injected readings for each case (others busy, others idle, own load high). The driver's night shard count follows the allowance instead of a constant. Never applied to a build's tree during a scan.
Accept: the tests; on the pod, `run-step` reports the allowance it computed and why.

(2) Selection code first, with tests: `c25_select.py --ani <table>` takes pairwise ANI (and alignment fraction) per species and picks 20 by greedy farthest-first from the best-quality genome, ties by quality order then accession; pairs skani does not report (below its screen) count as the largest distance; a brute-force oracle written separately; input-order independence. (3) Fetch the 28,483 genomes through the listed NCBI Datasets source, verified as phase 1 was (retry the phase-1 genomes NCBI did not serve once, DEC-31), into scratch on the pod's local disk, in batches so that FASTA is removed once sketched (state the peak disk; stop if `/tmp` would fall under 200 GB free); archive nothing on `/stor` except, as one file over 64 MB, the genomes phase 2 will keep (after the selection). (4) When the image with skani is on the pod: measure skani on one species first (sketch time per genome, time per pair, memory) against the 4 to 17 CPU-hour *estimate*; then sketch all, compute the pairs farthest-first needs (or all pairs within a species where that is cheaper to state: say which), write the table, select. Report: genomes that differ from the quality-only list (and by species for the ten largest), the distribution of the minimum pairwise ANI among the 20 picked per species under each rule (the evidence that the rule matters), phase 1 a subset, the new remainder's genomes and bases, list hashes.

### B-48 Gene calling in batch steps under the CPU budget
**M** · Pod: yes (read-only on the data root; scratch builds) · Depends: B-30 (its form), C-20 · blocks card C-26 part 2 (stage `PL1`)
Files: `src/biobox/l1/genecall.py`, `src/biobox/stage.py`, `src/biobox/cli.py` (the `stage genecall` actions), tests in `tests/r1` and `tests/r2`, `RUNBOOK.md` (§2 and the limits row), one measurement note
Why: gene calling is one step and one process, with `--workers` threads. It cost 143 to 154 CPU-minutes on each stage built so far (S1, P2, P3, H1; *measured*, their run records), under the 300 of AGENTS §7.2 by luck of their sizes: nothing estimates it before launch, and nothing splits it. The PLSDB stage `PL1` is estimated at about 860 CPU-minutes (*derived*: 7,102 Mbp at 7.26 s per Mbp; the rate *measured* on every 30th record, `planning/measurements/2026-11-15_plsdb_inputs.md`: circular genomes in meta mode cost 7.70 s per Mbp with their wrap flanks, linear ones 2.94, where the prokaryote stages ran at 0.93, *derived* from P3's record). So `PL1` cannot be built as the code is.
Do, test first: (1) an estimate of the step before it starts, from the stage's inputs (bases by kind, topology and the gene-calling mode the code will choose for each genome), at rates measured from the existing stages' run records and the `PL1` sample (state each rate with its source; a test holds each estimate within 20% above its record, as card W-35's does); a refusal (exit 2) of a one-process call over `SHARD_MARGIN` of the budget when batches are not possible, else the split, decided by `build-stage` as it decides ingest's (card B-30) and the scans'. (2) Gene calling in batch steps: a plan (genomes in a fixed order, cut by estimated cost so that each batch fits the margin), one `run-step` process per batch, up to `--scan-jobs` at once within the allowance of the hour, `STOP` checked between launches, each batch publishing its own part under a temporary name; then a merge under the step name `genecall` that writes exactly the files, ledger and run record's result of a one-process call. A batch with output is not rerun; a killed batch leaves nothing a reader takes. (3) **Byte identity with the one-process call**: on the fixture at 1, 2, 3 and 5 batches and at 1 and 3 jobs; and on the pod, in scratch (a view of the root, card C-33's `c33_view.py`), stage S1 or H1 gene-called again in batches and compared with the root's own gene-call files by sha256 (every table gene calling writes, and the ledger; the root is only read). If byte identity cannot hold for a table (for example a training step that sees the whole stage), stop and report what depends on the whole stage: do not change what gene calling outputs. (4) Bounded memory per batch (the memory test of AGENTS §4.2); determinism at any batch count, job count and thread count. (5) Optional, only if the bytes do not change: a genome all of whose contigs another stage owns (DEC-26) is not called at all (on `PL1` about 143 of the 860 CPU-minutes re-call 7,046 such genomes and drop the result, *derived*); say what the ledger records for it today and keep that.
Accept: the tests; the byte identity on the fixture and on a real stage in scratch; the estimate for `PL1` printed by `build-stage --dry-run` or its equivalent on the real inputs (`/tmp/if2453/s2/inputs/PL1/`), with the batch plan and each batch under the margin. The build of `PL1` itself is card C-26 part 2.
Done 2026-11-15 (STATUS row B-48; `planning/measurements/2026-11-15_genecall_batches.md`): byte-identical in batches on the fixture and on S1 against the data root; `PL1` plans as 4 batches of 218.3 CPU-minutes. Left: a batch is not a `run-step` step, so the root records only the merge's cost: card B-50.

### B-55 Buffer `crispr._Scan` scratch Parquet writes to `io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS` before multi-million-contig stages
**M** · Pod: yes (tests, and byte-identity check against one built stage in scratch; read-only on the data root) · Depends: E-12, E-15 · blocks `--crispr` on `PL2` (`C-48c`), `G1` (`C-41`) and unbinned metagenomes (`C-42`, `C-48e`)

When `crispr._publish` queried `contigs` twice in DuckDB (`memory_limit = 256MB`), parsing `3.93M` row-group footers grew process RSS to `29.90 GB` (`31,354,484 kB`, *measured*, `ps`) and burned `>105` CPU-minutes on metadata alone (AGENTS §4.1, §4.6). Stages `PL2` (`859,036` new contigs, *measured*, card C-40) and `G1` (`1,227,222` contigs, *measured*, card C-41) would hit the same bottleneck under `--crispr`.
Do, test first: (1) Reproduce first in a test: scan `10,000` synthetic contigs (with stubbed `_search` in `tests/r1` and with real MinCED on a smaller multi-contig batch in `tests/r2`) and assert that every scratch Parquet file (`contigs.parquet`, `arrays.parquet`, `elements.parquet`, `topology.parquet`) has at most `max(1, ceil(num_rows / io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS))` row groups (`io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS = 122_880`, matching `_TOPO_ROWS`), plus the 100× synthetic store memory test of AGENTS §4.2 (peak RSS growth stated and bounded). See the row-group assertion fail first on today's code (`>= 10,000` row groups). (2) Buffer scratch rows in `_Scan` for `contigs`, `arrays` and `elements` (flushing in chunks of `io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS` and flushing the remainder in `close(ok=True)`, passing `row_group_size=io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS`), keeping bounded memory (at most one `io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS` batch per scratch table in Python). (3) Byte identity: published `feature`, `l1_crispr` and `ledger` files of both `crispr.run` and `crispr.run_near_cas` are byte-identical to today's on the `micro_s0` fixture (shared-contig and circular-origin cases included) and, on `wilkcomp02` in scratch (read-only on `/tmp/if2453/r10/root`), on stage `S1` or `H1` compared by `sha256` against the root's published CRISPR files.
Accept: the row-group and 100× RSS tests fail first and pass after; byte-identity on the fixture and against `S1` or `H1` on the pod; fixture `manifest_hash` unchanged.
Done 2026-11-19 (STATUS row B-55): `crispr._ScratchWriter` buffers `contigs`, `arrays`, `elements` and `topology` scratch rows up to `io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS` (`122,880`) and flushes full chunks via `write_batch(..., row_group_size=io.ROW_GROUP_ROWS)` (`6 passed` on new R1/R2 row-group & 100× RSS tests, `4/4` `H1` published CRISPR files sha256-identical, full pod suite `2,549 passed, 24 skipped`, *measured*).

### B-56 Avoid `KeyLookup` 64-row-group LRU thrashing above 7.86 M contigs in `genecall`
**L** · Depends: B-48, B-50 · uncovered 2026-11-19 by card C-42 (`planning/measurements/2026-11-18_spire_pilot.md` §4.4 #2) before multi-million-contig real-root stages (`PL2` + `G1..G41` + `S5` + `SPIRE`)
Files: `src/biobox/l1/genecall.py`, `src/biobox/l0seq/lookup.py` (only if needed), tests in `tests/r1` and `tests/r2`
Evidence (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-19, card C-42): `genecall` iterates `genome_input` ordered by `genome_uid = blake3(...)`, which is pseudorandom relative to `seq_locator`'s `raw_digest` sort key. Once cumulative stored contigs in a root exceed `64 × 122,880 = 7,864,320` rows (`> 64` row groups; observed when `SPN1` `4.38M` + `SPQ1` `4.99M` reached `9.37M` = `78` row groups), random `genome_uid` order misses `KeyLookup`'s 64-row-group LRU cache on `(78 - 64)/78 = 18%` of lookups, decompressing a `122,880`-row Arrow row group per miss (`>3×` CPU inflation and `>100 GB` aggregate RSS across workers).
Do, test first: order 1-contig genomes inside `genecall` work/batch execution by `raw_digest` / `seq_locator` order (while emitting identical sorted output tables and deterministic batch digests, or migrating only the plan hash if batch assignment changes — keep single-stage byte-identity on `micro_s0` and `S1`), or replace `KeyLookup`'s full-row-group cache in `genecall` with a bounded-memory streaming/sorted join; verify 100× RSS test and O(1) row-group reads above `64` row groups.
Accept: reproduction test with `> 64` `seq_locator` row groups fails/thrashes before and reads each row group O(1) times after; published gene-call outputs byte-identical.
**Not startable as written (2026-11-20; a Sonnet agent read the code and stopped before editing, AGENTS §9; its reading, not yet checked against a profile).** Gene calling resolves keys on two unrelated sort orders: `SeqStore.get` looks `contig_uid` up in `contig_index` (sorted by `contig_uid, raw_digest`) and then `raw_digest` in `seq_locator` (sorted by `raw_digest`); `genecall._genome_bases` and `ContigOwners.owner` are two more `contig_uid` lookups, each a `KeyLookup` with the same 64-row-group cache. Ordering genomes by `raw_digest`, as "Do" says, makes only the `seq_locator` lookup sequential; the three `contig_index` lookups stay random, so "reads each row group O(1) times" cannot be met inside `genecall.py` and `lookup.py`. A per-batch sorted prefetch inside the card's files gives about 2× (*derived* by the agent from the note's 18% miss rate: about 156 against 324 row-group reads per 600-contig batch), not O(1). A real fix is a sorted merge join of `contig_uid` → `raw_digest` → locator for the stage, which changes the order the gene-call plan and batch digests are defined on (card B-50) and touches `src/biobox/l0seq/inputs.py` and `store.py`. **This needs a design decision (tier H) before a card: STATUS open item 16.** It does not block any stage on the pod today: the root holds about 0.13 M contigs against the threshold of 7.86 M (*derived*: `r26.11.1`'s 122,626 contigs plus `C5`'s). **Corrected the same day**: `PL2` does not cross it. With `V1` (168,358 new contigs), `C1` to `C6` (16,470), `PL2` (859,036) and `G1` (1,227,222) the root holds about 2.4 M contigs (*derived*: the sum with 122,626; each count *measured* in its card's note); the stages that cross 7.86 M are the IMG-UMAG prokaryote MAGs (`J2` to `J15`, about 79,000 contigs per Gbp in card C-48d's pilot, *measured*) and GlobDB's `G2` on. The card's header names `PL2` among "multi-million-contig" stages; the threshold is cumulative, and `PL2` alone is 0.86 M.

**Amended 2026-11-21: the card's Evidence is wrong, and the card is now a measurement before any term is written** (a Sonnet agent measured before editing and stopped, AGENTS §9; *measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-21, one process in the test image at `nice 19`, CPU time from `getrusage`, with the pod's load average near 54). Reading gzip FASTA costs far less than the card says: Python's `gzip` on 6 of `SPX1`'s files, 0.0901 CPU-min per GB of FASTA (0.079 to 0.097); the ingest reader `biobox.fasta.iter_records` on 3 `SPX1` files, 0.1278 per GB from `.gz` against 0.0449 from a plain copy of the same bytes; on 100 of `C5`'s own `.fna.gz` files, 0.2664 per GB of bases. So decompression explains about 2 of `C5`'s 7.75 CPU-minutes over its estimate (*derived*: 45.803 − 38.057; 0.27 × 9.51 less the parsing already in the rate), not all of it as the card's 0.84 per GB assumed; about 5.6 are unexplained. `V1` read one plain file and ran at 1.021 of its estimate: no second gzip stage exists. The eleven built stages' ratios, record over estimate: `S1` 0.858, `P1` 1.072, `P2` 0.958, `P3` 1.058, `P4` 1.034, `P5` 1.075, `P6` 0.999, `H1` 1.012, `PL1` 1.034, `V1` 1.021, `C5` 1.2035 (*derived* by the agent from the run records and the code's formula).

Candidates, each with an arm that separates it: (1) gzip against plain: the same genomes ingested from `.fna.gz` and from plain copies; (2) a cost per object: the same bases as many objects and as few; (3) contigs an earlier stage holds: the estimate charges nothing for them (`bytes − held`), but ingest still reads and hashes them, and `C5` has 2,550 such contigs, 267,273,180 bp, where the stages the rate was fitted on had few: ingest a set as one stage, then the same set as a second stage of the same scratch root, where every contig is held; (4) load: CPU time per unit of work rises when the pod's hardware threads are shared; repeat an arm at different loads if the night gives them, and record the load average and what else of ours ran with each arm, and what ran beside `C5`'s ingest on 2026-11-19 from 11:22 to 12:08 against the fitted stages' ingests. Use a sample of `C5`'s own inputs (`/tmp/if2453/s2/inputs/C5`, read-only; about 200 genomes, each arm a few CPU-minutes, each arm three times, interleaved), through `build-stage` or the ingest step as `C5`'s driver ran it, in the image. Then write the note, and only if the arms name a cause that explains `C5`'s record to within 15% write the term for that cause, test first as the card's Accept says, with the fitted stages' estimates unchanged to the byte. If no arm explains it, stop with the note: a term fitted to one record with no mechanism is not written.

**`SPX1` no longer waits on this card for the gzip term** (decompression is about 2% of its ingest estimate, *derived*: 0.09 × 256.9 against 1,056). What `SPX1`'s ingest estimate does need is a look at `--min-contig-len`: the code charges every byte at the stored rate (*derived*: 256.9 GB × 4.11 = 1,056) although 85.23% of the bases are under 5 kb and are read and dropped; report what the arms say about the cost of a base that is read and not stored (arm 3 measures the same thing), because that sets how `SPX1`'s first-pass shards should be cut.

On 204 of `C5`'s own objects, three interleaved runs per arm in scratch roots (*measured*, wilkcomp02): no cost per object (one object against 204: 1.7% less); contigs an earlier stage holds cost 0.38 CPU-min per GB, 0.10 CPU-minutes on `C5`; the earlier stages' index adds nothing; the cost per byte does not grow with the stage; gzip costs 0.16 to 0.26 per GB (two reading passes), 1.6 to 2.5 of `C5`'s 7.75 CPU-minutes; plain copies of `C5`'s genomes already cost 4.39 per GB against the rate's 4.11; runs at a load of 52 to 56 cost 6.7% more than at 20 to 37. A third of `C5` (678 objects) projects the whole stage at 43.1 CPU-minutes, 5.9% under its record. So the record of DEC-56 is the sum of three small things and no one term explains it to within 15%; none is written. **What it found for `SPX1`**: with `--min-contig-len 5000` a base read and dropped costs 0.299 CPU-min per Gbp and a base stored 4.94, so its ingest is about 264 CPU-minutes (*derived*, from 4 files) where the code says 1,056. Card B-61 takes both terms into the estimate.

### B-62 The family postings do not fit DuckDB's 2 GB at `r26.11.2`'s size
**M** · Pod: yes · Depends: B-21, B-46 · found 2026-11-21 15:16 CST: release `r26.11.2` failed at its `postings` step (STATUS handoff H0) · **blocks the release**
Files: `src/biobox/l3/postings.py`, `src/biobox/cli.py` only if the step needs an option, `tests/r1` and `tests/r2` (the postings tests; a new test beside them), `RUNBOOK.md` if a rate or an option changes, a measurement note

The step's one DuckDB connection has `memory_limit = DUCK_MEMORY_LIMIT` ("2GB", `genecall.py` line 181), one thread, a temp directory. The same step passed on `rM.2` (23.0 CPU-minutes, peak 2.66 GB) and on `r26.11.1` (27.5 CPU-minutes, 2.72 GB). `_family_run` is one statement over every domain row of every stage (`SELECT DISTINCT`, a `row_number()` window by protein, a join with the tapes unnested): nothing in it is cut by size, where `tokens.run` cuts its work into protein ranges from the memory limit (`tokens.n_ranges`, card B-46).
Do, test first: (1) reproduce it small: a constructed store on which `_family_run` fails with this error at a memory limit scaled down to it (the existing tests pass a smaller limit for the other parts; find why they did not catch this), seen failing. (2) Make the family run bounded as the rest of the module is: cut it into protein ranges as `tokens.run` does (the window is by protein, so a range of proteins is independent), or another way that keeps one statement's memory under the limit whatever the release's size; say which and why. The output is the same rows in the same order to the byte as today's on every release where today's code passes (pin it on the fixture; and on the pod, read-only into a scratch output root, on `r26.11.1`: the published postings files' sha256 equal the new code's). (3) Not acceptable as the whole fix: a larger constant alone (it fails again two releases later); acceptable beside the ranges if the measurement says the limit is simply too small for one range. **The orchestrator's probe of 2026-11-21 found a second fault behind the first** (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 15:22 to 15:33, unit `b62-probe8`: `postings.run` of the frozen tree `rel1121` on `r26.11.2` with `memory_limit="8GB"`, the data root bound read-only, output and scratch under `/tmp/if2453/b62/`; `probe8.log`, script `b62_probe.py` beside it): the family run **passed** at 8 GB, and the process's peak RSS was 8.41 GB, so the statement took all it was given and what it needs is not known; then, after 649 s and 10.7 CPU-minutes, the step failed with `IOException: IO Error: Cannot open file ".../runs/canon-000544.parquet": Too many open files`. `_explode_runs` had written at least 545 `canon-*.parquet` files beside its runs, and `_check_canonical` and the final sort hand every one of them to a single `read_parquet([...])`; a user unit on the pod has a soft limit of 1,024 open files (*measured*, `ulimit -Sn` in a `systemd-run --user` unit: 1024; hard 1,048,576; `pod_run.sh` sets none). So the number of run files grows with the release and the step opens them all at once. (4) Fix that too, test first (a constructed store with more runs than a soft limit the test sets with `resource.setrlimit`): bound how many files one statement opens (larger runs, or a merge in passes), or raise the soft limit to the hard one at the step's start and say what bounds the count; not the limit alone if the count still grows without bound.
Accept: the reproduction fails first and passes; byte-identical postings on the fixture and on `r26.11.1`; peak RSS under the limit plus a stated margin on `r26.11.2`'s inputs (scratch, read-only); the whole suite on the branch. **Then the release**: `release_train.sh run` from a mirror that carries the fix resumes `r26.11.2` at `postings` (card A-15: `--dry-run` must say `Action: resume r26.11.2`; it compares the `build-stage` command and the profile with the first launch's, which a change under `src/` does not alter; the run records of the remaining steps will carry the new code's id, which is the honest record).
Done 2026-11-21 (STATUS row B-62; `planning/measurements/2026-11-21_b62_postings_bounds.md`): `_family_run` routes domain rows and unnested protein slots by `hash(protein_uid) % k` (`k = tokens.n_ranges`, `_ROUTE_OPEN_FILES = 64`, `_ROUTE_HELD_ROWS = 262_144`) and joins one range at a time; `_explode_runs` coalesces exploded batches up to `_RUN_MAX_ROWS = 50_000_000` rows and `_coalesce_parquet` caps `read_parquet` inputs at `_MAX_READ_FILES = 64` (2 descriptors open at once). Verified read-only on `r26.11.1` (16/16 files sha256 byte-identical, 28.09 CPU-min, 2.68 GiB RSS) and `r26.11.2` (`memory_limit="2GB"`, `RLIMIT_NOFILE=1024`: 252,132,516 rows, 16 files, 36.09 CPU-min, 2.80 GiB RSS). Resuming `r26.11.2` next.

### A-20 Post-manifest resume and host-profile pack directory in `release_train.sh`
**M** · Pod: yes (stub tests and read-only `--dry-run` check against `/tmp/if2453/c46/train`) · Depends: A-15, A-19 · found 2026-11-22 when `r26.11.2`'s `release_examples.sh` failed after `manifests/release=r26.11.2.json` and `pack` were published
Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/release_train.sh`, `tests/r1/test_a20_train_post_manifest_resume.py` (new), `RUNBOOK.md` (if documenting post-manifest resume and pack promotion)
Why: (1) In `release_train.sh`, `resume_state` checks `started(old["release"]) and not finished(old["release"])`, where `finished(rel)` is `(root / "manifests" / f"release={rel}.json").exists()`. When `build-stage` finishes (publishing `manifests/release=<R>.json`), and `pack`, `release_examples.sh` or `train_check.py` fails before `$WORK/DONE` is written (as happened on `r26.11.2` at `01:37` CST on 2026-11-22 when `seq-oracle` OOMed), rerunning `release_train.sh --dry-run` or `release_train.sh run` treats the run as `first`, sees `nothing_new = True` (`latest_release == <R>`), and exits `0` saying `0 new complete stages since <R>; nothing to cut` without finishing `pack` / `examples` / `check` or writing `$WORK/DONE`. Meanwhile, `do_run` must still withdraw `$WORK/DONE` immediately after reading its prior value (`prev_done`) so a second run that fails never leaves an earlier `DONE` behind (`test_train_a_second_run_withdraws_the_marker_of_the_first_when_it_fails`), and a `$WORK` whose `DONE` already holds `old["release"]` must proceed as `first` when cutting the next release (`test_a15_a_work_reused_after_a_finished_release_watches_a_unit_of_a_new_name`). (2) `make_profile` rewrites `tmp:` in `$PROF_COPY` to `$WORK/tmp`, so `pack build --profile "$PROF_COPY"` writes the verified pack under `$WORK/tmp/packs/release=<R>/<pack_id>/` instead of `<REALPROF.tmp>/packs/release=<R>/<pack_id>/` (`/tmp/if2453/bio-box/packs` on `wilkcomp02`, card W-22): `bio-box releases --profile profiles/wilkcomp02.yaml` then shows `-` for `pack` and `read` (not `read,search`) for `latest`, and cleaning `$WORK/tmp` after backup would delete the pack. (3) On a post-manifest resume where `$WORK/tmp/packs/release=<R>/<pack_id>/pack_verified.json` (or `<REALPROF.tmp>/packs/release=<R>/<pack_id>/pack_verified.json`) already exists on disk and `pack verify` exits `0`, re-running `pack build` wastes ~120 wall minutes (~620 CPU-minutes on `r26.11.2`); note that on a fresh launch (`resume = 0`), or when no `pack_verified.json` exists on disk, `pack build` must always run first so stub tests of `pack` unit failure (`test_train_a_unit_that_exited_nonzero_is_the_exit_status`) are untouched.
Do, test first: (1) Write stub tests in `tests/r1/test_a20_train_post_manifest_resume.py` covering: (a) `manifests/release=r26.11.2.json` published and `$WORK/DONE` absent -> `--dry-run` reports `Action: resume r26.11.2 (...; unit <n>)` and `run` resumes `r26.11.2` (keeping `before/listing.tsv`, `before/verify-*.log` and `estimate/before.json`, verifying earlier releases into `resume-<n>/`, skipping `pack build` when `pack_verified.json` is on disk and `pack verify` exits `0`, running `release_examples.sh` and `train_check.py`, and writing `r26.11.2` to `$WORK/DONE`); (b) when `manifests/release=r26.11.2.json` is published, `$WORK/DONE` is absent, and a new complete stage `S2` appeared meanwhile -> refused (`finish r26.11.2 before cutting over new stages`); (c) when `$WORK/DONE` already holds `r26.11.2` -> `0 new complete stages` noop keeps `$WORK/DONE` (or if a second run actually starts and fails on a step, `$WORK/DONE` is withdrawn as `test_driver_exit_status.py` requires), and when a new stage is complete it cuts `r26.11.3` as `first`; (d) when `ROOT == REAL_ROOT` (`REAL_ROOT=${REAL_ROOT:-/tmp/if2453/r10/root}` so tests can set `REAL_ROOT`) and `<REALPROF.tmp>/packs` differs from `$WORK/tmp/packs`, after `pack verify --profile "$PROF_COPY"` passes, `release_train.sh` publishes the pack directory into `<REALPROF.tmp>/packs/release=<R>/<pack_id>` (via `cp -al` into a dot-temp sibling + `mv -n`, falling back to `cp -a` across filesystems; never overwriting an existing directory), runs `pack verify --profile "$REALPROF"`, and refreshes `$WORK/after/releases.txt` with `--profile "$REALPROF"`. (2) Implement in `release_train.sh` without breaking any existing test in `test_driver_exit_status.py`, `test_c46_release_train.py`, `test_a19_driver_waits.py` or `test_stage_driver.py`. (3) Run read-only `WORK=/tmp/if2453/c46/train ROOT=/tmp/if2453/r10/root bash .../release_train.sh --dry-run --no-live-cpu` on `wilkcomp02` and confirm it prints `Action: resume r26.11.2 (122 run records published; unit 4)` and writes nothing.
Accept: new tests fail first and pass; `test_driver_exit_status.py`, `test_c46_release_train.py`, `test_a19_driver_waits.py`, `test_stage_driver.py` and `tests/r0` pass on the pod (including with pod tools hidden); read-only `--dry-run` on `/tmp/if2453/c46/train` prints `Action: resume r26.11.2`.
Done 2026-11-22 (STATUS row A-20): `release_train.sh` resumes post-manifest failures when `$WORK/DONE` does not record `old["release"]` (preserving `DONE` withdrawal on failing runs and restoring `DONE` on `0 new complete stages` noops), reuses an on-disk `pack_verified.json` pack on resume when `pack verify` exits 0, and promotes the verified pack to `<REALPROF.tmp>/packs/release=<R>/<pack_id>` when `ROOT == REAL_ROOT`; live read-only `--dry-run` on `/tmp/if2453/c46/train` prints `Action: resume r26.11.2 (122 run records published; unit 4)`.

**L** · Pod: yes (pytest + read-only verification on live stage `G1` in `/tmp/if2453/r10/root`; zero writes to data root, `/tmp/if2453/stages/g1`, or `/stor`) · Depends: A-12, B-47b, C-38f, C-38h · found 2026-11-22 at `23:49` CST when `G1` (`mode run` and `mode after` both `exit 0`) failed `stage_check.py` on 1 shared-contig protein between `C5` and `P2`

Why: On live stage `G1` (`/tmp/if2453/stages/g1/check/1/check.out`), all 106 build steps and `mode after` (all 8 releases verified unchanged) passed cleanly (`14,710` copied proteins, `19,286` copied rows, `stage_not_in_source = 0` and `source_not_in_stage = 0` on every source), and `stage_check.py` exited `1` solely on `copy_check` (`copied rows from C5 differ: {'stage': 'C5', 'proteins': 0, 'plan_proteins': 1, 'rows_in_stage': 0, 'rows_in_source': 0, 'plan_rows': 1, ...}; copied rows from P2 differ: {'stage': 'P2', 'proteins': 4199, 'plan_proteins': 4198, 'rows_in_stage': 6064, 'rows_in_source': 6064, 'plan_rows': 6063, ...}`). In `domains._measured_uids` (`src/biobox/l1/domains.py:710-762`), a stage copy source `s` measures `s["protein_files"]` **plus** any shared-contig proteins in `s["owned"]` (`C-38h`: `ps.protein_uid` from each `o` in `s["owned"]["stages"]` `SEMI JOIN` `fp.protein_uid` on contigs in `s["owned"]["genome_input"]`), and a round copy source (`"coverage_files" in s`, `C-38f`) measures `s["coverage_files"]`. `stage_check.py` lines 412–415 checked only `s["protein_files"]` (and assumed `s["stage"]`), so the 1 protein that `C5` (`k = 0`, preceding `P2` alphabetically in `copy["sources"]`) measured via `C5`'s `"owned"` block was missed under `C5` and claimed later by `P2` (`k = 3`), while a round source would raise `KeyError: 'protein_files'`. Every subsequent annotated stage (`V2`, `GV1`, `PL2`, `PL3`, `C1..C4`, `C6`) sees `C5` and `G1` in `copy["sources"]` and would hit the same check bug.
Do, test first (AGENTS §5.1): (1) Write `tests/r1/test_a21_stage_check_copy_sources.py` covering (a) a stage copy source `C5` (`"C5" < "P2"`) that measured a shared-contig protein via `s["owned"]` (`genome_input` + owner `P2`'s `feature_protein` and `protein_files`) before `P2` in `copy["sources"]` (seen failing first with `copied rows from C5 differ` / `copied rows from P2 differ`, passing after fix, and still failing when the copied domain row in the checked stage is altered), and (b) a round copy source (`"coverage_files" in s`, `"round": "r-..."`, seen failing first with `KeyError`, passing after fix, and still failing when the copied row is altered). (2) In `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stage_check.py` (keeping zero `to_pylist()`, `to_pydict()`, or `pq.read_table` calls so `test_the_check_reads_tables_through_duckdb_and_not_whole_into_python` passes), build the DuckDB subquery of measured `protein_uid`s for each source `s` in `copy["sources"]` matching `domains._measured_uids` (`s["coverage_files"]` when `"coverage_files" in s`, else `s["protein_files"]` union any `s["owned"]` `ps SEMI JOIN (fp SEMI JOIN gi USING (contig_uid)) USING (protein_uid)`), and use `s.get("stage") or s.get("round")` for the source label in `pf["sources"]`, `cc["sources"]`, and failure messages. (3) Run `pod_pytest.sh` on `wilkcomp02` (normal PATH and tools-hidden `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin` on `test_a21_stage_check_copy_sources.py`, `test_stage_driver.py`, `test_a16_rate_guard_bands.py`, `test_a19_driver_waits.py`, `tests/r0`) AND run `stage_check.py` read-only against live `G1` (`/tmp/if2453/r10/root:ro`, `/tmp/if2453/stages/g1/{before,after,estimate}:ro`, writing only to scratch `--out /tmp/if2453/ci-tmp/a21-g1-check` and cleaning `/tmp/if2453/ci-tmp/a21*` afterwards) to verify `RESULT: all checks pass` (`exit 0`, `failed == []`).
Accept: new tests fail first and pass (normal and tools-hidden PATH); `test_stage_driver.py`, `test_a16_rate_guard_bands.py`, `test_a19_driver_waits.py`, and `tests/r0` pass on the pod; live read-only `stage_check.py` on `G1` prints `RESULT: all checks pass` (`exit 0`).

Scaling `PACK_PARTS`, `PACK_JOBS`, `PACK_MEM`, `EX_THREADS`, and `EX_MEM` dynamically under the profile's `mem_scope` and live `C-28` thread allowance saves `~2.5–3 hours` wall per release with zero change to any output bytes (`W-35`).

(2) Implement in `src/biobox/l2/cluster.py` and `src/biobox/stage.py`. (3) Run pod pytest (normal and tools-hidden PATH).

### B-73 Coalesce undersized `seq_shards` ingest batches against the `LY-04` `64 MB` floor
**L** · Pod: yes (pytest only; zero writes to `/tmp/if2453/r10/root` or `/stor`) · Depends: B-05, B-57 · found 2026-11-25 at `17:08` CST when live `V2.ingest` exited `2` on `LY-04` (`shard-0000.fa.gz` `15 MB` + `shard-0001.fa.gz` `2.4 GB`)
Files: `src/biobox/l0seq/store.py`, `tests/r1/test_b73_ingest_shard_floor.py` (new)
Why: `store._batches(objs, shard_bases)` (`src/biobox/l0seq/store.py:358`) cuts a new greedy shard batch whenever adding the next input object exceeds `shard_bases` (`SHARD_BASES = 2**30`, `1.074 Gbp`), without checking whether the outgoing batch is smaller than the `LY-04` `64 MB` compressed file floor. Whenever a multi-object stage has a small object before a large object (`V2`: `jgi_inovirus` `50 MB` plain -> `15 MB` bgzipped before `jgi_img_vr` `8.1 GB` -> `2.4 GB`; same shape audited in `PL2`, `O1`, and `ECE1`), `_batches` emits a `< 64 MB` shard beside a `>= 64 MB` shard and `run-step` cleanly refuses publication (`exit 2`, `LY-04`).
Do, test first (AGENTS §5.1): (1) Write failing-first R1 tests in `tests/r1/test_b73_ingest_shard_floor.py` covering: (a) a small object (`50 Mbp`) followed by a large object (`2.0 Gbp`) under `SHARD_BASES` coalesces into 1 batch when `min_shard_bases = MIN_SHARD_BASES` (`320 * 2**20`, `~104 MB` bgzipped); (b) a large object followed by a small tail object coalesces into 1 batch; (c) two small objects on either side of a large object (all coalesced, preserving contiguous `obj_idx` runs so `ingest_batch` byte-identity holds); (d) a single-object stage (small or large) stays 1 batch; (e) two large objects (`>= MIN_SHARD_BASES`) still split into 2 batches; (f) `pass1_shards()` continues calling `_batches(..., min_shard_bases=0)` unchanged; (g) small `shard_bases <= min_shard_bases` test fixtures (`shard_bases=200_000`) do not coalesce; (h) an end-to-end `ingest` / `ingest_plan` + `ingest_batch` + `ingest_merge` byte-identity parity test with `MIN_SHARD_BASES` active (`store_params.json` unchanged). See the new tests fail first via `pod_pytest.sh`. (2) Implement `MIN_SHARD_BASES = 320 * 2**20` and optional `min_shard_bases: int = 0` on `_batches()` in `src/biobox/l0seq/store.py`, merging any batch `< min_shard_bases` into its smaller adjacent batch when `0 < min_shard_bases < shard_bases` and `len(batches) > 1`, and passing `min_shard_bases=MIN_SHARD_BASES` from `_decide()` (never from `pass1_shards()`). (3) Run `pod_pytest.sh` (normal and `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`) on `test_b73_ingest_shard_floor.py`, `test_ingest_batches.py`, `test_ingest_restart.py`, `test_b57_coalesce_tail.py`, and `tests/r0`.
Accept: new tests fail first and pass (normal and tools-hidden PATH); `test_ingest_batches.py`, `test_ingest_restart.py`, `test_b57_coalesce_tail.py`, and `tests/r0` pass; `store_params.json` unchanged; zero writes to data root or `/stor`.
Done 2026-11-25 (STATUS row B-73; commit `5d17859`): `src/biobox/l0seq/store.py` adds `MIN_SHARD_BASES = 320 * 2**20` (`335,544,320` bp) and optional `min_shard_bases: int = 0` on `_batches()`, coalescing undersized batches into their smaller adjacent neighbor when `len(batches) > 1` and `0 < min_shard_bases < shard_bases` (preserving contiguous `obj_idx` runs, `pass1_shards()` unchanged, `_params` unchanged); `_decide()` passes `MIN_SHARD_BASES`; pod pytest `277 passed` (0 warnings) on `test_b73_ingest_shard_floor.py`, `test_ingest_batches.py`, `test_ingest_restart.py`, `test_b57_coalesce_tail.py`, and `tests/r0`.

### A-28 External bare-metal compute & UT Austin TACC parallelization decision (`Profile A` stage builds & `Profile B` release clustering)
**H** · Pod: no · Depends: none · PI decision card (`2026-11-25`)
Files: `planning/design/A-28_bare_metal_compute.md`, `planning/DECISIONS.md` (append the next free row once PI decides; `DEC-78` was taken on 2026-11-25 by the wording of "linclust NR90" and `DEC-79` on 2026-11-26 by the members of `r26.11.5`, so this card's row has no number until it is written)
Why: `wilkcomp02` (`112` threads, `1.0 TiB` RAM, `1.1 TiB` `/tmp` NVMe, shared `40`-day / `80`-night cap) will remain comfortable through `r26.11.4` (`287 Gbp`, `DEC-77`), but `r26.11.5+` (`400–800+ Gbp`) will hit `/tmp` scratch limits during clustering and benefit from horizontal stage-build capacity. Because `bio-box` runs inside the pinned x86_64 `.sif` image (`bio-box_faec1f4.sif`) with stage-partitioned immutability (`AGENTS.md` §3.10) and cross-host byte-identity (§4.3), external bare metal requires zero core-code changes — only a dedicated `profiles/<host>.yaml` and a checksummed atomic sync workflow.
Findings (full tables & formulas in `planning/design/A-28_bare_metal_compute.md`):
- **Profile B (Burst 24–48 h big-release clustering, 128–512t, 1.5–3 TB RAM):** **Oracle Cloud (OCI) Bare Metal** wins (`BM.Standard.E6.256`: `256c/512t` Turin, `3 TB` RAM + `10 TB` block = `$14.42/hr` -> `~$346` per 24 h burst *derived*; `BM.DenseIO.E5.128`: `128c/256t` Genoa, `1.5 TB` RAM, `81.6 TB` local NVMe = `$15.60/hr` -> `~$374` per 24 h burst *derived*; `$0` setup, `10 TB/mo` free egress, `$0` idle burn). Avoid AWS EC2 `.metal` (`$90/TB` egress trap + Spot preemption risk) and Equinix Metal (EOL June 30, 2026).
- **Profile A (Sustained stage building, 64–192t, 256–512 GB RAM, 4–8 TB NVMe):** **Cherry Servers** US Chicago EPYC 9554P (`64c/128t`, `384 GB` DDR5, `$1.34/hr` or `$630–$783/mo`, `$0` setup, `100 TB` free egress) or **Hetzner Server Auction** EU (`~$165–$495/mo`, `€0` setup, unmetered egress) or **OCI `VM.Standard.E5.Flex`** on-demand (`128 vCPU / 512 GB` + `4 TB` block = `$3.18/hr`, `~$76`/24 h burst).
- **Free UT Austin TACC parallel track:** File a 30-minute **TxRAS Lonestar6** (`128c / 256 GB`, Profile A) + **NSF ACCESS Stampede3 `nvdimm`** (`4 TB RAM / 80c`, Profile B) Startup Allocation; native `tacc-apptainer` runs our x86_64 `.sif` unchanged with `$0` campus egress (skip ARM Vista).
Decisions needed from PI (record in this card's row): (Q1) approve pay-as-you-go **OCI** for Profile B bursts when releases outgrow `wilkcomp02`'s `1.1 TiB` `/tmp`; (Q2) select Profile A vendor (Cherry Servers vs Hetzner Auction vs OCI hourly vs defer to TACC); (Q3) approve submitting the free TACC TxRAS + ACCESS Startup allocations; (Q4) authorize follow-on **L** card `A-28a` (`profiles/<host>.yaml`, tar+checksum+collision-check sync script, and `S0` cross-host byte-identity gate).
Accept: PI answers Q1–Q4 and this card's row is appended to `planning/DECISIONS.md`.

Two physical effects caused `1.763×`: (1) environmental metagenomes + archaea (`SPX1 + G1`) collapsed less at NR90/NR50 step 0 than strain-rich `C5` in `r26.11.2`, so `input_step1` survivors jumped **`2.224×`** (`24,298,242` vs `10,927,842` targets; `6,476,355,769` vs `3,029,785,935` residues = `2.138×`); and (2) `mmseqs createindex` k-mer sorting scales super-linearly with target k-mer count (`12.08`, `12.03`, `17.58` CPU-min/`10^9` residues at `10.06M`, `10.93M`, `24.30M` targets for `-s 2`, and `15.62`, `15.56` CPU-min/`10^9` residues for `-s 3` on `r26.11.1` and `r26.11.2`), so upcoming `cluster.nr50.19.prefilter.cut` (`-s 3`) would likewise run around `~140` CPU-min against `79.05` (`~1.77×`) without refitting both `-s 2` and `-s 3`. Moreover, because `A-15` keeps `estimate/before.json` untouched on `resume`, `check_rates` itself (running stdlib-only via `python3 -I` on the host) must price shared-index `.cut` steps directly from `<root>/l2/cluster_plan/release=<R>/<call>.cut.json` (`parts` `units`/`keys` + `index.argv` `-s`), just as `check_rates` already prices `.part-*` from `<call>.cut.json`.

### A-22 `r50_watch.py` honours `DEC-29`'s `one_process` clustering exemption (`DEC-38`)
**L** · Pod: yes (pytest only; zero writes to data root or `/stor`) · Depends: B-45, C-46 · DEC-29, DEC-38 · found 2026-11-23 before launching `r26.11.3` (`104,510,909` `protein_seq` rows, `~102.34M` distinct proteins > `74M` `DEC-29` threshold)
Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/r50_watch.py`, `tests/r1/test_a22_watch_dec29.py` (new)
Why: Above `~74M` distinct proteins (`r26.11.3` is the first release above `74M`), `cluster.nr90.11.prefilter`'s per-part k-mer table rebuild overhead exceeds `part_cpu_min = 240.0`, so `l2.cluster._one_process_doc` (`src/biobox/l2/cluster.py:1515-1535`, card B-45, `DEC-29`) writes `l2/cluster_plan/release=<R>/<call>.cut.json` with `"one_process": {"decision": "DEC-29", "cpu_min": <est>, "cpu_min_max": <cluster_cpu_min_max>, ...}`, and `build-stage` (`src/biobox/stage.py:2520-2525`) checks `cpu_min <= prof.cluster_cpu_min_max` (`20,000` CPU-min on `wilkcomp02`) and runs that single step as one process with `cpu_budget_reason = "exempt: DEC-29"` while holding every other clustering step/part to `cpu_min_per_process` (`DEC-38`). However, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/r50_watch.py` (`lines 160-181`, invoked by `release_train.sh` without `--cluster-cpu-max` so regular parts stay capped at `280` CPU-min) checks `cm > ccap` (`280`) on every published record and `r["cpu_min"] > ccap` (`280`) on every live `release cluster` process without consulting `DEC-29`, so `r50_watch.py` would kill `r26.11.3`'s `cluster.nr90.11.prefilter` (~4,300 CPU-min) as soon as it crosses `280` CPU-min (~8 wall minutes in) and fail on its published record thereafter.

### B-65 Bounded row-group-pruning `export.protein_seqs` and `hits_faa` at multi-stage scale
**M** · Pod: yes (read-only verification on `r26.11.2` in `/tmp/if2453/r10/root`) · Depends: W-23, B-27 · found 2026-11-22 when `release_examples.sh r26.11.2` failed in `seq-oracle` on both worked examples
Files: `src/biobox/search/export.py`, `examples/crispr_cas_neighbourhoods/run.sh`, `examples/rnap/run.sh`, `tests/r2/test_b65_protein_seqs_bounds.py` (new), `planning/measurements/2026-11-22_b65_protein_seqs_bounds.md` (new)
Why: On `r26.11.2` (`11` stages, `59,418,086` `protein_seq` rows across `~490` row groups), both worked examples failed in `seq-oracle` (`bio-box seq --root ... --release r26.11.2 --proteins-file .../oracle_uids.txt`, `4,265` uids in `crispr_cas_neighbourhoods` and `2,752` uids in `rnap`) with `_duckdb.OutOfMemoryException: Out of Memory Error: failed to allocate data of size 2.0 MiB (1.8 GiB/1.8 GiB used)` at `src/biobox/search/export.py:996` (`MemoryPeak = 2.36 GB`). Three causes combined: (1) `export.protein_seqs` opened DuckDB with `relations=("prot",)` where `prot` is a `UNION ALL` of 11 per-stage `read_parquet` scans (`keep = ("stage",)`), and `set_join_key_filter` capped `dynamic_or_filter_threshold` at `largest = 490`; above `490` keys DuckDB dropped key-list pruning and pushed only `[min(uid), max(uid)]`, scanning all `59.4M` `seq` strings (~18 GB uncompressed) across all 11 branches simultaneously; (2) `open_store` left DuckDB's `preserve_insertion_order = True` enabled, buffering unordered Parquet chunks in RAM; (3) `examples/crispr_cas_neighbourhoods/run.sh` (line 128) and `examples/rnap/run.sh` (line 85) did not pass `--threads "$THREADS"` to `seq`, so DuckDB ran `EXPORT_THREADS = 4` inside a `2`-CPU unit. `export.hits_faa` (`export.py:844`) uses the same `SELECT protein_uid, min(seq) AS seq FROM prot WHERE protein_uid IN (SELECT protein_uid FROM _p) GROUP BY protein_uid` pattern and will hit the same wall as stage count grows.
Do, test first (AGENTS §4.1, §4.3, §4.4, §5.1): (1) Write a test in `tests/r2/test_b65_protein_seqs_bounds.py` on a multi-stage synthetic store with many small row groups (`row_group_size = 8`, e.g. `>= 120` row groups across `>= 4` stages) and a tight memory limit (or verifying row-group pruning when `len(uids) > num_row_groups` for clustered keys and bounded memory when `len(uids)` spans many row groups, plus byte-for-byte agreement of `protein_seqs` and `hits_faa` with an independent brute-force reader across shared cross-stage proteins). Show the pruning or tight-memory test failing first on the current DuckDB implementation (`dynamic_or_filter_threshold` exceeded -> scans all row groups / OOMs under a tight DuckDB cap). (2) In `src/biobox/search/export.py`: (a) set `"preserve_insertion_order": False` in `open_store.__enter__`'s `duckdb.connect(config={...})`; (b) rewrite `protein_seqs` to validate `files = release_files(root, release, manifest)` and read directly from `_store_files(Path(root), "protein_seq", {"gene_call_version": gene_call_version}, files)` one file and one matching row group at a time using Parquet footer `min`/`max` statistics on `protein_uid` (enforcing `LY-02`/`LY-03` sorted/stats checks as `KeyLookup` does, bisecting `wanted = sorted(set(uids))` against each row group's `[lo, hi]`, reading only `["protein_uid", "length", "seq"]` for row groups where `wanted` intersects `[lo, hi]`, filtering with `pyarrow.compute.is_in`, aggregating `(max(length), min(seq))` across stages, and releasing each row group immediately; return `(out, scans)` with `scans` reporting `files`, `files_read`, `row_groups_scanned`, `row_groups_total`, `rows_scanned` so `test_w23_commands.py` passes unchanged); (c) update `hits_faa` to stream rows from `_h`/`_genes`/`_plen` in chunks of `_BATCH_ROWS` (`8,192`) without scanning `prot.seq` in DuckDB, resolving each chunk's sequences via `protein_seqs` so `hits_faa` holds at most `_BATCH_ROWS` sequences in RAM regardless of release or hit-set size. (3) Add `--threads "$THREADS"` to `bb seq-oracle seq ...` in `examples/crispr_cas_neighbourhoods/run.sh` and `examples/rnap/run.sh`. (4) On `wilkcomp02`, run `bio-box seq` read-only on `/tmp/if2453/r10/root` (`--release r26.11.2`) against both `/tmp/if2453/c46/train/examples/crispr_cas_neighbourhoods/oracle_uids.txt` (`4,265` uids) and `/tmp/if2453/c46/train/examples/rnap/oracle_uids.txt` (`2,752` uids) inside a `MemoryMax=1G` scope (`--threads 2`, writing to scratch `/tmp/if2453/b65/`), recording wall time, CPU time and `MemoryPeak` in `planning/measurements/2026-11-22_b65_protein_seqs_bounds.md`, then clean `/tmp/if2453/b65/`.
Accept: new tests fail first and pass; `test_w23_commands.py`, all `tests/r2/*export*` / `*search*` tests and the full test suite pass on the pod (including tools-hidden); both real `r26.11.2` `seq-oracle` calls exit `0` under `MemoryMax=1G` with peak RSS `< 512 MiB`; measurement note committed.
Done 2026-11-22 (STATUS row B-65; `planning/measurements/2026-11-22_b65_protein_seqs_bounds.md`): `open_store` sets `preserve_insertion_order=False`; `protein_seqs` bisects Parquet row-group `min`/`max` statistics with `LY-02`/`LY-03` checks and streams matching row groups one file/batch at a time (`use_threads=False`, `pre_buffer=False`); `hits_faa` streams `_BATCH_ROWS` chunks through `protein_seqs`; both worked examples pass `--threads "$THREADS"` to `seq-oracle` and exit `0` on `r26.11.2` under `MemoryMax=1G` at `433.96 MiB` and `427.39 MiB` cgroup `MemoryPeak` (`318.2 MiB` `ru_maxrss`).

### B-66 `O(1)`-stage `SeqStore` point lookups and per-stored-contig CRISPR CPU-minute term
**M** · Pod: yes (read-only on `/tmp/if2453/r10/root` and `/tmp/if2453/s2/inputs/{G1,SPX1}`; scratch under `/tmp/if2453/b66/` only; nothing built in the data root) · Depends: B-53, B-60, A-12, A-17 · found 2026-11-22 when `G1` (`1,227,214` stored contigs, 12 stages on disk) stopped on `DEC-64`'s `> 1.50` rate guard at `crispr-minced-0.4.2-w8-nr3-r23x47-s26x50` (`66.117` CPU-min vs `28.136` estimated, ratio `2.350`, `wall_s = 2,469.9`) and recorded `genecall` at `1.298` (`212.639` vs `163.790`)
Files: `src/biobox/l0seq/lookup.py`, `src/biobox/l0seq/store.py`, `src/biobox/stage.py`, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stage_est.py`, `RUNBOOK.md` (the CRISPR rate row only), `scripts/stress/b66_crispr_contig_arms.py` (new), `tests/r1/test_b66_seqstore_and_crispr_contigs.py` (new), `planning/measurements/2026-11-22_b66_seqstore_and_crispr_contigs.md` (new)
Why: Two causes combined on the first million-contig, 12-stage build (`stage=G1`, *measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-22):
(1) **`SeqStore.get` scales linearly with `len(stages)` and calls `locator.get(rd)` twice**: `SeqStore` puts all 12 stages' `contig_index` files into one `KeyLookup` and all 12 stages' `seq_locator` files into one `KeyLookup`, and `KeyLookup.get(key)` binary-searches every file whose `[min, max]` covers `key` (every stage's `[0x00.., 0xff..]` hash range), even though `is_default_variant = True` per `contig_uid` and `raw_digest` in `seq_locator` are globally unique across stages (`DEC-26`, `store.ingest` anti-join); then `SeqStore.get` calls `length_of(rd)` and `raw(rd)`, running that 12-file `locator.get(rd)` scan a second time. On 20,000 `G1` contigs (`165.41 Mbp`), `store12.get` cost `1.422 ms/contig` (`default_digest = 0.322 ms`, `length_of = 0.350 ms`, `raw+upper = 0.750 ms` -> `29.1` CPU-min across `1.227M` contigs in both `crispr` and `genecall`), whereas stopping at the first matching stage (`stages[0] == "G1"`) and locating `rd` once cut `SeqStore.get` to `0.517 ms/contig` (`-63.6%`, saving `~18.5` CPU-min on `G1` and `~46+` CPU-min on `SPX1`, and preventing `cache_row_groups = 64` LRU thrashing once cumulative `seq_locator` row groups across stages pass 64).
(2) **`stage.crispr_cpu_min` has no per-stored-contig term**: even with `O(1)`-stage `SeqStore.get`, `crispr._scan` reads contigs in `contig_uid` order (`blake3`) from shards sorted by `raw_digest` (`sha256`), decompressing ~1.12 BGZF `65,280`-byte blocks per short contig (`8.86×` amplification on `G1`'s `8.18 kb` contigs: `0.394 ms/contig`), plus `ContigOwners` (`0.056 ms/contig`), `KeyLookup` (`0.123 ms/contig`), FASTA batch formatting, GFF parsing and per-contig DuckDB ledger sorting (`~0.58 ms/contig` on `G1`), totaling **`~1.15 ms` per stored contig** (`~19` CPU-min per million contigs). Card B-60 tested up to `168k` contigs (`V1`), where `168k × 1.15 ms = 3.2` CPU-min was small; at `G1` (`1.23M` contigs) and `SPX1` (`2.83M` contigs, `35.89 Gbp`) omitting the per-contig term under-estimates CRISPR by `1.8×–2.3×` and triggers `DEC-64` stops.
Do, test first (`tests/r1/test_b66_seqstore_and_crispr_contigs.py` seen failing before code edits; AGENTS §4.1, §4.3, §4.4, §5.1, §6):
(1) **Tests first**: on a synthetic multi-stage store (`>= 10` stages, each with sorted `contig_index` and `seq_locator`), assert that `SeqStore(root, ["S0", "S1", ..., "S9"]).get(cu)` for a contig stored in `"S0"` reads at most `1` `contig_index` row group and `1` `seq_locator` row group (`store.index.row_groups_read == 1` and `store.locator.row_groups_read == 1`, failing on the current code which reads all 10 stages' row groups!), still resolves contigs stored in earlier stages (`"S9"`), raises the identical `KeyError` when absent from all opened stages, and returns byte-identical slices across linear, reverse-complement, and circular origin-wrapping coordinates; and assert `bs.crispr_cpu_min` accepts keyword `contigs: int = 0` (`contigs=0` bit-identical to card B-53's formula, keeping `CRISPR_CPU_S_PER_GBP = 168.1` and `CRISPR_NEAR_CAS_COST = 1.15` unchanged), `bs.stage_contigs(root, stage)` counts default-variant rows in `ingest.json`'s explicit `contig_index` files in bounded memory (skipping contigs owned by earlier stages), and `crispr_refusal` plus `stage_est.py` (`before` and `rates`) pass stored contig counts to `crispr_cpu_min`.
(2) **Implement `KeyLookup.find_first` and `SeqStore` single-locate `get`**: in `lookup.py`, add `find_first(self, key, predicate=None) -> dict | None` (checking files in order and returning the first row matching `predicate`, or the first row with `key_column == key` when `predicate is None`, without decoding later files' row groups; `get(key)` stays unchanged for multi-row callers); in `store.py`, use `self.index.find_first(contig_uid, lambda r: r["is_default_variant"])` in `default_digest`, `self.locator.find_first(rd)` in `_where`, and call `self._where(rd)` once inside `SeqStore.get` (handling `circular` origin wrap with two `self._shard(rel).fetch(...)` calls on the already-located `(rel, n)`).
(3) **Measure in scratch on `wilkcomp02` and fit `CRISPR_CPU_S_PER_K_CONTIGS`**: with units named `b66-*` (`<= 8` threads total, `MemoryMax=16G`, `nice 19`, `/tmp/if2453/r10/root:ro`, scratch `/tmp/if2453/b66/` cleaned at the end), run end-to-end `crispr.run` (and `crispr.run_near_cas` on `G1` using synthetic/sampled domain inputs or `V1`/`PL1` plus `G1`'s real `crispr.run` on a `1-in-20` genome sample in a scratch view) and a `1-in-50` sample of `SPX1` (`>= 5,000` bp contigs ingested in scratch) with the fixed `SeqStore`: verify published CRISPR `feature`, `l1_crispr` and `ledger` Parquet files on `micro_s0` / planted-array fixtures are sha256 byte-identical (`test_b55_crispr_scale_and_parity.py`), measure the post-fix per-stored-contig CPU-seconds `CRISPR_CPU_S_PER_K_CONTIGS`, check the resulting `crispr_cpu_min(bases, flank, contigs=n_stored, near_cas=...)` across all 11 complete stages (`S1, P1..P6, H1, PL1, V1, C5`), `G1` (noting `G1`'s pre-fix default record `66.117` CPU-min vs post-fix estimate and `G1`'s upcoming post-fix `near-Cas` step), `SPX1` (`2,829,368` contigs, `35.885` Gbp — verify estimated and projected CPU-minutes sit safely `< 240` CPU-min and within 20% of the scratch sample!), and `PL3` (`745,871` contigs). Update `stage.py`, `stage_est.py` (`before` passes `contigs=n_stored`; `rates` passes `contigs=bs.stage_contigs(root, st)`), and `RUNBOOK.md`. Do NOT edit `stages/G1.conf` or `/tmp/if2453/stages/g1/` (lifting `G1`'s guard stop waits for the PI).

### B-59 A gene-calling rate for giant-virus genomes
**L** · Depends: B-48 · found 2026-11-20 by card C-48c part 3 (`planning/measurements/2026-11-20_virus_gv1_inputs.md`)
Files: `src/biobox/l1/genecall.py` (the per-kind cost model only), `tests/r1/test_b48_genecall_estimate.py` or a new `tests/r1/test_b59_giant_virus_rate.py`, `RUNBOOK.md` rates
Evidence (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-20, card C-48c part 3): on 695 sampled GVMAGs V2 genomes (174.3 Mbp) gv-meta gene calling took 2.7239 s per Mbp, 35.2% under the code's gv-meta rate of 4.2019 s per Mbp, which was fitted on phage-sized genomes; the code's estimate of stage `GV1`'s gene-calling step (354 CPU-min) is then 48% over what the sample predicts (236 at 2.80 s per Mbp with the step overhead). A driver's rate guard judges a step against the code's estimate (DEC-43, DEC-53: more than 20% under is recorded, not stopped, so `GV1` would not stop on it), and batch plans built on a rate 48% high cut more batches than needed.
Do, test first: the gene-call cost of a `virus` genome depends on its size class as well as its kind: a measured rate for giant-virus-sized genomes (state the length threshold from the data: the sample's genomes against `V1`'s and `S1`'s), leaving every estimate of the built stages (`S1`, `H1`, `V1` when built) within 20% of its run record and bringing `GV1`'s sample within 15%. No built stage's plan or published file changes (the plan hash of a stage already built is not recomputed).
Accept: the test fails first on the `GV1` sample (over 20%) and passes; the estimates of the built virus stages against their run records stay within 20%.
**Withdrawn 2026-11-20: the card's premise is wrong, and nothing needs changing before `GV1` is built** (a Sonnet agent read the root and the inputs read-only and stopped before editing, AGENTS §9; *measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-20, unless marked). (1) No length threshold separates giant viruses from the built virus stages: the largest genome of `S1` and `H1` is 735,411 bp and of `V1` 553,716 bp, while `GV1`'s median genome is 200,398 bp, and only 336 of its genomes (8.1% of its bases) are above 735,411 bp. A threshold there leaves the estimate at about 345 CPU-min (*derived*) against 236 from the sample; a lower one moves the plans of built stages. (2) The rate does not follow genome size: gv-meta ran at 4.20 s per Mbp on `S1` (median genome 45 kb), 3.78 on `H1` (249 kb), 3.72 on `V1` (25 kb) and 2.72 on the `GV1` sample (200 kb): `H1` and `GV1` have like sizes and rates 28% apart. What makes `GV1` cheaper is not known (contig mix, gene density); `GV1`'s genomes are multi-contig, median 11 contigs of median 9,215 bp. (3) `CALL_S_PER_MBP` is written into every stage's published gene-call plan (`rates.call_s_per_mbp`, hashed), so a new entry there would change the recorded plan of every later stage's siblings; a rate for one source would have to be a separate constant or a new genome kind, which is a design choice. **What stands instead**: `GV1` is built with the code's estimate (354 CPU-min, 2 batches). An estimate that is too high never stops a build (DEC-53) and only cuts one batch more than needed; the driver records the step against 236 CPU-min as the 2026-11-20 note says. `GV1`'s own run record will then give the rate on all 18,499 genomes, and a card to model it is written only if a later giant-virus source makes it matter.

### W-38 Protein & genomic language model search: `3Di` structural alphabet, on-demand `pLM` rounds, and ephemeral tape-context operon scoring
**Future / Deferred Umbrella** (`W-38a` to `W-38e` below; recorded 2026-11-19 at the PI's word — **do not start until on-disk corpus ingestion progresses and the PI approves GPU venue / `DEC-PLM-1..4`**) · Design & primary-source audit: `planning/design/pLM_remote_system_search.md` (§1–§5) and `planning/search/SEARCH_PLAN.md` (`SD-21`)
Why (`planning/design/pLM_remote_system_search.md`): primary-source dissection of Laub's `DefensePredictor` (DeWeirdt, Mahoney & Laub 2026 *Science* 392:eadv7924, `PMC13092281`; frozen `ESM2-150M` `640`d over `±2` CDSs + `119` nt/operon features $\to$ 5-fold LightGBM, `42/94 = 45%` wet-lab *E. coli* yield vs `1/24 = 4.2%` HHblits low-score controls, *published*), Mordret & Bernheim's `ESM_DF + GeneCLR_DF` (*Science* 2026 `10.1126/science.adv8275`; `12`L `480`d Double-Stranded Attention over `ESM2-35M` + `asinh(0.03*d)` distances), `gLM`/`gLM2`, `ProstT5`/`ESM-2 3B 3Di` + `Foldseek`, `TM-Vec`/`PLMSearch`, `pLM-BLAST`/`EBA`, and `BFVD`, audited by Carbon (§5), established five design constraints:
1. **Reject corpus-wide pooled dense vector ANN (`Option B`) by default:** whole-ORF mean pooling + `1,022`-aa truncation dilutes multi-domain fusions (`NACHT+effector`, `TniQ+nuclease`) while costing `2.3–3.9 TB` raw `fp16` at `14.6 Tbp` (*derived*), whereas per-residue **`3Di` strings (`Option A`, `~120 B/ORF` zstd)** preserve local Smith–Waterman alignment on fusions and `1.5–5 kb` fragments at **`1.11 GB`** (`r26.11.1`, `9.29M` `NR50`), **`12.0 GB`** (`0.92 Tbp`, `~100M` `NR50`, `65 GB` Foldseek RAM), and **`144 GB`** (`14.6 Tbp`, `~1.2B` `NR50`, `5` shards of `156 GB` RAM inside `wilkcomp02`'s `200 GB` cap).
2. **Stock `DefensePredictor` breaks on short metagenomic contigs and circular replicons (`core.py` audit, §5):** (a) its CV split held out `qcov > 0.8` & `fident > 0.3` (`05_04_02_split_data.ipynb:392-404`), not `<20%` ID / `hmmsearch E > 10`; (b) on 1-CDS contigs, `std()` (`ddof=1`) is `NaN` and `fillna(1.1)` overwrites all `105` nucleotide features with `+1.1 SD`; (c) `get_neighbor_df` lacks circular wraparound (`PL1`/`PL2`/`V1`).
3. **Genuine pLM marginal gains over `pyhmmer` + `Foldseek-3Di` + `nbr`:** (a) separating defensive effectors from housekeeping paralogs inside promiscuous superfamilies (PD-(D/E)XK, P-loop NTPases, Rossmann MTases) even outside defense islands (`AP 0.62` vs `0.42` HHblits); (b) surfacing recurrent Pfam/Foldseek-dark multi-gene defense operons (`32/73` validated in Laub); (c) candidate-scale `pLM-BLAST`/`EBA` local residue alignment (`663 KB/ORF` — viable **only** in `round=<id>` batches of `10^3–10^5`, never corpus-wide, and never bulk-embedding `r26.11.1`'s `123.44M` raw `dark_orf`s).
4. **Ephemeral GPU streaming for tape-context scoring (Refined `Option C`, §5):** instead of storing `230 GB`–`2.88 TB` of `NR90` `480`d vectors, stream exact `protein_uid` `ESM2-35M` embeddings ephemerally in GPU VRAM per contig batch and persist **only scalar per-gene scores (`~12 B/gene` zstd = `0.68 GB` at `r26.11.1`, `10.9 GB` at `0.92 Tbp`, `~150 GB` at `14.6 Tbp`; `17×` smaller, `0%` `NR90` substitution error)**.
5. **Hardware & SU envelope:** `wilkcomp03` has **`4× NVIDIA RTX 6000 Ada Generation (`48 GB` VRAM each = `192 GB` total, *measured* `2026-11-19`)**; TACC Lonestar6 `gpu-a100` (`3× A100-40GB`) bills **`3.0` SUs/node-h = `1.0` SU/A100-h** (*published*), so `3Di` across the entire `0.92 Tbp` on-disk harvest (`~100M` `NR50`) costs **`361` SUs (`0.90%` of `C-37a`'s `40,000` requested SUs)** or `~210` Ada-h on `wilkcomp03`.

Proposed ordered sub-cards (one session each when unheld):
