### W-39 One-pass multi-HMM panel search, per-ORF hit table, and co-occurring system caller (`bio-box search panel`)
**M** · Pod: yes (pytest + read-only parity check on `latest` with `anchors.hmm` + unified prokaryotic RT super-panel test case on a DB subset; zero writes to published releases or `/stor` except `refdb` staging if applicable) · Depends: W-28, W-29, W-35, W-37, C-38f
Files: `refdb.yaml` (PI-approved 2026-11-23: add version/commit/DOI-pinned prokaryotic RT HMM entries listed in step 7 below, recording exact URL, license, `redistributable` flag, and `sha256` on download per AGENTS §2 & §8), `src/biobox/search/panel.py` (new), `src/biobox/search/engine.py` and `src/biobox/search/worker.py` (only if small shared helpers for multi-HMM `pyhmmer.hmmsearch` or control checks avoid duplication without changing single-model `search hmm` outputs), `src/biobox/search/export.py` and `src/biobox/search/flanks.py` (only if `loci/*.tsv` or `nbr flanks` needs a small hook to accept a panel run's locus directory, keeping existing `search hmm` and `nbr` outputs byte-identical), `src/biobox/cli.py` (`bio-box search panel` and repeatable `--panel NAME=DIR` on `bio-box sql`), `GUIDE.md`, `AGENT_GUIDE.md`, `RUNBOOK.md`, `tests/r1/test_w39_panel_unit.py` and `tests/r2/test_w39_panel_fixture.py` (new), `tests/r0/test_runbook.py` (only if command parsing needs an update), `planning/measurements/<date>_w39_search_panel.md`. **Do NOT edit `src/biobox/registry/schemas.py` or schema locks** (`catalog.build_manifest` hashes every `TableSpec` in `schemas.TABLES` into `manifest["tables"]`, shifting `FIXTURE_HASH` across cross-host tests, and `search_hit.primary_key` omits `model_key`); keep explicit `pa.schema(...)` definitions in `src/biobox/search/panel.py` as `flanks.py` and `results.py` do for outputs outside the data root.
Why: Today `bio-box search hmm` (`src/biobox/search/recipe.py:528-534`) refuses an `.hmm` file holding more than one profile (`expected 1 HMM in ..., found N`). Screening a $K$-subunit or $K$-subtype panel ($K = 4$–$200+$ profiles for `TniQ + Cas6f/7f/8f + TnsA/B/C`, prokaryotic reverse transcriptases across retrons/`UG`/`DRT`/`DGR`/`GII`/`CRISPR-RT`, defense systems, secretion systems, or phage satellites) therefore runs $K$ separate `search hmm` jobs, decompressing the entire protein pack $K$ times (`36.01 GB` on `r26.11.2`, *measured*; `~45+ GB` on `100M+`-protein releases, *estimate*), staging full shard copies under `<work>/<run_id>/pack/` (`runner._stage`), emitting $K$ separate `protein_uid`-keyed hit files rather than a unified per-ORF DuckDB-queryable table, and leaving multi-HMM subtype competition and co-occurrence / operon assembly to bespoke shell and SQL scripts (`examples/crispr_cas_neighbourhoods/run.sh`). Passing `[hmm_1, ..., hmm_K]` to `pyhmmer.hmmsearch` in one streaming pass keeps two `BLOCK_RESIDUES` (`<= 10,000,000` aa, `< 2 GB` RSS) blocks hot in cache across the panel while reading each `.fastz` shard once.
Do, test first (`tests/r1/test_w39_panel_unit.py` and `tests/r2/test_w39_panel_fixture.py` seen failing before code edits; AGENTS §3.2–3.3, §4.1, §4.3–4.4, §5.1–5.2, §7.2, §8):
(1) **Panel loading, `model_key`, outgroup decoys (`--decoy-hmm` / `--decoy-library`), heterogeneous per-model cutoffs (`SD-04`), and optional DB subsetting (`--stages`)**: accept repeatable `--hmm [NAMESPACE:]FILE` and/or `--library [NAMESPACE@]LIB[:ACC1,ACC2,...]` for target profiles, plus optional `--decoy-hmm [NAMESPACE:]FILE` and/or `--decoy-library [NAMESPACE@]LIB[:ACC1,ACC2,...]` (`is_decoy = True`, e.g. viral `RdRP`, inline `*_REV`/`*_PERM` composition decoys, or housekeeping paralog outgroup HMMs that compete by bitscore on overlapping envelopes to veto cross-hits!). If a pinned pack publishes curated seed MSAs without prebuilt `.hmm` files, build deterministic HMMER profiles (`hmmbuild --seed 42`) at staging time and record the built `.hmm` `sha256`. Support optional `--stages S1,PL1,...` to restrict the search/expansion to proteins/ORFs present in a chosen stage subset of `--release` (recording the stage filter in `run.parquet`). Assign deterministic `model_key` (`[NAMESPACE:]ACC` when `ACC` is present and unique, else `[NAMESPACE:]NAME`; refuse duplicate keys with exit `2`) and record `hmm_sha256`, `name`, `acc`, `model_len`, and `is_decoy`. Because heterogeneous panels mix Pfam `GA`, NCBIfam `TC`, low-bitscore viral/exploratory profiles (`30–50` bits on `toro_types.hmm` / `RVT-All.hmm`, which lack `GA`/`TC` headers!), and repeat-prone accessory profiles (`NTERM`/`CTERM`/`DISTORF`, where `6–16` weak `4–14`-bit hits on bacterial tandem-repeat proteins stack to `35–46` sequence bits), support `--cutoff gathering|trusted|noise` (with optional `--fallback-bits B`), `--bits B`, and `--cutoffs-tsv FILE` (`model_key<TAB>seq_bits[<TAB>dom_bits[<TAB>i_evalue_max[<TAB>hmm_cov_min[<TAB>min_dom_frac]]]]]`, where `min_dom_frac = max_dom_bits / seq_bits` rejects multi-repeat bitscore stacking!). Evaluate all E-values at fixed `Z_PROT = DOM_Z = 100_000_000` (`S-03` Z-invariance, invariant to `--stages` subsetting).
(2) **Controls (`S-04`, including negative-clade `REF24`-style controls) and `results` compatibility (`W-28`, `W-29`)**: require at least one `--positive [MODEL_KEY=]SPEC` on non-decoy target models (repeatable, `ACC:START-END` or `protein_uid` hex; when `MODEL_KEY=` is given that model must recover the control above cutoff, else at least one target panel model must recover it, setting `status = "failed"` and exiting non-zero if missed), optional `--negative-control [MODEL_KEY=]SPEC` (known non-target paralogs such as non-retron RTs in `REF24` whose scores per model are recorded in `controls.tsv` for cutoff calibration), and deterministic per-model shuffled-consensus negative controls (`--negative shuffled_seed|none`, default `shuffled_seed`). Write `<runs>/run=<run_id>/run.parquet` (`search_run` schema, `recipe = "panel.v1"`, `limits_json["command"]` recording the user's CLI argv) plus `panel_hits.parquet`, and write a `RUN` marker (`W-29`) into `--out DIR` so `bio-box results archive|flush|list|get` works unchanged.
(3) **Bounded `< 200` CPU-min `(shard_slice, model_batch)` sub-steps and zero full-pack `/tmp` duplication (`AGENTS.md` §4.1, §7.2)**: `1` HMM over `10.62M` proteins took `~9.5` CPU-min on `rN.3` (`GUIDE.md` §6, *measured*), scaling to `~92` CPU-min per HMM at `102.34M` proteins (`r26.11.3`, *derived*), so a 10–200 HMM panel in one worker would exceed the `300` CPU-min per-process limit (`RLIMIT_CPU`). Plan deterministic `(shard_slice, model_batch)` tasks from `pack_manifest.json` per-shard `n_residues` (or stage-filtered protein FASTA stream when `--stages` selects a small subset of the release) and `sum(model_len)` using the codebase's measured residue×model-length rate (`domains.py`), keeping each sub-process estimated at `<= 200` CPU-min (`< SHARD_MARGIN * cpu_min_per_process`). Run up to `--jobs J` child scopes (`--cpus C` each, enforcing `J * C <=` profile thread allowance, `MemoryMax`, `RLIMIT_CPU`, `nice 19`, honouring `<root>/STOP` between sub-steps). When `pack_dir` is already on local `/tmp` (same filesystem as `work`), read `.fastz` shards directly while verifying `sha256` against `pack_manifest.json` instead of copying `35–150 GB` into `<work>`; when `pack_dir` is on NFS, stage only the active sub-step's shard(s) (`< 5 GB`) at a time. Write atomic per-task Parquet partials under `<runs>/run=<run_id>/shards/` so reruns skip completed tasks and merge deterministically (`AGENTS.md` §4.3).
(4) **Two-level hit tables (`models`, `protein_hits`, and per-ORF `orf_hits`) with overlap & decoy competition, repeat-stacking diagnostics, and fusion retention**:
  - `models.parquet` + `models.tsv`: per-model metadata (`is_decoy` included), resolved cutoffs (`min_dom_frac` included), `n_hit_proteins`, `n_best_proteins`, `n_hit_orfs`, positive/negative control outcomes.
  - `protein_hits.parquet` (sorted by `protein_uid, model_key, env_start` with min/max stats): one row per distinct `protein_uid` domain envelope passing cutoffs (`is_decoy: bool`, `n_model_domains: uint16`, `dom_seq_frac: float32 = max_dom_bits / seq_bitscore`), with `best_on_overlap: bool` computed per `protein_uid` across **both target and decoy hits** by greedy descending `(bitscore, -i_evalue, model_key, env_start)` — `True` iff overlap with every higher-scoring accepted envelope on that `protein_uid` is `< 50%` of `min(len_a, len_b)` (plus `best_in_namespace: bool` per `NAMESPACE` and `vetoed_by_decoy: bool` when a higher-scoring `is_decoy` envelope overlaps `>= 50%`!). Thus an RNA-virus `RdRP` or inline `*_REV`/`*_PERM` decoy outscoring a weak cross-hit sets `best_on_overlap = False` and `vetoed_by_decoy = True`, competing RT subtype profiles stay auditable without double-counting, and genuine multi-domain fusions (`ARTNTERM+RVT_1+CTERM`, `Cas6-RT-Cas1`, `RVT_N+RVT_1+GIIM+HNH`, `TnsA-TnsB`) retain `best_on_overlap = True` on every non-overlapping domain.
  - `orf_hits.parquet` + `orf_hits.tsv` (sorted by `contig_uid, start, feature_uid, model_key, env_start`, `122,880`-row row groups with min/max stats; excluding `is_decoy` rows unless `--include-decoys` is passed): expand non-decoy `protein_hits` through `feature_protein` and `contig_tapes` via bounded, row-group-pruning joins (`AGENTS.md` §4.1, §4.4 — never loading whole stage tables into Python), attaching `feature_uid` beside `ordinal` (`AGENTS.md` §3.3), `contig_uid`, `contig_accession`, `contig_len`, `is_circular`, `start`, `end`, `strand`, `genetic_code`, `stage`, `genome_kind`, `taxonomy`, `nr90_uid`, `nr50_uid`, `domain_state` (`annotated` / `annotated_empty` / `unscanned`), and `pfam_architecture` — directly queryable per ORF in DuckDB.
(5) **Streaming circular-aware co-occurring system / operon caller (`loci_summary` + `loci/*.tsv`) with flanking `l1_igr` tandem-array, split-ORF, and Cas-less MinCED hooks (`DEC-71`)**: sweep non-decoy `orf_hits` (`best_on_overlap = True` by default; `--all-overlaps` optional) along each hit contig's `contig_tapes` in coordinate/slot order — wrapping cleanly across the origin when `is_circular == True` (`contig_len`-wrapped bp gap and `n_slots`-wrapped gene gap, never splitting one origin-spanning plasmid/viral operon into two clusters) — merging adjacent hit ORFs within `<= --max-gap-genes G` intervening genes (default `5`) **and** `<= --max-gap-bp B` bp (default `10000`). Filter clusters by `--min-models M` (default `2` when panel has `>= 2` target HMMs, `1` when `1` or when `--min-models 1` is passed for single-anchor subtype panels), optional `--distinct-orfs` (when a 2-domain fusion on 1 ORF should count as 1 ORF toward quorum), `--require MODEL_KEY[,...]` (required anchor(s)), and `--forbid MODEL_KEY[,...]` (veto models within cluster/window, e.g. `--forbid Cas3` for minimal Type I-F3). Emit `loci_summary.parquet` + `loci_summary.tsv` keyed by `(contig_uid, anchor_feature_uid)` (only `ids.py` identifiers; `models_present`, `models_ordered` with relative strands e.g. `Cas6f(+)>Cas7f(+)>Cas8f(+)>TniQ(-)`, `fusion_orfs`, `n_panel_orfs`, `n_unmatched_internal_orfs` for dark internal effector candidates, `span_bp`, `censored`, `n_crispr_arrays`, **plus four zero-storage RT/RNAP locus diagnostics**: (i) `max_adjacent_igr_bp` and `adjacent_igr_uids` from `l1_igr` within `±2` slots of the cluster; (ii) `bridged_noncoding_bp` spanning across adjacent unannotated `< 100` aa `pyrodigal` smORFs so spurious short ORFs called *inside* `0.6–3.0 kb` tandem `msr-msd` ncRNA arrays do not mask `ART-RT` IGRs; (iii) `cas_less_crispr: bool` flagging clusters with `n_crispr_arrays > 0` and zero Cas domain hits within `±10` genes, directly surfacing tandem `ART` repeats miscalled as CRISPR arrays by MinCED; and (iv) `split_model_pair: bool` flagging same-strand adjacent ORFs hitting non-overlapping N- and C-terminal halves of the same model family — catching programmed ribosomal frameshifts, intein/intron-interrupted RTs, recoded ORFs, and split `ssRNAP`s!), plus `GUIDE.md`-compatible `loci/*.tsv` (`±--neighbors N` genes + `l1_crispr` arrays), `unannotated_proteins.txt`, and optional `--annotate` triggering `C-38f` on-demand `round_domains` Pfam annotation on `--depth called` metagenomic neighbours.
(6) **Incremental cross-release & new-HMM carry-forward (`--carry-from PREV_RUN`) and DuckDB session mounting (`bio-box sql --panel NAME=DIR`)**:
  - **Coverage record**: every `search panel` output directory writes a compact `coverage.json` recording `release`, covered `stages`, `Z_PROT`, `DOM_Z`, `tool_versions` (`pyhmmer`, `pyarrow`), and the explicit `l0seq/protein_seq/gene_call_version=<v>/stage=<S>/...` file list + `sha256`s from the release manifest (which remain permanently in the data root even after `bio-box release retire` retires an old release's `l2`/`l3`/pack under `DEC-66`).
  - **`--carry-from PREV_RUN` (`MR-12` / `B-47b` invariant, bounded streaming)**: when updating onto a newer release (`r26.11.4`, `r26.11.5`, ...) and/or adding/modifying HMMs in the panel, compare each current model's `(model_key, hmm_sha256, cutoff_spec, is_decoy, Z_PROT, DOM_Z, pyhmmer_version)` against `PREV_RUN/models.parquet` + `coverage.json`:
    1. **Unchanged models on new-release delta**: stream-merge `PREV_RUN`'s covered `protein_seq` UIDs (without holding a Python `set` of all UIDs in RAM, `AGENTS.md` §4.1), **skip scanning** every already-covered `protein_uid` (`0`-hit covered proteins emit `0` rows; hit proteins stream-copy their rows from `PREV_RUN/protein_hits.parquet`), and run `pyhmmer` **only on newly added `protein_uid`s** in stages added since `PREV_RUN`!
    2. **Newly added or cutoff-changed models**: scan all target `protein_uid`s of the current release/stages for only the new/modified models while reusing `PREV_RUN` for all unchanged models (`60×` speedup when adding 3 new clade HMMs to a 180-HMM panel!).
    3. **Exact recomputation**: merge carried + newly scanned `protein_hits`, recompute `best_on_overlap`, `best_in_namespace`, and `vetoed_by_decoy` across the full current panel, and rebuild `orf_hits.parquet`, `loci_summary.parquet`, and `loci/*.tsv` across all target stages/contigs of the current release (capturing new contigs that carry previously measured `protein_uid`s). Output files must be **sha256 byte-identical** to a full from-scratch `search panel` run on the same release and panel!
  - **`bio-box sql --panel NAME=DIR` (repeatable)**: validate `NAME` (`^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$`, refusing collision with `schemas.TABLES`), check `DIR/coverage.json` (printing a 1-line stderr note if `DIR`'s release differs from `--release`), and register read-only DuckDB views `<NAME>_models`, `<NAME>_protein_hits`, `<NAME>_orf_hits`, and `<NAME>_loci` over the explicit Parquet paths (`AGENTS.md` §3.9) inside the `catalog.duckdb` session so saved panels are queried directly alongside all release tables with zero re-searching.

       - **General, auxiliary & ART-partner models**: `Pfam` **`PF00078` (`RVT_1`)**, **`PF13456` (`RVT_3`)**, **`PF07727` (`RVT_2`)**, **`PF06817` (`RVT_thumb`)**, **`PF06815` (`RVT_connect`)**, **`PF13655` (`RVT_N`)**, **`PF17917` (`RT_RNaseH`)**, **`PF17919` (`RT_RNaseH_2`)**, **`PF12009` (`Telomerase_RBD`)**, ART partner families **`PF29562` (`DUF8567`)**, **`PF01471` (`PG_binding_1`)**, **`PF29427` (`DUF8549`)**, and accessory domains `PF08388` (`GIIM`), `PF01867` (`Cas1`), `PF01881` (`CRISPR_Cas6`); `NCBIfam` **`TIGR04416` (`group_II_RT_mat`)**, **`NF038233`** (retron-type RT), plus all `NCBIfam 20.0` `reverse transcriptase` / `RNA-directed DNA polymerase` models;
       - **Outgroup `decoy:rdrp` models (`--decoy-library`)**: *Orthornavirae* RNA-virus RdRp members of Pfam clan `CL0027` (**`PF00680` `RdRP_1`**, **`PF00978` `RdRP_2`**, **`PF00998` `RdRP_3`**, **`PF02123` `RdRP_4`**, **`PF00972` `Flavi_NS5`**).
    Run the combined `prok-rt` super-panel in one pass on an ART-RT-enriched stage subset of `latest` (`--stages S1,H1,PL1,P1,C5,G1,SPX1`: phages + **unscreened `H1` huge phages** + plasmids + complete prokaryotes + archaea + unbinned soil/aquatic metagenomes, verifying recovery of all `S1` *Staph* jumbo-phage ARTs and `LPJP1`'s triple-RT Group II + DGR + ART architecture!). Verify 1:1 domain-hit concordance against standalone `hmmsearch` on a sampled contig set, evaluate new `H1`/`SPX1`/`PL1` `ART-RT` candidates (`NTERM`/`CTERM`/`PROXORF`/`DISTORF` or `clade11` + `max_adjacent_igr_bp >= 500` or miscalled Cas-less `l1_crispr`), run the **decoy-filtered novel/unclassified RT discovery query**, and record counts, wall/CPU time, and peak RSS in `planning/measurements/<date>_w39_search_panel.md`.

### W-40 Unified Single-Subunit & Single-Polypeptide Viral RNAP Super-Panel (`ssrnap` on `bio-box search panel`) *(later rolling releases — `ssRNAP` track)*
**M** · Pod: yes (read-only benchmark on `latest` + `refdb.yaml` additions; zero writes to published releases) · Depends: W-39, C-59a · DEC-69
Files: `refdb.yaml` (archived PHROGs/VOGdb RNAP subset + custom seed HMMs for N4 split `RNAP2` `gp15`/`gp16`, mitochondrial invertron `rpo`, and divergent `RpoT`/`crAss-vRNAP`/`YonO`), `examples/rnap/` (optional companion panel check beside the locked `PF00940` release check), `planning/measurements/<date>_w40_ssrnap_panel.md`
Why: `examples/rnap` searches a single Pfam model (`PF00940.25` `RNA_pol` at `30` bits), which empirically **misses N4's `3,500` aa virion `vRNAP` + `252` *Schitoviridae* relatives (`< 10` bits, *measured*, `2026-11-09_ssrnap_workflow`)**, misses naturally split heterodimeric *Schitoviridae* **`RNAP2` (`gp15` + `gp16`, no standalone Pfam family)**, drops `11` GA-passing `PF00940` hits on `rN.3` (`examples/rnap/README.md`), misses twilight-zone (`< 20–30%` id) fungal/plant **mitochondrial linear-plasmid ("invertron") `rpo`s**, leaves **`145` split-RNAP contigs (`418` partial hits `< 500` aa)** unpaired, and is blind to the evolutionarily unrelated **Fold B single-polypeptide fused-`DPBB` RNAPs** (*Crassvirales* `phi14:2` `6VR4` ~2,180 aa `vRNAP`, *Gubaphages*, *Lak* megaphages, *Bacillus* `SPβ` `YonO`, and yeast cytoplasmic killer plasmid `pGKL2` `ORF6`+`ORF7`).
Do, test first: build and validate a two-namespace `W-39` panel keeping the two catalytic folds strictly distinct:
  1. **`palm:` namespace (Fold A — right-handed `T7 / Pol I` RRM superfamily)**: Pfam 38.2 `PF00940` (`RNA_pol` at GA `22.0`), `PF14700` (`RPOL_N`), `PF29426` (`Phage_RNApol`), **Pfam 38.2's N4 `vRNAP` quartet** (`PF21769` `vRNAP_dom`, `PF21867` `vRNAP_dom_2`, `PF21894` `N4_RNAP_helical`, `PF21624` `vRNAP_plug`), NCBIfam 20.0 phage/organellar `ssRNAP` models, VOGdb + archived PHROGs `ssRNAP` subset, and deterministic `hmmbuild --seed 42` seed profiles for **N4 heterodimeric `RNAP2` (`gp15` + `gp16`)**, **mitochondrial linear-plasmid invertron `rpo`s** (`kalilo`, `pAL2-1`, `pClK1`, `S-2`), psychrophilic/low-dsRNA *Autographiviridae* (`VSW-3`, `KP34`, `EM1`), and basal protist `RpoT`s (euglenozoan/dinoflagellate/apicomplexan), guarded by `--decoy-library pfam:PF00476` (`DNA_pol_A`, Pol I-fold outgroup).
  2. **`dpbb_single:` companion namespace (Fold B — single-polypeptide / split viral & plasmid `DPBB` RNAPs)**: Pfam `PF05183` (`RdRP` / `QDE-1`-like) + VOGdb/seed HMMs for *Crassvirales*/*Lak*/*Gubaphage* single-chain `vRNAP`s (`6VR4`), `YonO`, and yeast killer-plasmid `pGKL2` `ORF6`+`ORF7`, guarded by cellular `RNA_pol_Rpb1_*`/`Rpb2_*` outgroup decoys.
  3. Use `W-39`'s co-occurrence (`--max-gap-genes 2`) and `split_model_pair` diagnostic to automatically pair N4 `gp15`+`gp16` and split/recoded `ssRNAP` ORFs, verify 100% recovery of T7 `gp1`, `phiKMV`, `VSW-3`, `Syn5`, N4 `vRNAP` (`gp50`) + `RNAP2` (`gp15`+`gp16`), `phi14:2` `vRNAP`, `YonO`, and `kalilo`/`S-2` (once `LPL1`/`O1` land) with `0` `DNA_pol_A` or cellular `rpoB`/`rpoC` false positives, and shortlist twilight-zone (`700–1,300` aa and `2,000–4,000` aa) viral/plasmid dark ORFs for `W-38d` `Foldseek`/`pLM-BLAST` structural screening.
Accept: `ssrnap` panel config and seed profiles pinned in `refdb.yaml` with `sha256`s; 1:1 `hmmsearch` oracle concordance on sampled stages; N4 `vRNAP` (`253+` phages missed in `2026-11-09_ssrnap_workflow`), N4 `gp15`+`gp16`, and all `S1` canonical controls recovered with `0` `DNA_pol_A` false positives; measurement note committed.
Done 2026-11-24 (STATUS row W-40; `planning/measurements/2026-11-24_w40_ssrnap_panel.md`): two-namespace `46`-model `ssrnap` super-panel (`25` `palm:` + `5` `dpbb_single:` + `1` `decoy_pol1:` + `15` `decoy_cell_rnap:`) pinned in `refdb.yaml` (`phrogs_rnap`, `vogdb_rnap`, `ssrnap_custom_seeds`, mirrored `4/4` to `tower`) with `examples/rnap/ssrnap_cutoffs.tsv` + `ssrnap_panel.sh` and benchmarked on `r26.11.2 --stages S1,H1,PL1,P1,C5,V1` (`9,090` clean target proteins, `10,279` loci, `363` `split_model_pair`, `1,872` twilight dark ORFs shortlisted, `10/10` `S1` canonical controls incl. `400` N4 `vRNAP` proteins + `267` paired `gp15+gp16` loci, `112` staged `LPL1` invertron/`pGKL2` contigs, `0` `DNA_pol_A` or cellular `rpoB`/`rpoC` false positives, `155/155` exact `hmmsearch` oracle match; `224 passed`).

### W-35 `pack build` under the CPU budget: the pack of a 50 Gbp release
**M** · Pod: yes · Depends: W-16, W-19, W-22 · blocks the worked examples on `rM.2` (AGENTS §5.7) and so its announcement
Files: `src/biobox/search/pack.py`, `src/biobox/cli.py` (the `pack` subcommands only), `scripts/pod_run.sh` only if a part needs a unit of its own, tests in `tests/r1` and `tests/r2`, `RUNBOOK.md` (§7 and the limits row), `GUIDE.md` where it shows `pack build`, one measurement note
Why: `pack build` is one process. On `rN.1` (12.28 Gbp, 10.6 M proteins) it took 86.0 CPU-minutes, of which the dark-ORF part about 71 of 88 minutes of wall time (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-11, `planning/measurements/2026-11-11_pack_build_rN1.md`). `rM.2` holds seven stages, about 50 Gbp: about 350 to 430 CPU-minutes in one process (*derived*: 86.0 × 50.4 / 12.28 and 86.0 × 5), above the 300 CPU-minutes of AGENTS §7.2, with the dark-ORF part alone near or above it. So the pack of `rM.2` was not built when the release was (2026-11-14), and no search or worked example can run on it.
Do, test first: (1) an estimate before any work, from the release's own counts (genome bases, proteins) at rates measured on `rN.1` and `rN.3`, printed, and a refusal (exit 2) when a single process would pass `SHARD_MARGIN` of the profile's `cpu_min_per_process` and no parts were asked for. (2) The build in parts, each its own process under the budget: at least the three targets separately, and the dark-ORF target (and any other whose estimate needs it) cut by contig ranges into parts whose sorted runs are merged by a last, cheap process; say how the cut is chosen and that it does not change the output. (3) **The pack is byte-identical to the one-process build**: the same pack id for the same inputs, on the fixture (test) and on `rN.3` on the pod (its pack id is `a334c89ccf048904f297de3b2597af22`, built 2026-11-11; build it again in parts into a scratch pack directory and compare every file's sha256 with the manifest of the existing one, which is not touched). (4) Resumable: a part with its output present is not rerun; a killed part leaves nothing a reader takes; `STOP` in the root is honoured between parts. (5) Bounded memory as W-16 (the memory test still passes; state each part's peak). (6) Every part through `run-step`'s rules where they apply (a memory cap, `nice 19`, the thread allowance); parts at once limited by `--jobs` and the allowance.
Accept: the tests; `rN.3`'s pack reproduced byte for byte in parts; then the pack of `rM.2` built on the pod into the profile's pack directory (`/tmp/if2453/bio-box/packs`), read-only on the data root `/tmp/if2453/r10/root`, with the runtime image of `faec1f4` and the tree's `src`, each part's CPU-minutes (none over 300), wall time, peak memory and the pack's size in the report, and `pack verify` clean. Models: Pfam 38.2 as for `rN.3` (`--model pfam-38.2=<the file the build's driver names>`). If a measured rate misses the estimate by more than 20%, stop and report (AGENTS §6).
Done 2026-11-14 (STATUS row W-35; `planning/measurements/2026-11-14_pack_build_in_parts.md`): the pack of `rM.2` is `26e10f05401faeefb78769e83f7f6cb4`, built in 8 parts and verified.

Why: the release check of AGENTS §5.7 stopped the announcement of `rM.2` at its first step (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-14 22:13 to 22:15, unit `ex-rM2`, `/tmp/if2453/ex-rM2/out/summary.tsv`: 37 invariants missing, 19 and 18). ### C-34 Label files for stages ingested before card B-27
**M** · Pod: yes · Depends: W-36, B-27 · before the next release over stages `S1`, `P1`, `P2` (card C-33 part 2)

A release built over them later should be fully labelled.
Do, test first: (1) `stage labels --stage S` writes `l0seq/contig_label/stage=<S>/` as new files from the stage's own `contig_occurrence`, through `biobox.io.write_table`, with the code ingest uses, bounded in memory, deterministic, with a ledger row and a run record; it refuses when the stage already has label files. Oracle: a re-ingest of the same inputs into scratch gives label files with the same sha256 (fixture; and on the pod for `S1`, whose inputs are archived). (2) A rule, stated and tested, for what a release that was published before the new files existed says about them: today a file of a table the release lists for other stages would be reported "not in manifest" by `release verify` of `rN.1`, `rN.2`, `rN.3` and `rM.2`; it must instead be ignored as a stage file added after the release's manifest (as card C-19's rule ignores a producer or library the release does not list), without hiding a file that was deleted or changed. Say exactly what distinguishes the two cases and test both. (3) Then, on the pod, the label files for `S1`, `P1` and `P2` in the real root, with the before-and-after check that no existing file changed, `release verify` of every release still passing, and the list of new files for the durable copy.
Accept: the tests; the oracle on the fixture and on `S1`; every existing release verifies before and after; a release defined afterwards over the seven stages shows `contig_label yes` in `bio-box releases` (on a scratch view; the real release is card C-33 part 2).
Done 2026-11-15 (STATUS row C-34; `planning/measurements/2026-11-15_label_files_old_stages.md`): S1, P1 and P2 have label files; all six published releases verify before and after. The next release over them is card C-35.

Now **M** · Pod: yes · Depends: an image built from `main` that holds MinCED, pulled to the pod and made the default as card C-29 did.
Files: `src/biobox/l1/crispr.py` (new), `src/biobox/registry/schemas.py` and the schema locks by `scripts/update_schema_lock.py` (a new `l1` table; no existing column changes), `src/biobox/stage.py` and `src/biobox/cli.py` (the step in `build-stage`, `bio-box stage crispr`), `src/biobox/doctor.py` if it lists the native tools, tests in `tests/r1` and `tests/r2`, `RUNBOOK.md` (the step and its limits row), a measurement note; the CRISPR example's checks in a second commit (`examples/crispr_cas_neighbourhoods/expected.tsv`, `sql/`, a new `recorded/` file only for a release that has arrays).
Do, test first: a producer in the form of the ncRNA producer: features of kind `crispr_array` keyed by `feature_uid`, one row per array in the `feature` table and one row per repeat and per spacer in a new `l1` table with 0-based half-open coordinates (an array across a circular origin found once, with end > contig length), written through `biobox.io.write_table` one genome at a time, deterministic at any thread count, with ledger rows naming MinCED's version; in `build-stage` after gene calling, sharded under the CPU budget if a stage's estimate needs it (measure MinCED's rate on one stage first and say whether it does); a contig another stage owns is skipped as DEC-26 and DEC-27 say. Oracle: MinCED's own output for the same contig, parsed separately in the test, and a constructed contig with a planted array (repeats and spacers known by construction; also one across the origin). State MinCED's parameters (minimum repeats, repeat and spacer length ranges) and why. Orientation (CRISPRDirection in `PLAN.md` step 4) and the spacer corpus of the search pack (spec invariant 16) are later cards: say what they would need.
Accept: the tests; the fixture's other files byte-identical (the manifest hash changes with the new schema: say so); MinCED's arrays on one real stage of the pod's data root in scratch, read-only on the root, with counts and cost; no array checks added to the example until a release holds arrays.
Done 2026-11-13 (STATUS row E-12; `planning/measurements/2026-11-13_crispr_arrays.md`). Follow-ups: cards E-13 and E-14; orientation (a pinned tool or rule, a nullable `orientation` column) and the spacer corpus of the search pack (spec invariant 16) are later cards.

### E-13 CRISPR arrays of two repeats: measure before changing the minimum
**M** (measure, then propose; the setting is the PI's) · Pod: yes · Depends: E-12 · found by E-12 (2026-11-13)
Files: a script under `scripts/stress/`, one measurement note with small tables; `src/biobox/l1/crispr.py` only to let the minimum be chosen where the producer name already encodes it (no default changes)
Why: MinCED's default of at least 3 repeats misses the minimal arrays the CRISPR worked example is about: the first Tn7-like element of the control genome (P. damselae chromosome 2) has a 2-repeat, 1-spacer array 128 bases from Cas6f, found only at a minimum of 2 (*measured*, card E-12). Two-repeat calls also arise by chance and inside repeated genes, and their rate here is not known.
Do: on the built stages, read-only on the root, into scratch: arrays at a minimum of 2 against 3; for the extra calls, how many lie within 10 genes of a Cas domain (the release's Pfam scan), how many inside a called gene, how many in genomes with no Cas domain at all; the same on shuffled contigs of the same composition (a null written in the script); and how many of the worked example's Tn7-like loci gain an array. Propose a rule (for example 2 repeats only beside a Cas gene, as a second producer) with its measured false-positive rate.
Accept: the note; no default changed.
Done 2026-11-14 (STATUS row E-13). Result: a minimum of 2 everywhere adds 2,960 calls per Gbp, nearly all false; kept only within 1 kb of a Cas-domain gene, outside any called gene and not overlapping an array found at 3, they are 7.35 per Gbp (at most 2.96 by chance, *derived*) and include every Tn7-like locus of the worked example that gains an array. Proposed: keep 3 everywhere and add that filtered second producer after gene calls and the Pfam scan (a card to be written when the PI agrees).
The PI agreed on 2026-11-14 (DEC-40): card E-15.

### E-15 A second CRISPR producer: two-repeat arrays beside Cas genes (DEC-40)
**M** · Pod: yes · Depends: E-12, E-13, E-14 · DEC-40 (PI, 2026-11-14)

Accept: the tests; the default producer's bytes unchanged; on a scratch view of `rN.3` with both producers, the worked example's control Tn7-like element shows its array within the window, and 14 of the 20 Tn7-like TniQ loci have an array (7 at three repeats, 7 more at two: E-13's count).
Done 2026-11-14 (STATUS row E-15; `planning/measurements/2026-11-14_crispr_two_repeat_producer.md`). Open: the phage stage keeps three times as many calls per Gbp as P1 and P2, with no measured chance bound: card E-16.

### E-16 The filtered CRISPR producer on phages: measure the chance rate on S1
**M** · Pod: yes · Depends: E-15
Files: `scripts/stress/e13_crispr_min_repeats.py` only if its null needs an argument for the stage (or a new `scripts/stress/e16_*.py`), one measurement note `planning/measurements/<date>_crispr_near_cas_phages.md` with its tables; no code under `src/`
Why: the filtered producer keeps 34 calls on S1 (phages, 1.66 Gbp), 20.4 per Gbp, against 7.35 per Gbp on P1 and P2 (*measured* and *derived*, card E-15). Card E-13's bound of at most 2.96 per Gbp by chance (calls placed at random in the same genome) was computed for P1 and P2 only. Phages are gene-dense and some carry Cas-like domains for other reasons (Cas4-like nucleases, helicases with Cas3 domains), so the chance rate there may be higher; some phages (ICP1) do carry real CRISPR-Cas systems.
Do: a measurement, no fix. On S1, with E-13's method (the calls at 2 repeats placed at random in the same genome, the same three rules, fixed seeds, enough draws for an interval), the calls per Gbp expected by chance and the enrichment of the 34 kept; the 34 listed with their phage, the Cas family beside them, its role, the gap, the repeat count and whether the phage has two or more distinct Cas families within 10 genes (a system, not a lone domain); which of the 31 families account for the kept calls, and how many of the 34 are beside a lone Cas4 or Cas3-domain gene. Read-only on the data root and on `/tmp/if2453/e15/view`; scratch under `/tmp/if2453/e16/`; at most 3 threads while a build runs.
Accept: the note, with every number labelled, and one of three outcomes stated with its evidence: the rule holds on phages as it is; the rule should exclude named families on virus contigs (then a card, and a new filter tag: a new producer, never a change of this one); or the rate cannot be bounded at this size. No default changes in this card.
Done 2026-11-15 (STATUS row E-16; `planning/measurements/2026-11-15_crispr_near_cas_phages.md`): the rule holds on phages as it is; all 34 kept calls on S1 are the type I-F locus of ICP1-like phages, against 2.43 expected by chance. The same script checks a later virus stage with `--stages`.

**M** · Depends: E-07, E-08, 0-08a (merged) · DEC-28, DEC-30
Files: `src/biobox/l1/rfam_sets/prok-2.tsv` (new), `src/biobox/l1/ncrna.py` (`DEFAULT_SET`, `FROZEN_SETS`, docstrings), `src/biobox/stage.py` and `src/biobox/cli.py` only where they name the default set or its shard rate, `RUNBOOK.md`, tests

### W-26 An accession lookup table, before the first stage of a million contigs
**M** · Depends: W-15 (measured) · not needed for `rM.2`; do it with the first draft-assembly or metagenome stage
Files: `src/biobox/registry/schemas.py` (one lookup table, then the lock by `scripts/update_schema_lock.py`), the builder that writes `contig_label`, `src/biobox/cli.py` (`find`, `search hmm --positive`, `seq --contig`), `GUIDE.md`, tests
Why (card W-15, `planning/measurements/2026-11-12_positive_lookup_rN3.md`): resolving an accession scans every label row of the release. On `rN.3` (39,633 rows, one row group per stage) that is 0.034 to 0.047 s (*measured*); it crosses one second at about 1 M label rows and would be about 110 s at S6's 111.5 M contigs (*derived*, linear, about 1 µs per row at 2 threads). Also: since card B-27 `contig_label` holds only each contig's first record accession, while `contig_occurrence` holds every record's; on `rN.3` a second accession of a contig (for example `MK327939` beside `NC_054918`) resolves only because its stages predate B-27 and the lookup falls back to `contig_occurrence` (*inferred* from the B-27 definition, not run on a B-27 release).
Do, test first: reproduce the second point on a fixture built by the current code (a contig with two record accessions: the second must resolve). Then a per-stage table sorted by accession holding every record's accession and its `contig_uid`, written by the step that writes `contig_label`; `find --accession`, `--positive` and `seq --contig ACCESSION` resolve through it with O(1) row groups per stage (`profile_query`, on the 100× store split into 20 stages); the header-substring form stays the documented slow path; stages built before the card answer as today with one note. Accept a protein accession where the release records one.
Accept: the tests; the pruning check; `GUIDE.md` §9 updated.

### W-25 Group a search's loci by their flanks, with a tree and a synteny figure
**M** · approved by the PI 2026-11-12; merged; its defaults (`--level nr50`, `--group-by anchor-nr90`, `--window 5g`, `--cut 0.5`, `--distance j`) and the rule that an unannotated gene is a token of its own were accepted by the PI on 2026-11-12 · Depends: W-23, C-21b, C-21c, W-24 (merged) · source: Thorn, Aarestrup and Munk, "Flankophile: a bioinformatic pipeline for prokaryotic genomic synteny analysis", Microbiol Spectr 12(1):e02413-23 (2023), PMC10783016, CC BY 4.0
Why: Flankophile finds a reference gene set in assemblies (ABRicate, 99% identity and coverage), keeps hits with at least 1,500 bp of flank on each side, groups hits by their closest reference gene, computes distances between flanks (KMA k-mer Jaccard on nucleotides), builds UPGMA trees, annotates flanks (Prokka) and draws synteny plots with metadata. A search here already gives the anchors (`search hmm`), their gene neighbourhoods (`nbr`, `loci/*.tsv`, tapes) and family and cluster names (Pfam, NR50, NR90), but no way to say which loci share a context. Doing it on tokens instead of nucleotides needs no new tool and reads only the tapes the release has. Method: distance on flank tokens as in Domainator (Jaccard and adjacency index, from BiG-SCAPE) and UPGMA as in Flankophile; review in `planning/design/W-25_flank_tools_review.md` (amended 2026-11-12 from it).
Do (design note first, half a page, then test first): `bio-box nbr flanks RUN --window Ng --level nr50|nr90|pfam [--group-by anchor-nr90|anchor-nr50|none] [--max-loci K] [--cut D] [--export gff]`. Each locus is the anchor's flanks as two ordered token lists (upstream, downstream) oriented to the anchor's strand. Each token is (level id, side, strand relative to the anchor). At `pfam` the id is the protein's Pfam architecture. A gene without an id at the level gets a token unique to it (never shared). Censoring by a linear contig end is stated per locus. Distances, both reported: J = 1 − Jaccard of the two loci's token sets (default), and A = 1 − adjacency index (the Jaccard of adjacent oriented token pairs, BiG-SCAPE). The combined distance 1 − (w·J_sim + (1 − w)·A_sim) is offered with w stated in the note and chosen on the planted test, not copied from BiG-SCAPE. Where a contig end cuts a flank, a pair is compared only over the positions both loci observe on that side, and the number of positions compared is written per pair. Loci are grouped by the anchor's own cluster. Within a group, loci with identical token lists collapse to one leaf with a count before any pairwise step. Per group: the distance matrices (TSV, leaves named by `feature_uid`), a UPGMA tree (Newick, inputs sorted by `feature_uid`), context types from cutting the tree at D (one row per locus: `feature_uid`, group, `context_type`), and a conservation table per context type and neighbour token (share of loci, modal offset along the anchor's strand, share on the same strand, distinct genomes through `genome_member`). Also one figure (SVG and PDF, matplotlib; no R): genes as arrows coloured by family, families below a set share in grey, rows in tree order, columns for context type, genome kind, stage, taxonomy and the anchor's NR90. Optional `--export gff`: one GFF3 per locus with `protein_uid` and the token as attributes, plus a protein-to-group TSV, readable by LoVis4u, clinker and Domainator. Bounded: at most K leaves per group after collapsing (default 2,000; above it, a deterministic sample by `feature_uid`, said in the output). Deterministic output (AGENTS §4.3). Every row names `feature_uid` beside any ordinal (AGENTS §3.3). The method is reimplemented from the papers; no code is copied (BiG-SCAPE is AGPL-3.0; the others state no code licence).
Accept: tests on a constructed release with planted contexts. (i) Two flank types around one anchor family, one shared by phages and prokaryotes, are recovered as two context types. (ii) A pair with the same families in a rearranged order is separated by A and not by J. (iii) One inserted gene changes A by at most the pairs it touches. (iv) A censored locus is compared only over the positions it observes. (v) Unannotated neighbours do not make two loci similar. The oracle for J and A is a brute-force loop written separately, and Domainator `compare_contigs` on the export once the image has it. On `rN.3`, the RNA polymerase run (2,581 loci for 1,957 proteins, `GUIDE.md` §6) is grouped with time and peak memory recorded (Pod: yes, read-only).
Not proposed: running Flankophile itself (its tools, Prokka, ABRicate, KMA, CD-HIT and R, are not in the image; `container/environment.yml` is the PI's), importing its evaluation data (2,006 Danish clinical isolates, PRJEB37711, and 273 pig gut metagenomes: strain-redundant clinical species, and the metagenomes are not public as assemblies), or ResFinder as a nucleotide reference set (a source entry for the PI if wanted).

### B-46 The tokens step runs out of memory at 46 M proteins
**M** · blocks `rM.2` (the 50 Gbp release) · found 2026-11-12 by the 50 Gbp build · Pod: yes (read-only on the root)
Files: `src/biobox/l3/tokens.py`, tests; and, only if the same failure is found there, `src/biobox/l3/postings.py` and `src/biobox/dim.py` (say so in the report)
Evidence (*measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-12, unit `r50-P6-5`, log `/tmp/if2453/r50/tmp/logs/r50-P6-5.log`): release `rM.1` (stages S1, P1 to P6; 45,815,843 proteins; 31,183,876 NR90 and 8,192,065 NR50 clusters) published its clustering at 10:53 CST; `rM.1.tokens` failed 4 minutes later in the first `COPY` of `tokens.run` (the slot join: tapes unnested, left-joined to `fam` and to `cl` twice) with `_duckdb.OutOfMemoryException: failed to allocate data of size 32.0 MiB (1.8 GiB/1.8 GiB used)`. The connection has `threads 1`, `memory_limit DUCK_MEMORY_LIMIT`, `preserve_insertion_order True`. The same code built `rN.3` (10.6 M proteins). Nothing was published. The build now scans Pfam on P3 to P6 and waits for this card before `rM.2` (`scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/r50_driver.sh`, `SKIP_RM1`).
Do: reproduce first, at a size that fails with the current code: on the 100× synthetic store with a lowered memory limit if that reproduces the same operator's failure, and on the pod against `rM.1`'s real inputs, read-only, writing to a scratch `out=` under `/tmp/if2453/b46` (never into `/tmp/if2453/r10/root`; bind it read-only). Find which operator cannot spill (the `arg_max` aggregates of `cl` and `fam` over BLOB keys, the order-preserving `COPY`, or the joins) and change the query so memory stays bounded by DuckDB's limit at any size (AGENTS §4.1): for example materialise `cl` and `fam` as sorted Parquet first, or join in ranges of `protein_uid`. Output byte-identical to today's on the fixture and on `rN.3`'s inputs (compare with `rN.3`'s published `tape_tokens` and `token_dict`, read-only). Then run the release steps after tokens (postings, dim, manifest, catalog) of `rM.1` into the same scratch `out=`, to find the next failure before the build meets it; report each step's peak memory, wall time and CPU-minutes. Pod threads: at most 8, inside the account's total (a Pfam scan of 32 threads is running); `MemoryMax=32G` scopes, nice 19.
Accept: the reproduction fails first; byte-identity on the fixture and `rN.3`; `rM.1`'s tokens step completes into scratch with its peak memory recorded; the later steps' results reported.

### B-45 Run an unsplittable clustering call as one process (DEC-29)
**M** · Depends: B-41, B-43, C-22 (merged) · DEC-29 · before any release above about 74 M proteins
Files: `src/biobox/l2/cluster.py`, `src/biobox/stage.py`, `RUNBOOK.md`, tests
Why: the cut refuses a prefilter call whose per-part table rebuild alone exceeds the part budget (`cluster.py`, the C-22 table: the NR90 step's prefilter from about 74 M proteins, NR50 step 0 from about 108 M, *derived*). DEC-29 runs that call unsplit instead.
Do, test first: when the cut would refuse a call for that reason and the profile sets `cluster_cpu_min_max`, plan the call as one part (its own table, `--add-self-matches` as today, the log check for `Process prefiltering step 1 of 1` unchanged), with its estimate (work plus table) checked against `cluster_cpu_min_max` before launch and refused above it, and run it with `budget_reason` `exempt: DEC-29`; without `cluster_cpu_min_max` keep today's refusal and name DEC-29 in it. Every other call is cut as today. The one-part call's output equals the workflow's (the 1-part equality test of B-41 covers it; add the case to it with a forced overhead over the budget on the fixture and the constructed set). `release cluster plan` shows which calls run as one process and why. Restate the C-22 table's 95 M row with the call as one process, and the total.
Amended 2026-11-12 (the B-45 agent stopped with evidence: the cut runs in its own process under `run-step`, which carries no profile, and the plan's `cut` record holds only `parts` or `part_cpu_min`): Files gain `src/biobox/cli.py`, for one option on `release cluster plan` only (the one-process cap), which `stage.py` passes when the profile sets `cluster_cpu_min_max` and the plan records in `cut`, so the cut stays a function of the plan; a forced overhead for tests is likewise an explicit plan parameter recorded in `cut`. Also: when clustering runs split, the release-level DEC-25 check (`stage.cluster_refusal`, which compares the one-process estimate T(n) with the cap and refuses above about 62.7 M proteins) checks instead the estimate of each call the plan runs as one process against the cap; the unsplit one-process mode keeps today's check until card B-42 (2).
Accept: the tests fail first; equality at 1, 2 and 4 parts unchanged; with the profile key absent the refusal is unchanged except its text.

### B-43 A shared prefilter index for split clustering
**M** · Depends: B-41 · needed above about 68 M proteins
Evidence (*measured*, wilkcomp02, MMseqs2 18.8cc5c, 2026-11-11, B-41 report, `/tmp/if2453/b41/idx.sh`): `prefilter` uses a prebuilt index only when the index itself is given as the target (`db.idx`); it then equals the workflow's output on the sensitive NR50 calls (`-s 2`, `-s 3`, with `--comp-bias-corr 1 --diag-score 1`) at 0.5 to 0.8 CPU-s against 5.2, but aborts with `munmap_chunk(): invalid pointer` on the `-s 1 --comp-bias-corr 0 --diag-score 0` calls (the NR90 step and NR50 step 0). Without a shared index each part rebuilds it: at 95 M proteins that alone is about 333 CPU-minutes per part (*derived*), over the budget, and at 50 M the largest prefilter call costs about 3.7 times its unsplit CPU (*derived*).
Do: use the shared index on the calls where it works, with a test that the result equals the rebuilt one; for the calls where it crashes, find the cause (the parameters the index was built with against those the call asks for; try an index built with the call's own settings) or, if it is a defect of this MMseqs2 version, say which version fixes it and propose the pin change for the PI (`container/environment.yml` is the PI's to change). State the part sizes and total CPU at 50 M and 95 M with whatever works.
Accept: equality tests on the fixture and the constructed set; the derived budget table.

### B-44 Clustering above about 87 M proteins needs an MMseqs2 without the index abort
**H** (PI) · deferred by the PI on 2026-11-11: do nothing now · Depends: B-43
Evidence (*measured*, wilkcomp02, MMseqs2 18.8cc5c, card B-43): `prefilter` aborts (`munmap_chunk(): invalid pointer`, exit 134) whenever its target is a prebuilt index and `--diag-score` is 0, at every index and run setting tried (sixteen variations). Reproducer: `createdb in.faa db; createindex db tmp; prefilter db db.idx pf --diag-score 0`. The fast clustering calls (the NR90 step and NR50 step 0) use that setting, so their parts each rebuild the index; the cut refuses the NR90 step from about 87.2 M proteins and NR50 step 0 from about 108.1 M (*derived*, card B-43). 18-8cc5c (2025-07-27) is the latest release on GitHub and bioconda as of 2026-11-11; no upstream issue describes the abort.
When it is needed (a release over about 87 M proteins): (a) build the current upstream commit in a scratch image and run the reproducer; (b) report the abort upstream with the reproducer (from the PI's account, text approved by the PI); (c) pin a fixed version only if it passes the reproducer, the module-list test, the 1, 2 and 4-part tests with those calls added to `INDEX_SETTINGS`, and gives clusters equal row for row to today's on the fixture and on `rN.1b` (DEC-24: otherwise cluster ids change between releases and that is its own decision). `container/environment.yml` is the PI's to change.

### C-09 The file-size rule does not refuse small release records
**L** · Depends: C-08 (found in its report) · blocks the second release under C-04
Files: `src/biobox/contracts.py` (`check_file_sizes`), `src/biobox/registry/schemas.py` (a `small_ok` flag on `ArtifactSpec` if it lacks one), `tests/r2/test_micro_s0_contracts.py`, `tests/registry/operations.yaml` (drop `catalog` and `release_manifest` from `external` if R0 accepts it)
Why: `check_file_sizes` groups artifacts by (artifact, stage) and refuses a group with more than one file under the profile's floor (64 MiB on wilkcomp02). The release manifest and the catalog have no partitions, so every release's manifest falls in one group: the second release's manifest and catalog would be refused by `run-step` (`_check_sizes`). The same grouping would refuse two small domain-library files in one stage. Derived from the code by the C-08 agent; not yet run at the pod's floor.
Do: reproduce first at the real floor (a test profile with `small_file_floor_bytes: 67108864` and two releases built through `run-step`). Then: artifacts and tables that are small by nature (manifest, catalog, `store_params`, and every `small_ok` table) are exempt by a registry flag, not by name in the check; for the rest, group by the full partition tuple, not by stage alone. Do not lower or bypass the floor for data tables.
Accept: the reproduction fails first and passes after; a deliberately tiny second file in a data table's partition is still refused.

### B-30 Ingest by batch processes
**M** · Depends: B-22, C-08
Files: `src/biobox/l0seq/store.py`, `src/biobox/stage.py`, `src/biobox/registry/schemas.py` (one `ArtifactSpec`, `ingest_plan`, and nothing else), `src/biobox/cli.py` (`stage ingest plan|pass1|batch|merge`), tests
Amended 2026-11-10 (found by the B-30 agent before editing): `run-step` refuses an empty or unclaimed staging, so the plan and pass-1 steps need a registered record to publish. Add one artifact `ingest_plan` at `l0seq/ingest_plan/stage=*`, `in_manifest=False`, `small_ok=True`, as B-24 added `domain_scan`. The commands are `bio-box stage ingest plan|pass1|batch|merge`, matching `stage domains`. Pass 1 stays sharded by source object as written below; one shard is a valid plan for a small stage. Lost scratch is rescanned and checked against the published sha256, as C-12 does for domain scans.
Why: one ingest process runs at about 3.7 Mbp/s (*measured*, wilkcomp02), so a stage over about 60 Gbp exceeds 300 CPU-minutes (AGENTS §7.2). `ingest` decides new sequences and `contig_index` for the whole stage in one call, so it cannot be split today.
Do: split a stage's ingest into a plan step (pass 1 over all sources, itself sharded by source object, writing a scratch decision table: which records are new, their batch, their shard) and one process per batch that writes that batch's shard and rows (DEC-21: one shard per batch), then a merge step for the per-stage index tables. Batches are a deterministic function of the sorted inputs and the batch size. Output must be byte-identical to the single-process ingest.
Accept: with the batch size forced small on the fixture, the batch build equals the single-process build file for file; each process's CPU-minutes are in its run record and under the budget on the 1,000× synthetic stage.

### C-07 Scope on the host, around the container
**M** · Depends: C-05 · Pod: yes (the acceptance probe only)
Files: `src/biobox/runstep.py`, `src/biobox/search/runner.py`, `scripts/pod_run.sh`+, `scripts/pod_pytest.sh`, `src/biobox/profile.py`, `profiles/*.yaml`, `tests/r1/test_profile.py`, `tests/r1/test_runstep_units.py`, the two scope-argv assertions in `tests/r2/test_runstep.py` and `tests/r1/test_search_units.py`, `RUNBOOK.md` (if C-06 has merged)
Amended 2026-11-09 after the agent's first stop: inside the image `/sys/fs/cgroup` is empty unless bound (`--bind /sys/fs/cgroup:/sys/fs/cgroup:ro`; *measured*, wilkcomp02, 2026-11-09, probe units `c07-cgprobe*`), so both launch scripts add that bind; "the profile requires one" is a new optional profile key `require_mem_cap` (default false, true for wilkcomp02); the pod has 1117 GB of swap (*measured*, same probe), which is why `MemorySwapMax=0` is needed for a cap to kill.
Why: `systemd-run` is not visible inside the `.sif` (found by the import ladder on wilkcomp02, 2026-11-09), so a `run-step` or a search worker started inside the image cannot open its own scope and today runs with no memory cap.
Do: (1) `scripts/pod_run.sh <unit> <bio-box args>`: the one host-side launcher. It starts `systemd-run --user --unit=<unit> --collect -p MemoryMax=<profile> -p MemorySwapMax=0 nice -n 19 singularity exec <sif by digest> bio-box ...`, detached. (2) Inside the container `run-step` and the search runner detect that they are already in a scope (read `/proc/self/cgroup` and the cgroup's `memory.max`), do not call `systemd-run`, and write the detected cap into the run record. If there is no `systemd-run` and no cap, and the profile requires one, they refuse to start with a message that names `scripts/pod_run.sh`. (3) Add `MemorySwapMax=0` wherever a scope is built.
Accept: unit tests for the three cases (scope available; already capped; neither) using a fake cgroup tree, written first and seen to fail. On the pod: a step launched through `scripts/pod_run.sh` records the cap in its run record, and a deliberate 1 GB over-allocation in a 512 MB unit is killed by the kernel, not by polling.

## Phase W. One-command discovery workflow

From the walk-through in [`planning/measurements/2026-11-09_ssrnap_workflow/`](../measurements/2026-11-09_ssrnap_workflow/README.md):
mining single-subunit RNA polymerases with a custom HMM worked in about 30 s of compute but needed
150 lines of ad hoc code, and the recipe runner stopped in six places. These cards close that gap.
They do not depend on phase B, except where noted; cards that touch slot keys wait for B-16.

### W-01 Pack build survives genetic code 15
**L** · Depends: none
Files: `src/biobox/search/orfs.py`, `tests/r1/test_search_units.py`, `tests/r2/test_micro_s0_search.py`
Background: `orfs._translate` calls `pyhmmer.easel.GeneticCode(15)`, which does not exist; 61 phage contigs carry code 15 (TAG read as Gln) and the dark-ORF stage crashes.
Do: translate code 15 as table 11 with TAG → Q (a small explicit table, tested against a hand-written oracle); any other code pyhmmer lacks is a clear error naming the code and the contig, raised before the build starts.
Accept: a fixture contig with code 15 builds a pack; the translation test passes.

### W-02 A user's HMM file as a seed
**M** · Depends: none

Do: a seed kind `hmm_file` (path; the file's sha256 and the model's name, accession and length are recorded in `search_run`); the recipe hash covers the file's sha256, not its path. A seed kind `sequences` (FASTA of seeds) that builds a profile by aligning the seeds to a single-sequence profile of the first seed and running the builder, as the walk-through did.
Accept: the Pfam `RNA_pol` model given as a file returns the same hits as the same model named from a library; a moved copy of the file gives the same recipe hash.

### W-03 Pack and runs outside the data root
**M** · Depends: none
Files: `src/biobox/search/pack.py`, `src/biobox/search/runner.py`, `src/biobox/cli.py`, `src/biobox/catalog.py` (`attach_search`), tests
Do: the pack is built once per release as a step of `build-stage` (card C-04) into `search_pack/release=<R>/` and is read-only afterwards; a run's output directory is `--out DIR` (default: a per-user directory from the host profile, never the data root), and `attach_search` takes a list of run directories.
Amended 2026-11-10 after the agent's stop (probe on the pod: the fixture pack is 7 files, 2.14 MB, byte-identical across two builds; the size rule refuses it at the 64 MiB floor, and would at any scale because the pack manifest is a few KB): the pack does **not** go into the data root and is not a `build-stage` step or a registered artifact. It is a rebuildable cache of a release in a pack directory outside the root (`--pack-dir`, default under the profile's `tmp`), at `<pack-dir>/release=<R>/<pack_id>/`, written once and never overwritten. A search whose pack is missing exits 2 and names the command that builds it. The sentence above about `search_pack/release=<R>/` under the root is superseded.
Accept: a full run against a read-only store root succeeds and writes nothing under it.

### W-04 Worker threads, exit and kill labelling
**M** · Depends: C-05
Files: `src/biobox/search/worker.py`, `src/biobox/search/runner.py`, tests
Do: the worker sets the Arrow CPU and I/O pools, OpenMP and DuckDB threads to the run's CPU limit before importing anything that starts a pool; any uncaught exception ends the process with a non-zero exit (no hang); a SIGKILL that the runner did not send and that the cgroup does not report as an out-of-memory kill is recorded as `failed`, not `oom`.
Accept: the thread count of a worker with `cpus=2` on a many-core host stays under 16 (measured in a test through `/proc` or `psutil`); an injected exception in round 2 of an iterative run ends the run as `failed` within seconds.

**L** · Depends: W-05, B-25

Do: (1) `export proteins` without `manifests/release=<R>.json` refuses with exit 2 instead of reading every `protein_seq` file it finds (AGENTS §3.9: readers use the manifest's file list, never globs); migrate the W-05 test that compares "with and without manifest" to assert the refusal. ### W-13 A search launched on the pod can be watched and cancelled
**M** · Depends: C-06, A-07 (same files) · found while testing the runbook on the pod, 2026-11-10
Files: `src/biobox/search/runner.py`, `src/biobox/cli.py` (`search`), `scripts/pod_run.sh`, `RUNBOOK.md`, `tests/r0/test_runbook.py`, tests
Evidence (*measured*, wilkcomp02, units `c06-*`): (1) `search run` without `--wait`, launched through `pod_run.sh`, prints a run id; the unit then ends with its main process and systemd kills the detached runner, so the run ends `failed: runner exited without closing the run`. (2) A run started with `--wait` has no pending record while it runs, so `search status` finds nothing and `search cancel` cannot be used; the only cancel is `systemctl --user stop <unit>`. (3) After that stop the unit's log has no `pod_run: <unit> exited` line (the wrapper shell is killed too), and `run-step`/`build-stage` output reaches the log only when the unit exits (buffered). (4) The default search work directory is `/tmp/atd/search`, not under the profile's `tmp`.
Do: a run started with `--wait` writes the same pending record as a submitted one, so `status` and `cancel` work on it from another shell; `search run` without `--wait` under a unit either keeps the unit alive until the run closes or refuses with a message that says to use `--wait` there (decide by what `systemd-run --collect` allows; say which); `pod_run.sh` traps termination so the last log line is always written, and runs Python unbuffered; with a profile, the work directory defaults to a per-user directory under the profile's `tmp`. Update the runbook sections and its appendix rows for each.
Amended 2026-11-10 after W-03 merged: (a) the per-user default work directory is done (W-03: `<tmp>/search/<user>/work`, runs in `<tmp>/search/<user>/runs`, packs in `<tmp>/packs`); do not redo it. (b) The termination trap and unbuffered Python in `pod_run.sh` belong to card C-13; do them here only if C-13 has not merged. (c) W-03 moved the pack and the runs out of the data root and left `RUNBOOK.md` untouched: bring the runbook up to date here. `pack build` and `pack verify` need `--pack-dir` or `--profile` and a sentence that the pack lives outside the root; `search run`, `status`, `cancel`, `hits` and `nbr` need `--runs` and `--pack-dir`, or `--profile`; the work-directory note, the locations and `/tmp` cleaning notes (add `<tmp>/packs` and `<tmp>/search/<user>/`), the known-issue notes and the appendix rows change with them. (d) `catalog.EXCLUDED_TREES` still lists `search_pack` and `search` for old roots; correct its comment, which names `search/run_index.json` (add `src/biobox/catalog.py` to Files for that comment only).
Accept: tests first for the pending record and the default work directory; on the pod, a search launched through `pod_run.sh` is visible to `search status` while running and ends `stopped` after `search cancel` from another shell; the log of a stopped unit ends with the exit line.

### B-27 Lookups that cannot prune: contig labels and cluster membership
**M** · Depends: B-25 (found in the W-05 report; AGENTS §4.4)
Files: `src/biobox/registry/schemas.py` and lock (two tables), their builders (`src/biobox/l0seq/store.py`, `src/biobox/l2/cluster.py`), `src/biobox/catalog.py` (`contig_label`, macros), `src/biobox/search/export.py`, `src/biobox/search/nbr.py`, tests

### W-10 `bio-box search run` takes the host profile
**L** · Depends: C-07 (found in its report)
Files: `src/biobox/cli.py`, `tests/r1/test_cli_search.py`+
Why: `Limits.require_cap`, `mem_gb` and `cpus` cannot be set from the command line, so a search started by hand on the pod never refuses to run uncapped and ignores the profile's thread cap.
Do: `bio-box search run` and `search submit` take `--profile PATH` (default: `$BIOBOX_PROFILE` when set). With a profile, `Limits.require_cap` is the profile's `require_mem_cap`, `cpus` is at most `runstep.choose_threads` for the current hour, and `nice` is the profile's. Explicit `--cpus` and `--mem-gb` flags may lower, never raise, what the profile allows.
Accept: tests, written first: with a profile that requires a cap and neither `systemd-run` nor a cgroup cap (injected), the command exits 2 and names `scripts/pod_run.sh`; `--cpus` above the profile's cap is refused.

### E-07 ncRNA scan in shards, and in `build-stage`
**M** · Depends: E-01, C-12, PI card 0-08 (which hits, which models)
Files: `src/biobox/l1/ncrna.py`, `src/biobox/stage.py`, `src/biobox/cli.py` (`stage ncrna plan|shard|merge`, `build-stage --ncrna-cm`), tests, `RUNBOOK.md`
Why: one `cmsearch` process scans about 23.7 Mbp inside the 300 CPU-minute budget (*derived* from 1,319 bases per CPU-second, *measured*), so any real stage needs shards; and the producer is not in `build-stage`'s step list.
Do: shard the ncRNA scan as card B-24 did the domain scan (plan by cumulative bases over contigs sorted by `contig_uid`, one process per shard, merge in sort order, byte-identical to the unsharded run), reuse C-11's shard pool with a memory term (each `cmsearch` peaked at 12.3 GiB: jobs × 13 GiB must fit the unit's cap); fill the `features` slot of `build-stage` when `--ncrna-cm` is given; pass both producers to the IGR and tape steps, which changes the tape version by design.
Accept: sharded equals unsharded on the fixture; a `build-stage` run with and without `--ncrna-cm` gives two tape versions and the gene-call tables are byte-identical between them.
**Amended 2026-11-11 after DEC-28** (the design note `planning/design/0-08_ncrna_models.md` is the specification; read §2, §4 and §6 whole). Files gain `src/biobox/l1/rfam_sets/prok-1.tsv` (the frozen model set: accession, name, and whether the model is scanned on whole genomes; generated once from the note's rule script `planning/design/0-08_model_rules.py` and its tables, then never regenerated: a new set is a new file with a new version) and new test files. The producer scans (a) intergenic regions with a 200-base margin into the flanking genes, overlaps merged, with a map back to contig coordinates and strand and the origin of circular contigs handled, using `cmsearch --notrunc`, against the set's intergenic models; and (b) whole contigs against the set's 23 in-CDS models; never the same model on the same base twice. Hits are mapped back to contig coordinates (0-based half-open; a feature across the origin has `end > contig_len`). The producer name carries the set's version and the region rule, so a change to either is a new producer and a new tape version. A contig another stage owns is skipped (DEC-26, card C-20). Shards are cut by genome bases at 80% of the CPU budget from the measured rate (about 202 Mbp per process at 247.4 CPU-hours per 10 Gbp, *derived* in the note); memory per `cmsearch --cpu 2` was at most 6.4 GiB on intergenic records (*measured*, 0-09c), which replaces the 12.3 GiB above for the pool's memory term. Clan competition is card E-08, not this one: keep every hit, as today.
Tests first (each against an independent oracle): the regions of a fixture's gene calls equal a separately written per-base loop (margin, merging, linear ends, origin wrap); hits map back to the coordinates the `cmsearch` binary reports on the whole contig; the subset's hits equal the full fixture library's hits restricted to the subset; in-CDS models are scanned whole and nothing is scanned twice; sharded output is byte-identical to unsharded at 1, 2 and 4 shards.
Accept: the tests; then on the pod (Pod: yes) on the 8 genomes of measurement 0-09c the step reproduces that measurement's margin-200 `--notrunc` hits restricted to the set plus the whole-genome hits of the in-CDS class, at a CPU cost within 20% of 247.4 CPU-hours per 10 Gbp; and the two-tape-version check above.

### E-08 Clan competition for ncRNA hits
**M** · Depends: E-07 (same file) · DEC-28
Files: `src/biobox/l1/ncrna.py`, `src/biobox/l1/rfam_sets/Rfam-15.1.clanin` (in the repository; pinned by sha256 in `refdb.yaml`, entry `rfam_clanin`: check the hash at load), `src/biobox/registry/schemas.py` (`l1_ncrna`: append nullable `clan` and `clan_winner`), the schema locks by `scripts/update_schema_lock.py` only, tests
Do: as §5 of `planning/design/0-08_ncrna_models.md`: among overlapping hits of models in one clan, on the same strand, the best bit score wins; state the overlap rule exactly and where it differs from Rfam's own (`cmscan --oclan`), and test against that binary's output on a constructed case if the image's Infernal offers it. Every hit stays in `l1_ncrna` with its clan and whether it won; only winners (and hits of models in no clan) become `feature` rows and go on the tape. Session 8 merged 2026-11-13 (§2.1 S11: `emit.py` writes per-source FASTA, the ingest manifest and `build-stage` inputs, the 14 truth tables of §3 in emitted-contig coordinates keyed by `contig_uid` and `feature_uid`, a GFF and `manifest.json` with the world digest; `scripts/stress/f028_synthworld_builders.py` builds the emitted world with the normal builders and compares it with the truth: gene recall 0.990 by stop, 0.825 exact starts on untouched genes, duplicates and shared contigs handled as DEC-26 and DEC-27 say, no builder defect). **Item (5) is done: the generator runs S1 to S11 (227 to 232 s on the loaded pod).** Chosen values for the PI to confirm: mislabel rate 0.05 (lineage of a genome of another genus); six biomes, one per class taxon, none for phages; `source_class` isolate, MAG or "MGE database"; `replicon_class` chromosome, plasmid or virus with a `prophage_bearing` flag; quality complete, contig, `mag_hq` at completeness 0.9 or more, else `mag_mq`; `gene_id` is the S8 copy label and the row key is `feature_uid`; `release_tag` = "sw-" + BLAKE3-64 over the stage digests. Left over from §3: registering the truth as Parquet (proposal in the note: a registry and lock of its own, tables in the world directory, never in a data root); the empty columns (`ref_bitscore`, `gene_tree_extant`, `strength_class`, realized transfers and loci, `powered_expected`); IGRs made by S6 to S8; tandem-paralog traps; Cas and smORF domain architectures; the views. Remaining of the card: item (7) §4 null worlds; item (8) §6.2 to §6.4 (macOS against Linux digest, a CLI, `test_run` columns) and §7 (realism against the golden genomes, starting with gene starts: the caller gets 82.5% of true starts in this world; tool parity beyond pyrodigal). Session 7 merged 2026-11-13 (§2.1 S8 to S10: `assembly.py` applies the call errors, the code-4 and code-25 recoding and the assembly cuts in one rewrite, with `edits.tsv` as the lift from the S7 frame and `contigs.tsv` placing every contig on the S8 frame; `sources.py` adds contamination with a mock FCS-GX sidecar, exact and near duplicates, and accessions). Chosen values for the PI to confirm: call errors 30/30/30/30/40/40 (frameshift insertion and deletion, upstream and downstream alternative start, smORF, internal stop) on genes of at least 100 codons; alternative starts 8 to 30 codons upstream, 2 to 20 downstream; smORFs of 10 to 50 residues; code-4 clade of 2 to 6 genomes with 0.9 of internal Trp as TGA, code 25 with 0.1 of internal Gly as TGA; class weights 0.4/0.4/0.2, draft N50 50 or 200 kb, MAG N50 20 kb, completeness 0.70 to 0.95, contigs under 500 bp dropped, repeats of at least 1 kb at 98%; 3 contaminated genomes with 1 to 4 contigs of 3 to 30 kb from the farthest genome of the same world; 2 exact and 2 near duplicates at ANI 0.999. Left over: smORFs use uniform residues; call errors are per genome, not inherited; no outgroup world and no vector-like contaminants; truth tables are TSV. **Time: S1 to S10 take 195 to 209 s on the loaded pod (*derived*).** Remaining of item (5): S11 (emit: per-source FASTA, the ingest manifest, truth in contig coordinates, `manifest.json`), and feeding the world through the normal builders. Session 6 merged 2026-11-13 (§2.1 S7 only: `fusions.py`; 15 fusions, 5 splits and 2 NOGD events, each on a host branch and inherited by its clade; output under `world/fusions/` with `edits.tsv` as the lift from the S6 frame; `mobile.phage_preset` reads a fixed list of preset sections). Chosen values for the PI to confirm: clade size 2 to 12; linker 0 to 10 residues drawn from GSAPTEKDNQ; split stop TAA, spacer 20 to 60 bases with AGGAGG 5 to 10 bases before the ATG, split point midway between two domains; NOGD on planted-module members only. Left over from S7: a gene caller's view of fused and split genes (pyrodigal), divergence of linker and spacer inside a clade. **Time: S1 to S7 take about 177 s on the loaded pod, so the 3-minute target will not hold (PI open item); the next session applies S8's edits and S9's cuts in one rewrite of the genomes, measures each step, and does not thin the world.** Remaining of item (5): S8 (call errors; genetic codes 4 and 25 by recoding in S8, since S4 is code 11 and changing it would change the host world), S9 (assembly classes, contig breaks, fragments, `truth_edges`), S10 (contamination, duplicates, mock FCS-GX sidecar), S11 (emit). Session 5 merged 2026-11-13 (§2.1 S6 only: `mobile.py`; a phage set built as a world of its own by the S1 to S5 code with the host's HMM panel; 4 prophages after single-copy anchor genes; 10 CRISPR-Cas loci, each a type II-A Cas operon, leader and repeat-spacer array; spacers acquired at the leader end and deleted along the clade; a spacer-by-target table; host replicons rewritten under `mobile/` with `insertions.tsv` as the lift from S5 coordinates). For the PI: the Cas operon is planted by S6 with its array (families 900000 + 10a + k), not a ZOMBI2 group as §2.1 S2 lists, because a host group would change every world of sessions 1 to 4; confirm, or ask for the group (a world-changing step). Chosen values to confirm: 12 phages, 60 root families, duplication 0.005, transfer 0.025, loss 0.05 (fitted), origination 0.015; 4 prophages, clade size 2 to 10, rate 0.05; 10 arrays, clade size 2 to 12, repeat 36 with a 6-base stem, spacer 30, leader 150, PAM NGG, 5 to 40 spacers, 15 acquisitions and 0.3 deletions per spacer per mean path; mismatch weights 0.5/0.2/0.1/0.1/0.05/0.05, no-PAM fraction 0.1, decoys 0.05, targets within 5 mismatches. Left over from S6: plasmid targets; repeats and leaders do not change down a clade; targets are scanned in phages and prophages only; the spec's "as built" notes. **Time: S6 takes about 11 s, so S7 to S11 have about 12 s of the 3-minute target (*derived*); the next session measures first and reports if the target cannot hold.** Remaining of item (5): S7 (fusions, splits, NOGD), S8 (call errors, genetic codes 4 and 25), S9 (assembly classes, contig breaks, fragments, `truth_edges`), S10 (contamination, duplicates, mock FCS-GX sidecar), S11 (emit). Session 4 merged 2026-11-13 (intergenic regions, RNA motifs, replicon sequences). Chosen values for the PI to confirm: tight share 0.55; overlap tries 0.6 (4-base) and 0.06 (1-base), realized 0.12 and 0.065; median lengths tight 12, loose 130, divergent 240, convergent 70; cassettes 800 upstream, 500 downstream; element consensus probability 0.7; intergenic GC = expit(0.8·logit(GC3) − 0.25); motif rate factor 0.04, clade height 0.5 of the crown age, at least 15 genomes per linked family; Rfam models RF00050, RF00059, RF00162, RF00167, RF00168, RF00174, RF00504, RF00522, RF00234, RF01051 at `cmemit --exp 1.5`; circular replicons cut at a random origin, linear flanks of 200 bases. Left over: `ref_bitscore`, motifs inside coding sequence (DEC-28's whole-genome models are not exercised), a golden fit of the junction lengths (§7.1). What remains starts at item (5) below. Session 3 merged 2026-11-12 (families and coding sequences: profile-F81 on the gene trees, synthetic panel fitted to Pfam, GC3 codon model, code 11 only; output keyed by `copy_label`). Chosen values for the PI to confirm: F81 with site-specific frequencies; gamma alpha 1 in 4 categories; uncorrelated log-normal clock (sigma 0.3); kappa 3 for synonymous changes; tail and linker indel rate 0.05; GC3 by Brownian logit (sigma 0.25); start codons ATG/GTG/TTG 0.90/0.08/0.02, stops TAA/TGA/TAG 0.64/0.29/0.07; difficulty weights 0.5/0.35/0.15; domain counts 0.6/0.3/0.1. Open before session 6: detectability does not fall with divergence under this model (PI). Left over from §2.3: genetic codes 4 and 25, `real_seed_member` roots, named promiscuous domains, `ref_bitscore_median`, a Pfam panel usable in CI, a bounded `iter_genome`, and a way to bind the Pfam file in `pod_pytest.sh` for the parity tests. Session 2 merged 2026-11-12 (taxonomy, plasmids by excision, vehicles, hubs, decoys, distance decay, lineage-varying rates, typed events and gene trees; format `synthworld-backbone/2`); what remains starts at item (3) below, and the truth-table session must decide whether plasmid and vehicle pairs are excluded from null evaluation; N0 must drop decoys. Session 1 merged 2026-11-12 (backbone; output `out/backbone/{genomes,genes,families,groups,truth_units,truth_adjacency}.tsv` and `backbone.json`, documented in `backbone.py`). Remaining, in a workable order: (2) the rest of the backbone of §2.1 (plasmids as further replicons, vehicles, hubs, decoy recipients, distance-decay transfer, lineage-varying rates for the §5.1 size range, typed `gene_trees` and `genome_events` tables) and the synthetic taxonomy; (3) §2.3 sequences; (4) §2.4 and §2.5 IGRs and RNA motifs; (5) §2.1 S6 to S11 (phages and CRISPR, fusions, call errors, assembly, contamination, emit); (6) §3 truth tables; (7) §4 null worlds; (8) §6.2 to §6.4 and §7. Each session also applies the spike corrections (its §7, ten items) that touch its sections: items 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8 and 9 are still open. The `micro` strength grid (member transfer rate {0.05, 0.15, 0.30} by together fraction {0.3, 0.7, 1.0}) was accepted by the PI on 2026-11-12.
