## Phase R. Remediation of the audit of 2026-11-25

Written by the auditing session on 2026-11-25 at the PI's word ("fix all these issues top to bottom"). The findings and
their evidence are in [`../measurements/2026-11-25_audit.md`](../measurements/2026-11-25_audit.md). The cards do not
depend on each other unless one says so. None of them writes to the data root or to `/stor`; none launches or stops a
build. Tests that need MMseqs2, `hmmsearch` or MinCED must skip cleanly without them (GitHub Actions has none); the
orchestrator pushes each task branch to `origin` and reads its `ci` run before the merge.

Done 2026-11-25 (`STATUS.md` rows R-01, R-04, R-05, R-06, R-07, R-08; merged together through `task/R-int`): R-01
(`f68c911`), R-04 (`1553f4d`), R-05 (`c80969b`; its part (3), the default of `train_est.py --cluster-method`, was
withdrawn by the orchestrating session because it needs a test of card A-27 changed, which the card does not list),
R-06 (`a779fbd`, `1cdb5d9`; the function is `_resolve_library_path`), R-07 (`8e2d24c`), R-08 (`d600173`, `2ba0cc8`; six
job lists, not four, were on `tower` by then). R-03 (`c97ec8e`, its 20 passing checks; the 13 tests against plain
`mmseqs linclust`, `5811b41`, stay on branch `task/R-03` and are settled by DEC-78: the code stays). R-02 (`6c0d2e2`)
and its follow-up R-05b (`5ca7589`: the watch gives no DEC-77 cap to a cut under a format-1 plan, as the library no
longer does after R-02), merged together.

### R-01 Four tests fail on GitHub Actions since `f5c644b`
**M** · Pod: yes (pytest only) · Depends: none · audit §2.1 (1)
Files: `tests/r1/test_a25b_example_search_mem.py`, `tests/r1/test_b67_genecall_mode_gvmeta.py`, `tests/r1/test_b54_cluster_estimates.py`, `tests/r2/test_w39_panel_fixture.py`, `tests/conftest.py` (only to share a helper the two first tests import), `src/biobox/search/panel.py` (only if the memory bound is a fault of the code)
Why: `uv run pytest -q` on Actions (run `45711591139`, `3369387`) ends "4 failed, 3616 passed, 269 skipped"; the pod's runs passed, so the faults depend on how the suite is started. (a) and (b) `from tests.r1 import ...` inside two tests: there is no importable `tests` package under `uv run pytest`. (c) `test_b54_legacy_pre_b54_cut_records_remain_reusable_under_b49` builds a plan without `"method"` and indexes it by `cluster._INPUT_KEYS`, which card B-71 extended: `KeyError: 'method'`. (d) `test_w39_100x_synthetic_memory_bound` reads `ru_maxrss` in a child started by `subprocess.run` and finds 3,982,860,288 B on Actions. One hypothesis to test, not a finding: on Linux a child's `ru_maxrss` is not below the resident size its parent had at the fork, and on Actions the whole suite is one pytest process; cards C-15, C-16 and W-14 met the same class of fault and left helpers.
Do, test first (AGENTS §5.1): (1) Reproduce each failure on the pod by starting pytest as Actions does where that matters (one process over `tests/`, or the failing test after a large allocation in the parent for (d)); put the failing output in the report. (2) (a), (b): import the sibling helper the way the suite already shares helpers; no `sys.path` edit inside a test. (3) (c): the fixture is a release clustered before B-54, so it is a format-1 plan: build it and its expected inputs as the code reads a format-1 plan (`_plan_input_dict`); the test must still reject a wrong `cpu_min` and accept the legacy one. (4) (d): find the cause with evidence. If the measurement is the parent's, measure the child's own peak with the helper the suite has (or `VmHWM` of the child after its own start) and keep the 2 GiB bound; if the scan really holds more than 2 GiB, fix `panel.scan_fastz_shards` and keep the bound. Never raise the bound, skip or xfail.
Accept: the four tests fail first in a reproduction and pass; `sh scripts/pod_pytest.sh R-01 tests/r1/test_a25b_example_search_mem.py tests/r1/test_b67_genecall_mode_gvmeta.py tests/r1/test_b54_cluster_estimates.py tests/r2/test_w39_panel_fixture.py` passes; the `ci` run of the pushed branch is green.

### R-02 Clustering scratch pruning survives a kill, a format-1 plan can be redone, and the method is recorded
**M** · Pod: yes (pytest only) · Depends: none · DEC-77, DEC-24 · audit §2.2 (code)
Files: `src/biobox/l2/cluster.py`, `src/biobox/stage.py` (only where it calls `clean_consumed` or `lost`), `tests/r1/test_r02_cluster_prune_crash.py` (new)
Why: (a) `clean_consumed` (`src/biobox/l2/cluster.py`, the loop that calls `shutil.rmtree` and `_clear` and only then `_write_pruned`) removes the intermediates before `.pruned.json` records the prune. A process killed in between leaves intermediates gone and no entry; on resume `lost()` lists the pruned steps and the whole NR90 clustering is redone, with the scratch DEC-77 sized `/tmp` around. (b) Once an entry exists, intermediates that a redo wrote again are never pruned again. (c) Since card B-71 `_cut_doc` writes `createindex_cpu_min` into every shared-index cut record in every plan format, and `redo()` requires the redone record to equal the stored one: a format-1 clustering whose scratch is lost cannot be redone. (d) DEC-77 (2) says the method is recorded "in `cluster_plan` and the ledger"; the ledger rows hold level counts and tool versions only, and the one-process `run()` writes no plan record, so there the method is only in the step's argv.
Do, test first (AGENTS §5.1): (1) Failing-first tests in the new file: a kill (an exception raised from a patched `shutil.rmtree`, and one raised before `_write_pruned`) at each point of `clean_consumed`, followed by the resume path `stage.py` takes, redoes no clustering call and ends with tables byte-identical to an uninterrupted run; a redo after a lost milestone prunes again; a format-1 work directory (written with the format-1 code path, not a downgraded format-2 plan) is redone to byte-identical cut records; the method is readable from the release's ledger and plan records for `hybrid-b1`. Tests that need MMseqs2 skip without it; the ordering and record tests must not need it. (2) Record the intent to prune atomically before removing, and make the resume finish a recorded prune. (3) Write `createindex_cpu_min` only for plan format 2 and later. (4) Record the method as DEC-77 (2) states. `cascade` outputs, including its ledger and plan records, must stay byte-identical to what the published releases hold; if that and DEC-77 (2) cannot both hold, stop and report (AGENTS §1.7). Do not change `params_hash` or `cluster_uid`.
Accept: new tests fail first and pass; `tests/r1/test_cluster_pruning.py`, `tests/r1/test_cluster_hybrid.py`, `tests/r2/test_cluster_split.py`, `tests/r2/test_cluster_reuse.py`, `tests/r1/test_b54_cluster_estimates.py` (apart from the test card R-01 repairs), `tests/r2/test_build_stage.py` and `tests/r0` pass on the pod; no schema or lock change.

### R-03 An independent oracle for the hybrid clustering modes
**M** · Pod: yes (pytest only) · Depends: none · AGENTS §5.2 · audit §2.2 (code)
Files: `tests/r2/test_r03_hybrid_oracle.py` (new)
Why: for `hybrid-b1`, `hybrid-a` and `linclust`, `cluster.run()` replays the same `workflow(level, mode)` call list that `run_split` uses; only `cascade`'s `run()` calls `mmseqs cluster` itself. Every byte-identity check of `tests/r1/test_cluster_hybrid.py` and `tests/r1/test_cluster_pruning.py` compares two drivers of one decomposition, which is the function under test (rule 5.2).
Do, test first: on the S0 fixture's proteins (and a second, larger synthetic set with planted families at known identities, fixed seed), compare each hybrid level with an implementation that is not `workflow()`: (1) NR90 of `hybrid-b1` against one direct `mmseqs linclust` call with the parameters `LEVELS["nr90"]` and `COMMON` name, as sets of member sets; (2) NR50 against the single MMseqs2 command that the decomposition claims to equal, if there is one, and otherwise against properties checked by an independent alignment (every member meets the level's identity and coverage against its representative by `mmseqs align` or `easy-search` run separately; no two representatives of the planted families that should merge are apart). State in the file which of the two each check is. A difference is a finding: report it with the smallest input that shows it; do not edit `src/`.
Accept: the tests are seen failing against a deliberately wrong expectation (state how), then pass, or the card stops with a reported difference; they skip without MMseqs2 and are marked with the ring and `covers`.

### R-04 `release retire` checks what the tar holds and what the copies say
**M** · Pod: yes (pytest only) · Depends: none · DEC-66 (`S-4`), card C-54 · audit §2.2 (code)
Files: `src/biobox/catalog.py` (`retire_release` and its helpers), `src/biobox/cli.py` (the `release retire` arguments), `tests/r1/test_r04_retire_guards.py` (new), `tests/r1/test_c54_release_retire.py` and `tests/r2/test_c54_release_retire_fixture.py` (only to migrate calls to the `--copy` form below; no assertion loosened), `RUNBOOK.md` (the retire section)
Why: DEC-66 allows retiring a release "whose `.tar` is verified on both `/stor/work` and `tower`". `retire_release` checks that each `--tar` exists and that its sha256 equals the value typed; it never compares the tar's members with the files it removes, and stores `--copy` values as strings. A tar of another release, or a partial one, passes and the release's large files are deleted.
Do, test first: (1) Failing-first tests: a tar that lacks one file to be removed, one that holds it with another size, one that holds it with other bytes, and a tar of a different release are each refused before anything is removed; a `--copy` without `=<sha256>`, with a sha256 that is no `--tar`'s, or no `--copy` at all is refused; a correct call still retires, and an archive record written before this card still verifies. (2) Read each tar once as a stream: every file to be removed must be a member with the manifest's size and sha256 (bounded memory: hash by blocks). (3) `--copy <location>=<sha256>`, at least one, each sha256 equal to a `--tar`'s; the record stores location and sha256 (the pod cannot reach `tower`, so this is the operator's statement, recorded as such). (4) RUNBOOK: the command as it now is, and that the copy's sha256 is read from `tower`'s `SHA256SUMS`.
Accept: new tests fail first and pass; `tests/r1/test_c54_release_retire.py` passes with only its calls migrated; the archive records of `r26.11.1`, `rN.1a` to `rN.3` and `rM.2` (copied as fixtures by shape, not by path) verify unchanged.

### R-05 The watch raises a running cut's CPU cap only where the plan grants DEC-77's exemption
**M** · Pod: yes (pytest only) · Depends: none · DEC-77 (3), DEC-29, DEC-38 · audit §2.2 (code)
Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/r50_watch.py`, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/train_est.py` (the default of `--cluster-method` only), `tests/r1/test_r05_watch_live_cap.py` (new), `tests/r1/test_a27_hybrid_train.py` (only the test that asserts the fallback for a non-exempt live cut, migrated to the rule below)
Why: `dec77_cut_cap(..., live_proc=True)` returns the plan's `cut.one_process_cpu_min_max` (20,000 CPU-minutes in `profiles/wilkcomp02.yaml`) for any running shared-index cut step whose record is not yet written, whether or not that step's `createindex` is estimated over the part budget. Every live `cluster.nr50.15`/`.19` prefilter cut is watched at 20,000 instead of 280. `train_est.py --cluster-method` defaults to `hybrid-b1` while `train_plan.py` and `bio-box` default to `cascade`.
Do, test first: (1) Failing-first tests: a live cut step whose `createindex` estimate (the shared `createindex_cpu_min` of `l2/cluster.py`, from the plan's own inputs) is under the part budget is capped at the default; one over the part budget and within `cluster_cpu_min_max` gets the exempt cap; one with no plan, an unreadable plan, or an unsafe name gets the default. (2) Implement that rule: the same test `stage.py` applies when it grants `exempt: DEC-77`. (3) `train_est.py`'s default method equals the library's; `release_train.sh` already passes it.
Accept: new tests fail first and pass; `tests/r1/test_a27_hybrid_train.py`, `tests/r1/test_a22_watch_dec29.py`, `tests/r1/test_a16_rate_guard_bands.py`, `tests/r1/test_c46_release_train.py` and `tests/r0` pass.

**L** · Pod: yes (pytest only; the script is not run on a real stage by this card) · Depends: none · audit §2.1 (2)

### R-10 Memory-bound tests read the child's own peak, not `ru_maxrss`
**L** · Pod: yes (pytest only) · Depends: R-01 · found 2026-11-25 23:01 CST when `ci` of `2ea134d` failed
Files: `tests/r1/test_c48b_env_crosswalk.py`, `tests/r1/test_c48d_jgi_dedup.py`, `tests/r2/test_crispr_memory.py`, `tests/r2/test_e15_near_cas_memory.py`, `tests/r2/test_b48_genecall_memory.py`, `tests/r2/test_synthworld_sequences.py`, `tests/r2/test_w19_search_memory.py`, `tests/r2/test_micro_s0_search.py`, `tests/conftest.py` (one shared helper, if the suite's way of sharing helpers allows it there)
Why: `ci` at `2ea134d` (run `45754246039`) fails on `test_c48b_env_crosswalk.py::test_bounded_rss_on_100x_synthetic_inputs` (`Peak RSS too high: 575.82 MiB`, bound 512), which passed at `4015cde`. The test reads `resource.getrusage(RUSAGE_SELF).ru_maxrss` inside a child started with `subprocess.run`. Card R-01 measured that on Linux such a child reports the lifetime peak of the pytest process it was started from (a child that wrote 64 MiB read 3,011,248,128 B under a parent that had freed 3 GB), so the bound fails as soon as an earlier test raises the suite's peak; card B-74's new tests, which sort before this file, did. Every test that bounds a child's memory by `ru_maxrss` has the same latent fault.
Do, test first: (1) Reproduce the failure of `test_c48b` on the pod as card R-01 did (one pytest process started without the repository root on `sys.path`, whose parent has held more than the bound). (2) In each listed file, find every bound on a child's memory read from `ru_maxrss` (a bound on the test process's own `ru_maxrss` is a different case: list it in the report and leave it). Make the child report its own `VmHWM` from `/proc/self/status` (the high-water mark since its exec), with `ru_maxrss` only where there is no `/proc`, as `tests/r2/test_w39_panel_fixture.py` does since card R-01; one helper, not eight copies, if a shared place exists. No bound is raised, no test skipped or marked. (3) For each changed test show that it can still fail: a child that allocates over its bound fails it (a temporary edit, reverted; state it in the report).
Accept: `test_c48b` fails first in the reproduction and passes; each changed file passes on the pod started both ways; `tests/r0` passes; the `ci` run of the pushed branch is green.

### R-13 `fetch_to_tower.sh` keeps a file whose provider md5 matched when its archive test fails
**L** · Pod: yes (pytest only, one test file at a time while a build runs) · Depends: R-08 · found by card R-11 (its note §1, Integrity)
Files: `scripts/orchestrator/fetch_to_tower.sh`, `tests/r1/test_r08_fetch_to_tower.py` (new cases only) or `tests/r1/test_r13_fetch_keep_md5.py` (new)
Why: on `tower`, `EUK_Y1000/y1000p_genome_files.zip` was fetched in batch 3, its md5 equalled the provider's (`f3f5391df84c4133ba82fb45b0633652`), and `unzip -tq` then failed, so `_fetch_batch2.sh` removed a file identical to the provider's (`FAIL_ZIP` in `_batch3_detached.log`). `fetch_to_tower.sh` has the same order of checks (md5, then the archive test of the extension, then removal). `tower`'s `unzip` is 6.00 with `ZIP64_SUPPORT` (*measured*, `unzip -v`, 2026-11-26), so the cause is not known; an archive test is weaker evidence than the provider's own checksum.
Do, test first (AGENTS §5.1): (1) Failing-first tests with the stub `curl` of the existing test file: a file whose md5 equals the job's and whose archive test fails is kept, lands in `SHA256SUMS` and `SOURCE_URLS.tsv` like any other, and is reported on stdout as `OK` with a second line `WARN_<test>` naming it (and the run still exits 0 if nothing else failed); a file with no md5 (`-`) whose archive test fails is still removed and reported `FAIL_<test>`; a file whose md5 differs is still removed whatever the archive test says. (2) Implement exactly that; change nothing else of the script. (3) The header comment says the rule in one sentence.
Accept: new tests fail first and pass; the file's existing tests pass unchanged; `bash -n` passes; `tests/r0` passes; the `ci` run of the pushed branch is green.

### A-30 The stages of one wave share their rehearsal and their release verification (DEC-81 (3))
**M** (it changes what the driver checks before and after a build: read by the orchestrating session line by line before merge) · Pod: yes (pytest; the rehearsal fixture in scratch, never the data root) · Depends: R-07 · DEC-81 (3), DEC-66, DEC-43, DEC-64
Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stage_driver.sh`, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stage_queue.sh` and `stage_wave.sh` (only if the sharing needs the wave's name passed down), `tests/r1/test_stage_driver.py` and `tests/r1/test_r07_stage_wave.py` (new tests; an existing test is changed only where this card's rule changes what it must expect, each one named in the report), `RUNBOOK.md` ("Building a stage", "A wave of stages"), `planning/measurements/<date>_a30_wave_checks.md` (new)
Why: every stage of a wave runs the same fixed checks whatever its size. *Measured* (`wilkcomp02`, the `stage_queue` lines of `/tmp/if2453/stages/{g1,spx1}/queue.out` and `r26-11-4-wave.out`, read 2026-11-26): `rehearse` 28 to 36 min (G1 33, SPX1 28, V2 36), `before` 15 to 25 min (G1 15 and 17, SPX1 16, V2 25: it verifies every published release, and there are more each time), `after` 15 to 16 min, `check` 2 min, `backup` 12 min (SPX1). About 1.6 h a stage, about 29 h of the 18 remaining stages of `r26.11.4` (*derived*). The rehearsal builds the same fixture through the same driver for every stage of the same depth, least length, gene-call mode, library and CRISPR choice; `before` of one stage verifies the releases that `after` of the stage before it has just verified. The PI agreed to share them (DEC-81 (3)), knowing it removes some repetition of safety checks.
Do, test first (AGENTS §5.1): (1) Measure before designing: from the three driver logs on the pod (read-only), the wall time of each part inside `rehearse`, `before` and `after`, so that the note says what each sharing saves. (2) Rehearsal: a `rehearse/DONE` may be taken from another stage's directory when all of these are equal: the conf's kind (`DEPTH`, `MIN_CONTIG_LEN`, `GENECALL_MODE`, `LIBNAME`, `LIBVER`, `LIBCUT`, `CRISPR`), the code the rehearsal exercises (a sha256 over the tree's `src/` and the stage scripts, taken the same way each time; say how), the image digest and the profile. The marker of the taking stage names the stage it came from and the four values; a difference in any of them runs the full rehearsal. Never across trees. (3) Verification: `before` may skip `release verify` of a release when an `after` or `before` marker of this wave records that release verified with the same manifest sha256, and the root holds nothing new since (state exactly what is compared: the listing the driver already takes, with sizes and names, and the sha256 of the earlier stages' files that `after` records); anything that differs, or a marker from another wave or older than the newest file of the root, runs the full verification. `after` always verifies in full. (4) Not touched: the sums of the inputs, `estimate`, every guard of `run` (process CPU-minutes, rates, `/tmp`, scratch), `check`, `backup`, the handling of `FAILED`, `REFUSED`, `STOPPED` and `STOP`. (5) Tests with the stubs of `tests/r1/test_stage_driver.py`: a share happens only under full equality and is logged with what was shared from where; each single difference (one conf value, the code hash, the image, the profile, a new file in the root, a changed manifest, another wave) forces the full check; a stage alone (no wave) behaves as today. (6) An environment switch that turns all sharing off, documented in the RUNBOOK with what is shared and what is not.
Accept: new tests fail first and pass; the existing tests of the two files pass unchanged except those named; `bash -n` on each changed script; `tests/r0` green; the note gives the measured parts and the saving a wave of 18 and of 25 stages can expect (*derived*); the `ci` run of the pushed branch is green.

### A-31 The release-diversity figure: audit its numbers, and bring it up to the planned releases (`r26.11.4`, `r26.11.5`, `r26.11.6` and what waits on `tower`)
**M** · Pod: yes (read-only queries of the root for the audit; the rendering, one thread, a minute) · Depends: none to start; card A-29's ownership table replaces the staging notes' figures for `r26.11.5` when it is merged · AGENTS §6, DEC-79, DEC-85 · written by the orchestrating session, 2026-11-27, at the PI's word "launch worker to audit and update with all planned releases r26.11.4, r26.11.5 and whatever else"
Files: `scripts/plot_release_diversity.py`, `planning/measurements/release_diversity.json`, `planning/measurements/2026-11-27_release_diversity/` (new: the figure as `.png` and `.svg`, and nothing else), `planning/measurements/2026-11-27_release_diversity.md` (new: the audit and the sources of every number), `tests/r1/test_a31_release_diversity.py` (new), `planning/measurements/README.md` and `RUNBOOK.md` (only the lines that name the figure or say how it is made)
Why: an earlier orchestrating session drew a four-panel figure of how the catalog's diversity grows by release against outside benchmarks (`planning/measurements/2026-11-24_release_r26.11.3/source_diversity_r26.11.3.png`, from `scripts/plot_release_diversity.py` and `planning/measurements/release_diversity.json`): source composition with an effective-classes index, cumulative stored sequence by source class against OpenGenome 1, distinct proteins and NR50 families against UniRef50, UniRef100 and NCBI ClusteredNR, and new NR50 families per stored Gbp by source. Nobody audited it: its numbers were typed into a JSON file by that session, the `r26.11.4` column is a projection (287.0 Gbp, about 265 M proteins, about 64 M families), and it stops there, while `r26.11.5` is now staged (24 stages, DEC-85) and `r26.11.6` and about 60 further sets on `tower` are planned.
Do: (1) **Audit.** For every number in `release_diversity.json` and every benchmark line, find its source and label it *measured* (host, date, command), *derived* (formula and inputs) or *estimate* (AGENTS §6); recompute the measured ones for the released releases read-only from the root on the pod (the manifests and tables of each release: stored bases by stage, distinct proteins, NR50 families) and from the release notes, and report every difference; a number with no source that can be found is marked so and drawn as an estimate or removed. Check the class each stage is put in, the effective-classes index (formula, and what it is computed over), and panel d's per-source yields against `c35_check.py`'s outputs or their notes. The benchmark values are outside figures: do not fetch anything (AGENTS §8); give for each the citation and date the file states, say that it was not re-verified, and flag any that the documents of this repository contradict. (2) **Data, not typing.** Each number in the JSON carries its label and source; where a number can be read from a file of the tree (a note's table, a conf's sums, a staging report), the script or a small collector reads it or the test compares the JSON against it, so that the next release changes the figure by a command and not by retyping. (3) **Update.** Add: `r26.11.4` as it stands (two stages built, 17 staged: measured staged bases, not the old projection, with what is built shown apart); `r26.11.5` (the 24 stages of DEC-85: the staging notes' measured bases and new bases; `RMD1` and `GBRMGD` are being staged by card C-83 and are drawn as estimates until measured); `r26.11.6` (its members as DEC-79 and DEC-85 leave them, with the catalog goal's and the plan's estimates); and what is fetched on `tower` and assigned to no release (from `sources.yaml` and the ingest audit), as one honest remainder with its bytes on disk where bases are not known. Three states must be told apart at a glance and in the legend: measured in a release, staged with measured bases but not built, planned on an estimate. Projections of proteins and NR50 families for unbuilt releases are drawn only where a stated formula gives them (say it in the note and on the figure); otherwise they are left out, not guessed. New source classes (single cells, giant viruses, mirusviruses, reef and wetland MAGs) are placed in the existing classes or given a class, with the rule written down. (4) **Figure.** Keep the four panels and the style unless the audit shows a panel misleads (say which and why); every benchmark line labelled with its value and date; the footnote says what is measured, staged and estimated. Render on the pod in the image (no plotting on the laptop), copy the two files back. (5) The old figure and its release note are left as they are (a record of `r26.11.3`).
Accept: the test fails first and passes (it checks that every number of the JSON has a label and a source, that the sums by class equal the totals, that each stage is in exactly one class and one release, and that the measured values of the released releases equal the audit's recomputed ones); the note lists every difference the audit found, with the old and the new value; the figure is in the tree and the note says how to regenerate it in one command; `tests/r0` passes; the `ci` run of the pushed branch is green.
Done 2026-11-27 (STATUS row A-31; `planning/measurements/2026-11-27_release_diversity.md`). Follow-ups: redraw with exact `r26.11.5` ownership and measured coral stages once C-83 merges (card A-32); re-verify UniRef50 and UniRef100 benchmark lines before external presentation.

### R-17 `stage_check.py` counts a stage's non-default variant of a contig another stage holds as stored, and fails a stage that is as ingest makes it
**M** (a gate of every stage build; read line by line by the orchestrating session before merge) · Pod: yes (pytest only; one read-only run of `stage_check.py` on `V2` in a scratch directory, no marker written) · Depends: none · DEC-26, DEC-65, AGENTS §3 · found 2026-11-26 16:32 CST when `V2`'s `check` stopped wave-4
Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/stage_check.py`, `tests/r1/test_r17_stage_check_variants.py` (new), `RUNBOOK.md` (the `check` row of "Building a stage", one sentence), `planning/measurements/2026-11-26_v2_check_variants.md` (new)
Why: `V2`'s build ended with exit 0 and complete (`run/DONE`, 16:07) and its `after` passed (16:31); `check` then failed with "contigs both stored by the stage and held by another: 43" (`/tmp/if2453/stages/v2/check/1/check.out`). *Measured* (`wilkcomp02`, 2026-11-26 17:30, a read-only DuckDB query over `l0seq/contig_index/stage=*`, `diag/v2_variants.py` in the orchestrating session's scratch directory): `V2`'s index has 243,915 rows for 243,509 contigs; 243,466 rows are default variants with 8,199,089,073 bp, the numbers the stage was staged with and the 243,466 contigs its CRISPR step searched; the 43 other contigs are held by `S1` as default, and for each `V2` has one row with `is_default_variant` false and a `raw_digest` that no earlier stage has for that contig (320,799 bp in all, about 7.5 kb each: the same phage genomes written differently). That is what ingest is written to do: `_write_tables` in `src/biobox/l0seq/store.py` gives a new `(contig_uid, raw_digest)` pair of a contig whose default an earlier stage holds a row with `is_default_variant` false. `stage_check.py` builds its `ci` from every row of the stage's index and asserts that none of those contigs is held by another stage; it took the rule from `c47_check.py` (`owned`), written when no stage had such a variant. So the check fails a stage that is as the builder makes it. No stage before `V2` had a variant of a contig held elsewhere.
Do, test first (AGENTS §5.1): (1) Find where the data model and the suite say what a non-default variant in a later stage is (the plan documents, `tests/r2/test_micro_s0_l0seq.py` and the other tests that read `is_default_variant`); put the references in the note. If nothing establishes that a later stage may hold a non-default variant of an earlier stage's contig, stop and report: then the question is ingest's, not the check's. (2) Failing-first tests on a small fixture root built with the library (two stages, the second with a contig equal by `contig_uid` and different by raw bytes to one the first stores, as a rotated circular record gives it): today's check fails it with the message `V2` got. (3) Change the check: "stored by the stage" for the ownership assertions means the stage's default-variant rows; a non-default row of a contig that another stage holds as default is allowed and counted in the report under its own name; and add the assertions that keep the rule tight: every contig of the stage's index has exactly one default variant among all stages (in this stage or in one other), no non-default row's `raw_digest` repeats one an earlier stage has for that contig, and the genecall ledger's skips still equal the held set. A default-variant row of a contig another stage holds still fails, with today's message. `stored` in the summary stays the count the estimates use (default variants), with the index's contig and row counts beside it. (4) Run the changed check on `V2` read-only on the pod (its own snapshot directories copied to scratch, or the script's dry mode if it has one; no file under `/tmp/if2453/stages/v2/` is written) and put its counts in the note: expected 243,466 stored, 43 variants of contigs held by `S1`, 18,112 held. (5) Say in the note whether `c47_check.py` has the same rule and whether anything still runs it.
Accept: the new tests fail first and pass; the existing tests that run `stage_check.py` (`tests/r1/test_stage_driver.py`, `tests/r1/test_a21_stage_check_copy_sources.py`) pass unchanged; `tests/r0` green; the read-only run on `V2` reports no failure of the ownership assertions; the `ci` run of the pushed branch is green.

(6) No new dependency: bash and the tools the script already uses (it runs on Unraid's bash and curl; no GNU parallel). (7) README: when to use it (many small files from one host), and that a provider's stated connection limit comes first (CNGB and NCBI ftp: stay at 4 unless the provider says more).

### B-75 Ingest refuses a byte that is not a base (DEC-84 (1))
**M** · Pod: yes (pytest; one measurement of the ingest cost on a staged stage, read-only inputs, a separate output root) · Depends: R-20, R-23 (their notes) · DEC-84 (1), MR-14, DEC-43 · written by the orchestrating session, 2026-11-27
Files: `src/biobox/fasta.py` or the nucleotide ingest path that calls it (`src/biobox/l0seq/store.py`), whichever the reading in (1) shows to be the right place, and no other file under `src/`; `tests/r1/test_b75_ingest_alphabet.py` (new); `tests/r1/test_c76_lr2_sag1_nx1_r232.py` (only card R-20's fixture test of the behaviour as it was, migrated to the new behaviour); `planning/measurements/2026-11-27_non_base_bytes.md` (a dated section)
Why: ingest has no alphabet check: `iter_records` (`src/biobox/fasta.py` lines 47 to 72) appends every sequence line as it is, the store hashes and stores it, and the `contig_uid` is made from it, so a byte that is not a base becomes part of a contig and of its identifier (*measured* by card R-20's fixture test). On 2026-11-24 a staging session wrote 691 gzip files into `LR2`'s FASTA as sequence and the input check passed it twice; cards R-19 and R-23 then found such bytes only in three staged first files, all re-staged, and nothing in any built stage, so a refusal at ingest refuses nothing that is held or staged today (`planning/measurements/2026-11-27_non_base_bytes.md`, its section on what a check would refuse). The PI decided that ingest refuses (DEC-84 (1)).
Do: test first. (1) Find every caller of `iter_records` and of the ingest path (`rg -n "iter_records|fasta\." src`): the same reader may read protein FASTA (search seeds, exports), which must keep working; the check belongs where nucleotide sequence enters the store, or behind a parameter of the reader that only that path sets. Say which and why. (2) A record of a nucleotide ingest with a byte outside the IUPAC nucleotide letters `ACGTUNRYKMSWBDHV` of either case stops the ingest with an error that names the object, the record's header, the 1-based position and the byte value; nothing of that object is stored (the stage's files are written under temporary names and renamed, §3.9: show that a refused ingest leaves no file of the stage in place). An empty record is refused the same way if it is not refused already; say what happens today. (3) Cost: the check must not move the ingest step out of its estimate (`stage.ingest_cpu_min`, judged by the rate guard at more than 20%): use a whole-line test (`bytes.translate` with a delete table, or equal), measure the ingest of one staged stage of a few Gbp with and without the check on the pod, in a separate output root, and give both CPU times; if the difference is above 5%, stop and report. Files: `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/release_saturation.py` (new), `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/train_check.py`, `scripts/stress/build_stage_pod/release_train.sh`, `RUNBOOK.md` ("Cutting the next release" and "Release diversity figure"), `tests/r1/test_a33_release_saturation.py` (new), `planning/measurements/release_saturation_history.json` (new), `planning/measurements/2026-11-27_release_saturation/` (new: `.png` and `.svg`), `planning/measurements/2026-11-27_release_saturation.md` (new)
Why: raw NR50 cluster counts grow quasi-linearly with metagenomic contig fragments because linear-contig edge truncations (`protein_occurrence.dist_to_contig_end_bp == 0`) create unclusterable singletons (`planning/measurements/2026-11-18_spire_pilot.md`: `27.81 M` distinct proteins across `4.29 M` contigs `>= 5 kb`, *measured*). To judge diminishing returns across releases and habitats without distorting discovery decisions, every release closeout needs fast, bounded-memory saturation metrics over complete ORFs and syntenic gene pairs (`DEC-86 (1)`).
Do, test first (AGENTS §5.1): (1) Failing-first unit/fixture tests in `tests/r1/test_a33_release_saturation.py` covering: (a) manifest-only reading (no globs, AGENTS §3.9); (b) linear-contig edge-truncated CDS (`dist_to_contig_end_bp == 0`) excluded from complete-ORF metrics while circular-contig CDS (`dist_to_contig_end_bp == contig_len > 0`) are kept; (c) identical multi-copy ORFs within one genome (`contig_dim.genome_uid`) counted as `1` genome incidence (`g_c = 1`, singleton `f_1`) rather than inflating `f_2` or `k >= 3`; (d) Good–Turing genome-incidence sample coverage `C_hat = 1 - f_1 / N_inc` and bias-corrected Chao1 `S_obs + f_1 * (f_1 - 1) / (2 * (f_2 + 1))` exact against a brute-force Python oracle (safe when `f_2 == 0` or `N_inc == 0`); (e) marginal actionable family yield (`k >= 3` and `k >= 5` distinct genomes, complete ORFs) decomposed into `brand_new_ge_k` (`0` genomes in earlier stage prefix -> `>= k`) plus `promoted_to_ge_k` (`1..k-1` genomes in earlier stage prefix -> `>= k`), plus cumulative collector curve across all stages in `release_def` order; (f) strand-invariant adjacent CDS NR50 pair counting from `contig_tapes` + `tape_tokens` + `contig_dim` (`((t_a, s_a), (t_b, s_b))` canonicalized against reverse-complement `((t_b, -s_b), (t_a, -s_a))`, wrapping `(n_slots - 1, 0)` iff `is_circular` and `n_slots >= 2`, skipping non-CDS null tokens), giving `pairs_ge1`, `pairs_ge3`, `pairs_ge5` distinct genomes and `C_hat_pairs`; (g) `train_check.py` formatting when `--saturation-summary` is passed. (2) Implement `release_saturation.py` with bounded memory (`VmHWM <= 8 GiB` at `4` threads; note `src/biobox/l3/tokens.py`'s warning that unbounded DuckDB hash joins over whole-release slot tables exceed `memory_limit`: either stream co-sorted Parquet tables by `protein_uid` / `contig_uid` or partition into hash ranges / spilling DuckDB sort-aggregates under `--tmp`). Subcommands: `measure` (writes deterministic `saturation.json` and `saturation.txt` for one release, optionally naming `--earlier`), `history` (updates/verifies `release_saturation_history.json` with labelled entries), and `render` (renders a Wilke-style multi-panel `.png` + `.svg` of cumulative stage-prefix collector curves and per-stage marginal yields in the pod image, 1 thread). (3) Wire `release_saturation.py measure` into `release_train.sh` step 8 right before `train_check.py` (`bb_ro py`, writing `$WORK/saturation/saturation.json` and `$WORK/saturation/saturation.txt`, passing `--saturation-summary` to `train_check.py` so `$WORK/announcement.md` includes a concise saturation bullet), and document in `RUNBOOK.md`. (4) Measure read-only on `wilkcomp02` (`<= 4` threads, `MemoryMax=16G`, `nice 19`) on `r26.11.2` (`11` stages) and `r26.11.3` (`13` stages, `--earlier r26.11.2`), recording wall time, CPU time, and `VmHWM`; render the figure on the pod and copy `.png` and `.svg` back; run `release_train.sh rehearse` (`CLUSTER_METHOD=hybrid-b1`, `<= 4` threads) in `/tmp/if2453/a33/rh` (cleaned afterwards) to confirm end-to-end rehearsal passes.
Accept: new tests fail first and pass (with and without pod tools on `PATH`); `tests/r1/test_c46_release_train.py` and `tests/r0` pass on the pod; `r26.11.3` `measure` finishes in `<= 300 s` wall and `<= 8 GiB` `VmHWM` on `wilkcomp02`; `release_train.sh rehearse` exits 0; figure visually inspected with zero overlapping labels; `ci` of the pushed branch is green.
Stop if: `measure` on `r26.11.3` takes over `300 s` wall (`5` min, the budget of `DEC-86 (1)`) or `8 GiB` `VmHWM` after optimizing its pass.

## Phase H. S2 and later

Each stage starts with an **H** planning card: read the previous gate's measurement file, refit
`sizing.yaml`, and write that stage's cards from this template. Small models do not plan a stage.

Template (every stage): source contract → fetch and lock → `genome_input` → stage-specific
producers → `build-stage` → accretion equivalence → Z invariance and projection → measurements →
question cookbook → gate review.

Stage-specific work the planner must card:

| Stage | Adds |
|---|---|
| S2 plasmids (PLSDB) | license check before any redistribution; contig identity and deduplication (`genome_identity`, `contig_member`, skani); plasmid gene calling in meta mode; the DuckLake, K-07 and K-12 decisions |
| S2C complete genomes | selection of 25,458 genomes from GTDB metadata as a reproducible query; host retraining of plasmid gene calls (DEC-13); taxonomy into `contig_dim`; search tier ST2; null-world calibration at stage scale (kill criterion K7) |
| P1 | Hetzner rehearsal: same image digest, manifest hash equal to the pod's; costs into `planning/costs.md` |
| S3 small genomes | 38,750 genomes and 52.4 Gbp, not 9–12k (re-budget first); genetic-code detection; NR90-representative scans with projection (S-08); Q5 and Q6; edge censoring becomes mandatory |
| S4 IMG/VR | local-only source (JGI policy); statistics only in any pack; L2 drift test |
| S5 species representatives | 465.5 Gbp and 24.95 M contigs; go or no-go from the scaling fit (kill criterion K1); memory exception request if needed |
| S6 all genomes | 111.5 M contigs; per-genome work on the pod, global steps on Hetzner; pangenome planes |
